# Changelog

Builds in `~/los24` are logged here before they run (section "Build log"). Older history: `docs/history/` (entries before 2026-11-03: `docs/history/CHANGELOG-2026-10-28_29.md`).

## 2026-11-10: the phones' on-screen keyboard is the apps' IME inset, not the navigation bar (Termux's extra keys above it)
- Cause: geometry.c folded the Maliit keyboard's height (`ime_px`) into CONFIGURE's navigation bar value, so apps saw a taller navigation bar:
  no `WindowInsets.Type.ime()`, no softInputMode. An adjustResize app's bottom row (Termux's extra keys) ended up under the keyboard.
- Fix, the Android way: CONFIGURE carries the keyboard's height as its own field (10th int); the host (`NavigationBarInset.setIme`) puts the
  IME's insets source (InsetsSource.ID_IME) into every window's InsetsState, as InsetsStateController does (robolectric patch 0027 in
  ShadowWindowManagerGlobal), and sends the windows the new insets. ViewRootImpl then applies each window's softInputMode itself
  (adjustResize: the decor shrinks; adjustPan: the focused view pans into sight; unspecified: its own choice); `isVisible(ime())` and
  `getInsets(ime())` are right. The navigation bar inset stays the bar's own height; the bar stays under the keyboard (owner's decision);
  the desktop text-input-v3 path is unchanged (it has no keyboard area). Test input `ime:<px>` (TEST_IME) sets the keyboard area as maliit.c does.
- Checks: `tests/vm/ime-insets.py` (group input; hello.input `-Dhello.ime=resize|pan`): adjustResize ime insets 300 px, content 523 -> 223 px
  and back; adjustPan content kept, the number field panned from 424 to 232. Parallels (own tree): groups input, activities, mobile PASS.
  Volla X23 (Ubuntu Touch, own instance, this tree's shell and jars): Termux with Lomiri's keyboard (541 px), the extra-keys row directly
  above the keyboard (SurfaceFlinger's picture and Lomiri's screenshot), navigation bar inset still 84 px; hello.input typed through
  Lomiri's keys. Volla Quintus (Sailfish, own instance, shell built in the Sailfish build root): Lipstick's keyboard area 1080x868 reached
  Termux as the IME inset (extra keys end at y 1532, the keyboard's top), bar inset 135 px; hello.input's field took text.

## 2026-11-10: Ubuntu Touch: "Unknown app" cards in the spread, silent apps
- Spread: Lomiri (qtmir) gives a client to the app its pid was launched as. The shell (one client for every app) had no launch of its own, so
  it was `unknown.qtmir`: every window landed on an "Unknown app" card (seen on the X23: two Unknown app cards holding NewPipe's and UiBench's
  windows, NewPipe's own card black). A shell started by a drawer launch inherited that app's DESKTOP_FILE_HINT and would carry every window
  as that app. Fix (cli/stack.cpp ensureShell, bridge/shell/window.c): on Lomiri the shell starts without the starter's APP_ID/DESKTOP_FILE_HINT
  and with qtmir's hint for a client of many apps (`Waydroid`), and its xdg app id is the desktop entry's name (`apklayer-<package>`), so
  each window goes to its own app's card. Checked with a test shell (own runtime dir) and drawer-style launches (`lomiri-app-launch`): Lomiri's
  launcher items and spread show Calculator's card with its window, no Unknown app; swiped away, "No running apps"; the next app
  (hello.nativeaudio) on its own card; two apps at once are two windows of two apps in TopLevelWindowModel's log.
- Closing from the spread could end the shell: qtmir force-closes a window that is still there a few seconds after its close request, and
  Mir's force_close SIGTERMs the client (seen: hello.nativeaudio swiped away, `onCloseTimedOut`, the shell gone, so every app's window).
  The shell now destroys the window at the compositor's close (`window_close`, also the test input's `close`); the app ends on CLOSE as
  before. tests/vm/multi-app.py closes the survivor that way (Parallels: PASS). Not yet driven through Lomiri's spread with this shell (the
  phone was locked again on the owner's request; no Lomiri screenshots).
- Audio: the click (the noble deb's tree) had no `libapklayer-audio-pulse.so`: debian/control lacked libpulse-dev, and the phone has no
  PipeWire daemon, so audio was `(none: off)` and AudioFlinger played into nothing. debian/control (and docs/INSTALL.md) build it now.
  Checked with that library: hello.nativeaudio's PulseAudio sink-input on `sink.primary_output`, monitor RMS 2778 over 3 s (its tone),
  RATE PASS. Sailfish's RPM already had it (audio = pulse there).
- Lomiri screenshots (Volume Up + Down) open an editor over everything; taken in a row on the busy phone they left it unresponsive: closed by
  locking the screen, and docs/HALIUM.md now says to use `apklayer screenshot`.

## 2026-11-10: Android's fullscreen is the compositor's fullscreen; a requested turn is a rotation (NewPipe's player)
- Causes: (1) an app hiding the status bar (WindowInsetsController.hide(systemBars()/statusBars()), SYSTEM_UI_FLAG_FULLSCREEN,
  FLAG_FULLSCREEN) reached nothing: only the navigation bar's visibility went to the shell (immersive mode), so a desktop window kept its
  header bar and floating size, a mobile one the compositor's panel. (2) NewPipe's fullscreen button asks SENSOR_LANDSCAPE and enters
  fullscreen from the VideoDetailFragment that Android recreates on the rotation (MainActivity declares no configChanges); apklayer applied
  every resize in place (onConfigurationChanged for all activities), so the window turned but the player kept its portrait layout and never
  went fullscreen. (3) Leaving it, NewPipe asks portrait while the window is fullscreen: the shell kept the output's shape, the app stayed
  landscape.
- Fix: robolectric 0019 also reports the status bar's visibility (NavigationBarInset.statusBarChanged); the host sends the new MSG_FULLSCREEN
  (18) {id, on}; the shell (mobile.c window_set_fullscreen) calls xdg_toplevel.set_fullscreen / unset_fullscreen (a mobile window is
  maximized again), and the FULLSCREEN configure state drops the title bar (W_BAR, decor_hide) so the app gets the whole output. A turn the
  app asked for (setRequestedOrientation) is a rotation (AppHost.hostResize): activities without orientation|screenSize in configChanges are
  recreated through HostConfiguration.dispatch (the requested orientation carried over); drags and fullscreen stay in-place resizes. The other
  shape asked for while fullscreen on a desktop leaves fullscreen so the window turns back (geometry.c window_request_size). wl_shell
  (Lipstick) unchanged: it shows every window full screen. hello-camera's Portrait declares the configChanges it handles (its doc said so).
- Tests: new group `fullscreen` (tests/vm/fullscreen.py, script step `fullscreen:0|1|2|3`: hide/show(systemBars()), legacy flags on/off;
  desktop and a simulated phone): the configure is the whole output with no title bar / no navigation bar inset, the app's picture has that
  size, leaving restores size, bar and inset, SENSOR_LANDSCAPE then fullscreen then portrait recreates and turns back. Parallels (own tree
  ~/apklayer-fs, slots fs1/fs2): fullscreen, window-geometry, mobile, activities pass; camera passed 2 of 3 runs (one camera-portrait
  "preview is not upright and cropped"). NewPipe 0.29.1 (corpus build 1015) on Parallels: the fullscreen button turns the window and now
  recreates MainActivity into the landscape player layout; the video itself never loaded (the network gave ~20 KB/s), so NewPipe's own
  fullscreen call (MainPlayerUi.checkLandscape on playing) was not seen. The x86 PC was unreachable and the X23 overloaded: not checked there.

- The keymap fix (keyboard.c, 2026-11-10) was checked only with uinput keyboards. Now with a real Bluetooth one: the owner's x86 PC as an LE
  HID keyboard (`tests/vm/bt-keyboard.py`: BlueZ GattManager1 HID service 0x1812 with a boot keyboard's report map, advertised with
  appearance 0x03C1, a NoInputNoOutput agent, Pairable only for the run; text from a fifo as key-report notifications). Paired from each

  `Ab1`, then `Hello2` (Lipstick's screenshot shows it); Volla X23 (Ubuntu Touch 24.04) `format 1, 64755 bytes, ok`, `Ab1`, `Hello2`

  not pairable or discoverable, no advertisement); the Quintus' Bluetooth off again as found. Script fix on the way: its cleanup restores
  Pairable first (later it came back Busy) and survives SIGTERM.

## 2026-11-10: SIGTERM soak closes retest (c)
- tests/corpus/soak-sigterm.py (runs on the host, in one instance): the corpus runner's end of an app repeated: launch to "started ... depth=1", the first frame and 3 s without frames, a screenshot, SIGTERM to every pid in the instance's run.* records, the second launch as soon as the first `apklayer run` returns, SIGTERM again, `apklayer stop`. Each SIGTERMed process's time to exit through a pidfd (no polling) into $APKLY_OUT/soak.jsonl; one alive 5 s after its SIGTERM is captured before the deadline (/proc status with SigBlk/SigIgn/SigCgt, threads and wchan, kernel stacks where sudo -n works, jcmd Thread.print, the instance's logs) into $APKLY_OUT/keep/; one alive after 12 s is captured again, SIGKILLed and fails the soak. The capture path was checked with two shells that ignore SIGTERM (7 s: captured, recorded; 60 s: captured twice, killed, failed).
- Release 0.4.14 code, unsandboxed instances as tests/run makes them. Exit after SIGTERM, all processes (app JVM, spare JVM, its holder, the window sink):
  | host / app | rounds | launches | processes | median | max | over 5 s |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | Parallels, UiBench | 53 | 106 | 424 | 0.35 s | 0.37 s | 0 |
  | x86 PC, Telegram FOSS (arm64 libraries through the bridge) | 53 | 106 | 424 | 0.35 s | 0.48 s | 0 |
  | x86 PC, UiBench | 52 | 104 | 416 | 0.35 s | 0.46 s | 0 |
  Every launch settled, every `apklayer run` exited 143, no exit deadline hit, nothing captured. PLAN retest (c) closed: the original cause stays unknown, a stall is bounded by the 10 s deadline.

## 2026-11-10: release 0.4.13 (runtime 0.4.13)
- Bug-fix release: Termux (app uid and groups, program names, `ls -a`, its login and bootstrap on Sailfish's glibc 2.30), CDS archives that crashed the next app (every custom loader's classes linked first), DocumentsUI's overlay crash, the display's mode and real size, setup reinstalls system apps a runtime update changed (x86_64 MediaProvider), Rethink DNS and Go libraries through the bridge (the guest's own auxv), OpenGL ES 1 in packages (gfxstream from the prebuilts), XRGB window buffers shared instead of copied, the Flatpak keeps libdl_bio's .symtab (bytehook), SIGTERM ends an app at any moment (exit deadline, spare holder, bionic code cannot ignore it), cameraserver survives a provider restart (backtrace signal), the phones' on-screen keyboard (show again, Backspace on a selection, Enter, field types, hide) and keyboard devices on Sailfish (keymap mapped), App Manager's hidden package/user services and install dates, libhybris' messages out of programs' stderr, a component host ending on SIGTERM during its setup.

## 2026-11-10: install dates in PackageInfo
- PackageInfo.firstInstallTime/lastUpdateTime were 0 for every package (App Manager showed 1/1/70): HubPackages.installTimes takes them from the installed APK (its directory's creation, the APK's modification), for other packages (hub) and the app's own (AppEnvironment). hello-system's hiddenservices check requires real times for every package; system-apps passes on Parallels and the x86 PC.

## 2026-11-10: the hidden "package" and "user" services answer apps that call them directly (App Manager's app list, retest (d))
- Cause: App Manager 4.1.1 in no-root mode takes `ServiceManager.getService("package"/"user")`, wraps each in a binder of its own (ProxyBinder) and calls
  IPackageManager/IUserManager through `Stub.asInterface` on that wrapper, so every call is a `transact`. Robolectric serves no "package", a "user" that answers
  null, and attaches its fakes to a plain Binder whose onTransact answers nothing: no users, no packages, apps.db 0 rows, an empty list.
- Fix: `HostPackageService` (IPackageManager.Default with the calls apps make: getInstalledPackages/Applications, getPackageInfo, getApplicationInfo,
  get{Activity,Receiver,Service,Provider}Info, getPackagesForUid, getNameForUid, getPackageUid, getAllPackages, queryIntentActivities, resolveIntent,
  enabled settings, hasSystemFeature, checkPermission, install source; its `Users`: getUsers, getUserInfo, getProfiles, getProfileIds, serials...) answers from
  the app's own PackageManager/UserManager (the hub's package data, Robolectric's user store). `HostBinderStubs` generates (ASM) a real `Stub` subclass that
  forwards every method to it, so transactions are unparcelled by AIDL's own code; ShadowHostServiceManager hands those binders out for "package" and "user".
- Checks: hello-system `hiddenservices` (group system-apps: getService by reflection, getInstalledPackages through a wrapper binder lists itself, getPackageInfo,
  getUsers, getProfiles); Parallels (own tree): system-apps, activities, services, providers pass. App Manager on Parallels: after I Agree the list shows the
  instance's 25 apps (apps.db 25 rows).

's fake-camera checks pass on both Halium phones (the frames were turned, not mis-laid)

## 2026-11-10: a keyboard's keys reach apps on Sailfish OS; CDS links every custom loader's classes (an app's own ClassLoader subclass too)
- Sailfish keyboard. Cause: Lipstick (Qt 5 compositor, wl_seat v3) sends a real XKB_V1 keymap (64029 bytes) in an fd whose file offset sits
  at its end, so the shell's read() got 0 bytes, xkbcommon failed and every key went out untranslated. Fix (bridge/shell/keyboard.c): the
  keymap is mmap'd MAP_PRIVATE as the protocol says (offset-independent); a NO_KEYMAP or unusable one falls back to xkbcommon's default RMLVO
  keymap (as Qt clients do), whose state then follows the keys themselves. Checks: Volla Quintus (Sailfish 5.0, own instance, this tree's
  shell built in the Sailfish build root, run through APKLAYER_BUILD with its own XDG_RUNTIME_DIR): `xkb keymap loaded (format 1, 64029
  bytes, ok)`, a uinput keyboard types Shift+A, b, 1 into hello.input: `text=A`, `Ab`, `Ab1` (the installed 0.4.13 shell: `0 bytes,
  FAILED`, no text); Volla X23 (Ubuntu Touch, own instance, the Parallels-built shell): `format 1, 64755 bytes, ok`, `Ab1`; Parallels
  group input PASS (`format 1, 68405 bytes, ok`).
- CDS. Cause: CdsDump linked only the classes of loaders apklayer creates (tracked ones and their chains), so an app's own ClassLoader
  subclass left unlinked classes in the dynamic archive (the nestmate-resolved constant pool entry that crashed Termux). Fix: the classes come
  from JVMTI GetLoadedClasses (NativeLoader.loadedClasses, bridge/native/app_native.c), every class of a non-builtin loader is linked before
  the dump; the loader tracking (CdsDump.track) is gone. Test: hello-cdsloader (a ClassLoader subclass defines a nest's member, getNestHost
  loads the host unlinked), group cds runs `cds-dump.sh hello.cdsloader '[cdsloader] PASS'`: before the fix `FAIL cds-dump: 2 unlinked
  custom-loader classes in the archive`, after it PASS (no unlinked class, the next cold launch runs on the archive). Parallels (own tree):
  groups cds, startup, input PASS.

## 2026-11-10: hello.camera's fake-camera checks pass on both Halium phones (the frames were turned, not mis-laid)

- Cause: not the provider's YUV layout (BackendCamera::captureYUV already writes through lockYCbCr's y/cb/cr, ystride, cstride and
  chroma_step; the values read back exact). The phones give hello.camera's landscape-locked Main a portrait window (720x1528, 1080x2400), so
  HostCameraShadows.windowShape sets the camera compat info and cameraserver forces ROTATE_AND_CROP_90 on every request (Camera3Device
  overrides even an explicit NONE while compat rotate-and-crop is set). A row a quarter down the turned 640x480 frame crosses the scene's
  height: red for the bars' three quarters, black for the bottom quarter: exactly `81/90/240` x6, `16/128/128` x2.
- Fix (test app only): Main checks a portrait window's YUV frame and JPEG with Portrait's turned-frame check (Portrait.turnedAt/checkTurned,
  now shared). camera-reload's reopen retries an "unknown device" open: the evicted client's disconnect reports the camera present after the
  dead provider removed it (CameraService does so on Android too), and the restarted provider's add repeats that status, which CameraManager
  drops, so a slow phone provider gave no second availability callback.
- Checks: Volla X23 (UT, ut instance) and Volla Quintus (Sailfish, ut) with `providers set camera fake`: `yuv ok 640x480 170/166/16
  145/54/34 106/202/222 81/90/240 (turned)`, jpeg ok, PASS (front camera too on the X23); camera-reload (phone copy of
  tests/vm/camera-reload.sh): `reopened yuv ok ... (turned)`, PASS, no cameraserver crash, on both; camera = device again. Parallels (own
  tree): group camera PASS (camera.sh, camera-reload.sh, camera-portrait.py, CAMERA1=1). No prebuilt changed.

## 2026-11-10: SIGTERM ends an app JVM at any moment (retest (c): UiBench 9 min, Telegram 20 min past the runner's SIGTERM)
- Found: an app JVM's end on SIGTERM depended on Java code. HotSpot's handler only wakes its signal dispatcher, which runs Terminator's Java
  handler (a new thread, Shutdown.exit: System.getLogger, the Shutdown monitor, then the hooks); the exit deadline of 2026-11-10 was armed by a
  hook, so a SIGTERM whose end never reached the hooks was not bounded. Measured on the x86 PC (Telegram through the bridge): a SIGTERM during
  the CDS dynamic dump's safepoint is not dispatched until the dump ends (5.2-10 s after the first activity: the end came 0.7-5.3 s after
  SIGTERM, 0.4 s otherwise); a thread holding java.lang.Shutdown keeps the end from ever starting (new test case: it ran until killed).
- Fix: SIGTERM's arrival starts the 10 s deadline itself (native_loader.c apklayer_exit_deadline_on_term, from the native runtime's load in
  every app and spare JVM): its handler writes to a pipe that an idle native thread waits on, then chains to HotSpot's. A component host and
  the hub keep their own SIGTERM handling (sun.misc.Signal replaces it).
- Found reproducing the runner's flow (it SIGTERMs every run record, the spare's too, and launches again at once): the next launch took the
  spare that was ending (its holder still served it for ~300 ms): the launch died with 143 (delays 0-300 ms after the spare's SIGTERM, every
  time). The holder refuses a launch once it got SIGTERM ("the instance's spare is not for this launch: it is ending (SIGTERM)"), the launch
  starts cold (cli/spare.cpp).
- A library that ignores or catches SIGTERM (hypothesis (b)) kept the JVM running: hello.arm64's library calling signal(SIGTERM, SIG_IGN)
  and sigaction did on the x86 PC through berberis (SIGTERM ignored, still running 30 s later). bionic_translation 0064: bionic code
  neither ignores nor catches SIGTERM (bionic_sigaction reports success, the new bionic_signal goes through it), the guest's included.
  Not seen in Telegram (strace of its launch: only HotSpot sets SIGTERM's action), so not the cause of its stall.
- Not found: what kept the two corpus JVMs alive for minutes (no logs of those rounds were kept); with the deadline at SIGTERM's arrival any
  such stall now ends 10 s after it (exit 137, "the JVM had not ended by its exit deadline: killed" in the app log).
- Tests: exit-deadline.py cases held (Shutdown held: killed 10.0 s after SIGTERM), spare (the launch right after the spare's SIGTERM starts
  cold and ends on SIGTERM; before: it took the ending spare and died, 143) in group activities, native (the library ignores and catches
  SIGTERM, it ends in 0.4 s; before: still running 30 s later) in group native-bridge. x86 PC (own tree): activities, native-bridge PASS;
  the corpus runner on Telegram: both launches draw. Parallels (own tree): activities, native-bridge (native route), units PASS.

## 2026-11-10: deferred bugs: WebView Back (not a bug), input-inject under stalls, CDS of other loaders, FCL sound measured
- WebView Back in a text selection finishes the activity: Android does the same. From the WebView APK we ship (baksmali of
  external/chromium-webview arm64): ContentUiEventHandler.dispatchKeyEvent sends KEYCODE_BACK (with MENU, HOME, volume...) to
  View.dispatchKeyEvent, WebView's onKeyUp passes it on, and the selection's ActionMode is TYPE_FLOATING (SelectionPopupControllerImpl
  startActionMode(cb, 1)), which DecorView/ActionModeController does not end on Back (only a primary one). Chromium's only
  OnBackInvokedCallback is for its mouse selection drop-down. A TextView differs (Editor ends its action mode in onKeyPreIme).
- input-inject: every step waits for the previous step's effect (a list of preconditions on the app's ticks, every 500 ms) instead of fixed
  times; Ctrl+A's key and a tick before the IME text, `ime applied` after the empty done. APKLY_INPUT_STALL=<s> stops the app JVM after each
  step: passes at 2, 2.5 and 3 s on Parallels (own tree); group input passes. The run ends after its last step (~25 s, was 26 s fixed).
- CDS: CdsDump links the classes of every loader apklayer makes (AppClassLoader: the WebView provider's, the app's libraries'; an
  InMemoryDexClassLoader), not only the app's chain. Group cds runs cds-dump.sh on hello.wvprobe too: before, 99 unlinked custom-loader
  classes in the archive (7314 linked first), now 0 (8594). Parallels: cds, webview, webview-input, input pass.
- FCL's sound measured on the x86 PC (tests/vm/fcl-audio.py, instance xf1 of the proc tree): Minecraft 26.3's title screen, the instance's
  PipeWire stream recorded through parec --monitor-stream for 60 s: RMS -32.5 dBFS, peak -15.4 dBFS, every second above -50 dBFS, varying
  levels (music). The PC's default sink itself is muted by its owner (desktop setting, left as it is).

## 2026-11-10: release packages build OpenGL ES 1 (libapklayer-gles1.so); EGL window buffers in XRGB8888 no longer copied (regression retest gaps)
- libapklayer-gles1.so (gfxstream's GLES_CM translator, for hosts whose Mesa has no ES 1: Ubuntu's and Debian's) was built only with
  `-Dgfxstream=<source>`, which no package build passes: the debs, the Flatpak and the rpms shipped without it and OpenTTD (SDL 1.2, ES 1)
  drew nothing there. Meson now takes the prebuilts' `common/gfxstream` when `-Dgfxstream` is empty (bridge/hals/graphics/gles1/meson.build);
  `tools/stage gfxstream <prebuilts>` copies the tree's hardware/google/gfxstream (meson files, host, common, third_party; 58 MB) there.
  The prebuilts branch must carry that directory for the release packages (lead).
- An EGL window buffer from the host's XRGB8888 surface (OpenTTD's ES 1 window; also AR30/XR30/XB30) failed gralloc's import on every
  frame ("Stride didn't match, expected 814 got 3256": the mapper's bytesPerPixel had no XRGB8888 and reported the stride in bytes) and every
  frame went through the copy path. aosp/hals/graphics/common/gralloc_format.h: 4 bytes per pixel for them; mapper.apklayer rebuilt.
- Parallels (own tree, gfxstream from the staged prebuilts copy, new arm64 mapper): OpenTTD draws its menu, New Game and Generate open a
  running game (the map, date advancing), no "gralloc import failed" (129 before).

## 2026-11-10: Halium phones on 0.4.13: Termux on Sailfish (glibc 2.30 path layer, libhybris's stderr), a component host's SIGTERM during its setup
- Builds: main f1bf81d2 (0.4.12 + today's fixes) as 0.4.13, built locally (the CI dry run 42733674620 predates bionic_translation 0063): Sailfish
  RPM (sailfish-rpm.sh on the Mac), click (click.sh on Parallels), runtime bundle arm64 0.4.13 (runtime-bundle.sh in OrbStack, sha256 95f1b042...).
  `apklayer setup --update --bundle`: both phones reinstalled their 10 system apps (the per-APK record), 4 min (Quintus) / 11 min (X23).
- Termux on the Volla Quintus (Sailfish OS 5.0, glibc 2.30) never installed its bootstrap; three causes, fixed:
  1. glibc before 2.33 exports no stat functions (its headers make stat() a call of `__xstat`): the package's Temurin JDK (built against glibc
     2.17) reaches the file system through `__xstat64`, which the path layer's LD_PRELOAD did not wrap, so java.io.File saw the host's /data
     ("The termux files directory not found at path /data/data/com.termux/files"). paths_preload.c wraps `__xstat`, `__lxstat`, `__fxstatat`
     (and their 64 forms); where glibc has no such symbol (2.33+, compat only) they go to the kernel's newfstatat.
  2. The same glibc gap the other way: the wrappers of stat, stat64, lstat, lstat64, fstatat, fstatat64, mknod and mknodat called
     `dlsym(RTLD_NEXT)`'s NULL on 2.30, so an Android program bound to them by name jumped to 0 (Termux's dash: `[runtime-trace] signal 11
     pc=0x0` from libapklayer-preload's stat64). They fall back to the kernel's call.
  3. btload's constructor resolves the GL extension stubs through the host's libEGL.so.1, on Sailfish libhybris, whose linker prints every
     vendor library it does not find on stderr (two lines per eglGetProcAddress in an app's environment, ~1200 per program): the second bootstrap
     stage read `id -u 2>&1` as `library "libEGL.so" not found...10013`. stderr is /dev/null during the lookups (tools/gen/android-stubs.py).
  Test: tests/native/path-layer.c calls `__xstat64`/`__lxstat64` (fails without the wrappers on glibc 2.39: no such symbol). Quintus: a fresh
  bootstrap reaches the prompt, `id` typed on Lipstick's keyboard: `uid=10013 gid=10013 groups=10013,3003(inet),9997,20013,50013`.
- `apklayer --instance ut stop` took 5-10 s on the Volla X23 when it met a component host starting (F-Droid's, restarted by its job ~10 s after
  each instance start): the host took SIGTERM as an orderly stop from the start of ComponentHost.run, but only its main loop reads the flag,
  so its setup went on, found the hub ending (`Instance hub is exiting`, then a new `starting hub`) and waited, while the hub's shutdown hook
  waited 4 s for it and SIGKILLed it (exit 137; strace: SIGTERM forwarded to the JVM at once, the JVM alive 4.2 s later). The orderly-stop
  handler is now installed when the loop starts; before it, the JVM's default exit. Reproduced 3 of 3 before (stop at the host's registration:
  9.8, 9.8, 5.1 s, exit 137), after: exit 143 every time.
- Checks, instance `ut` on each phone (uinput taps, SurfaceFlinger and compositor screenshots):
  - UiBench starts and draws (both); Termux reaches its prompt, `id` = app uid and Android groups (X23: 10015 with 3003, 9997, 20015, 50015;
    Quintus after the fixes above);
  - keyboards (hello.input): shown on focus, `ok go `, a long press selects the word, one Backspace deletes it, Enter's newline, swiped down
    (`imInitiatedHide`) and shown again by a tap, the email field's `@`/Go layout and Enter = action 2, password (hidden text, Done), the
    number pad, Hide keyboard (`hide=true`, `host keyboard hidden, field kept`) and a tap shows it again: both phones;
  - camera = device: Open Camera's live preview from both phones' front cameras (camera 1: a room on the Quintus, the ceiling on the X23; the
    back ones face the desk); provider reload: with the fake provider under Open Camera, `providers set camera fake ... fps=15` restarted the
    provider: Camera3-Device's error state, `CameraTraces: Process trace saved`, cameraserver alive on both (the 0063 path; crashed before);
  - NewPipe opens (X23: the Live list with thumbnails; the Quintus' 0.20.1: Trending, empty as before); `stop` ends every instance process in
    1.0-1.9 s (after the fix above).
- Found, not fixed (docs/PLAN.md): hello.camera's YUV check fails with the fake provider on both phones (`yuv bad 640x480 81/90/240 ...`: six
  bars read as red, two as black; Open Camera shows the bars right), so tests/vm/camera-reload.sh stops before its reload there. Fixed the same day (the frames were turned for the portrait window, entry above).

## 2026-11-10: cameraserver no longer crashes when the camera provider restarts under an open camera (bionic_translation 0063)
- The SIGSEGV (in libc under libbinder, after BR_DEAD_REPLY and the drain timeout) was not binder: the open device's transactions to the
  dead provider failed as on Android, Camera3Device went into its error state (the app gets onDisconnected/ERROR_CAMERA_DEVICE) and
  setErrorStateLocked's CameraTraces::saveTrace unwound every thread with libunwindstack's ThreadUnwinder. It signals each thread with 33
  (BIONIC_SIGNAL_BACKTRACE); bionic_sigaction already puts that handler on glibc's SIGRTMIN (33 is glibc's internal SIGSETXID), but
  tgkill reached glibc's own (the linker and ld.so resolve the plain name there), so 33 arrived and glibc's setxid handler dereferenced
  its unset command (fault 0x8; strace: rt_sigaction(SIGRT_2), tgkill(SIGRT_1)). Fix: bionic_kill, bionic_tgkill and bionic_tkill send
  33 as SIGRTMIN. Test: tests/vm/camera-reload.sh (group camera; hello.camera -Dhello.reload): the provider restarts at "[cam] reload",
  the device reports the disconnect and error, the camera comes back available, opens again with a YUV frame of the bars, no fault in this
  run's cameraserver logs. Parallels (own tree): camera (also failed with the crash before the fix), hardware, binder, hub-services,
  native pass. No prebuilt library changed (bionic_translation is built from the patch series).

## 2026-11-10: SIGTERM that did not end processes (an app JVM's stalled end, a provider reload, the spare's records)
- Camera (PLAN: "cameraserver and the provider outlived `apklayer stop` after a restart, SIGTERM ignored"): the cause was the stack daemon's
  blocked SIGTERM inherited by the services it restarts, fixed 2026-11-04 (05d02fbc, children get the default mask; the camera note was
  written on a branch without it). Checked on Parallels: hello.camera open, the provider restarted (`providers set camera`) and cameraserver
  restarted, no instance process has SIGTERM blocked or ignored but the stack daemon (its signalfd), `stop` 0.75 s, nothing left.
- Found doing it: a provider reload (SIGHUP) stopped the stack's mediaserver and audio HAL, whose service names are front-less provider
  domains (`media`, `audio`): providerDomains() lists only the domains with a front (cli/providers.cpp); providers.sh checks.
- App JVM that outlived SIGTERM (retest 2026-11-10 (c), not reproduced: SIGTERM to UiBench at its first line, after its start and through
  the CDS dump, cold and from the spare, ended in 0.01-1.5 s each): every app JVM now has an exit deadline. From the start of its end
  (a shutdown hook, NativeLoader.killIfExitStalls from AppEnvironment.startRuntime) a native thread outside the JVM kills the process 10 s
  later if it still runs (native_loader.c exitDeadline), as init and ActivityManager kill what SIGTERM did not end: a stuck hook, the exit
  path's fflush or a safepoint no longer hold it. Test: exit-deadline.py (group activities; hello.acttarget with a hook that never returns:
  SIGTERM, exit 137 after 10.0 s).
- The spare's holder passes SIGTERM to its spare JVM (cli/spare.cpp): killed together from their run record (the corpus runner), the holder
  left the record with two dead pids, which later kills could hit as recycled pids.
- Parallels (own tree ~/apklayer-sig): hardware, activities, camera, startup, services, hub-services, units PASS.

## 2026-11-10: arm64 Go libraries through the x86_64 native bridge read the guest's auxv (Rethink DNS, binary_translation 0005)
- Rethink DNS died with SIGILL on the x86 PC: its Go library (libgojni.so, c-shared: Go reads /proc/self/auxv) got the host's file through
  berberis, took x86's AT_HWCAP bit 24 for arm64's HWCAP_DIT and executed `mrs x0, DIT`, which berberis does not decode.
- binary_translation 0005: InitKernelArgs records the auxv it gives the guest (the same the guest's getauxval reports), and OpenatForGuest
  serves it from a memfd for /proc/self/auxv and any path to the same file (/proc/<own pid>/auxv), beside the /proc/self/maps emulation.
- hello-arm64 checks that /proc/self/auxv's AT_HWCAP/AT_HWCAP2 equal getauxval's (`auxv ok`, group native-bridge).
- x86 PC (own tree, lead prebuilts + the new libberberis_arm64): native-bridge, native-bridge-vulkan PASS; Rethink DNS (tests/corpus/run.py)
  loads libgojni.so and draws its Welcome screen on both launches, no SIGILL. Library: Build log (lock set; not in the prebuilts branch yet).

## 2026-11-10: hideSoftInputFromWindow hides the phones' keyboards (Maliit)
- Cause: InputMethodManager.hideSoftInputFromWindow stayed in Robolectric's shadow. Its visibility handler reports only a change, and
  HostTextBridge resets the shadow's state to hidden after each show (so every showSoftInput counts): a hide never reached the handler.
- Fix: a shadow of our own (HostTextBridge.ImeShadow, a ShadowInputMethodManager) marks the keyboard shown before each
  hideSoftInputFromWindow, so the handler sees every hide; HostTextBridge sends MSG_TEXT_INPUT state 4 (the field keeps focus). The shell
  (textinput.c) passes it to maliit.c, which tells the server no field has focus and calls hideInputMethod, keeping the window's field: Lipstick
  kept its keyboard on screen after a bare hideInputMethod (the server reported a 0x0 area, the screenshot still showed it), Lomiri hides on
  either. Surrounding-text updates wait while hidden; the next showSoftInput (a tap in the field) or focus change sends the whole state again.
  text-input-v3: a no-op (the protocol has no hide request; a disable would also end a desktop input method's composing). Focus leaving all
  fields already hid the keyboard (state 0).
- Phones, own instance `hide` (the installed CLI, this tree's shell and host-classes.jar on a copy of runtime 0.4.11; the Sailfish shell built
  in the Sailfish build root, the UT one on Parallels): hello.input's new "Hide keyboard" button, uinput taps, the compositors' screenshots.
  Volla X23 (Lomiri) and Volla Quintus (Lipstick): the keyboard is up on the focused field, the button hides it with the cursor still in the
  field (`[hello-input] hide=true`, shell `host keyboard hidden, field kept`, area 0x0), a tap in the field shows it again.
- Tests: tests/vm/ime-fields.py (group input): the button's hide request reaches the shell, no focus loss, typed text still goes to the field.
  Group input passes on Parallels (own tree, two runs).

## 2026-11-10: CDS archive: custom-loader classes linked before the dump (Termux SIGSEGV)
- Cause (reproduced on Parallels, every time: UiBench's first launch writes the archive, then a cold Termux launch): HotSpot (JDK 21) links
  only the builtin loaders' classes before a dynamic dump and archives a custom loader's unlinked class with its constant pool as it is
  (ConstantPool::remove_unshareable_info returns early for unlinked classes). The framework's classes come from Robolectric's sandbox loader;
  UiBench's archive held 2054 of them unlinked (the spare's preload and verification load without linking), among them android.widget.Scroller,
  whose NestMembers entry for Scroller$ViscousFluidInterpolator a nestmate check had resolved. Termux maps Scroller from the archive, never
  loaded the member, and its first `new Scroller$ViscousFluidInterpolator` takes the archived klass with no mirror: SIGSEGV in
  InstanceKlass::init_lock. Not the SIGTERM: HotSpot writes to `<name>.temp` and renames it, an interrupted dump leaves only that file.
- Fix (runtime/host/java/apklayer/host/CdsDump.java): before the dump every class of the app's loader chain (app, sandbox) is linked
  (getDeclaredFields: JVM_GetClassDeclaredFields links), repeated until linking loads no more (7120 classes; 0 unlinked custom-loader
  classes in the archive, 87 -> 110 MB, the dump 1.3 -> 1.9-2.4 s on Parallels, once per instance); a JVM whose shutdown has begun (SIGTERM,
  System.exit) keeps no archive; an interrupted dump's `.tmp`/`.tmp.temp` (85 MB) is removed by the next dump. The archive's name is a hash of
  the class path files, so the new host jar replaces any archive written before. The class path HotSpot validates is the same for every
  app (the host's; app classes are in AppClassLoader, matched by their bytes).
- Test: group `cds` (tests/vm/cds-dump.sh): an app ended mid-dump keeps no archive, a full first launch writes one with no unlinked
  custom-loader class (fails on the old host jar: 2054), a cold launch runs on it. Parallels: `cds` PASS (FAIL with the old jar), Termux
  cold on UiBench's archive 3 of 3 launches up (crashed every time before), `startup` (startup.sh) and `activities` (all 7) PASS.

## 2026-11-10: setup reinstalls the system apps a runtime update changed
- `apklayer setup` installed the runtime's system apps (providers, DocumentsUI, WebView) only while `setup-done` was missing, so a runtime
  update never replaced them: x86_64 users who set up before 0.4.10 kept the arm64 MediaProvider (JNI not linking through the bridge,
  Downloads/Media broken) although the bundle now carries the x86_64 one.
- cli/setup.cpp records each installed APK's sha256 in `<data home>/apklayer/system-apps.sha256` and installs (`install --system`: the APK,
  native libs and classes replaced; uid, system mark and the instance's app data kept) only the APKs whose sha256 differs. A data home from
  before the record reinstalls all once; a failed APK stays out of the record (tried again next setup); a user's newer version
  (INSTALL_FAILED_VERSION_DOWNGRADE) stays. `setup --check` says `todo` while one differs. Same path for `--bundle`, the manager's update and Halium.
- x86 PC, own tree (fake runtimes `old`/`new` in the data home, the installed-layout CLI `inst/bin/apklayer`): first setup 9 installed,
  MediaProvider `nativeRoute=bridge-arm64`; record removed (pre-fix data home) and runtime switched to the x86_64 MediaProvider: 9 reinstalled,
  `nativeRoute=native`, uid unchanged; setup again: nothing installed; only MediaProvider changed: 1 installed. Then groups files and
  system-apps PASS (downloads, media). Parallels own tree: first-start PASS, tests/vm/setup-space.sh PASS.

## 2026-11-10: group files on the x86 PC: the data home had no storage provider
- `[files] FAIL ... Cursor.getCount() ... null` on the PC (main tree) was the roots query of `com.android.externalstorage.documents` with no
  provider for it: the lead data home (`~/apklayer-x86/main/data`) has no system apps installed (only the WebView; "remote providers
  registered: 10", no provider process started). Parallels' `apklayer-ks` data home without them fails the same; not an x86_64 bug.
- groups.toml: `files` now `needs` ExternalStorageProvider and MediaProvider (VOLUMES=1 reads MediaStore), so a data home without them SKIPs
  with the reason, as `volumes` and `system-apps` do. Checked in own trees with the prebuilts' system APKs installed: PC (x86_64 APKs) files
  PASS, system-apps all checks but the known `display` one; Parallels (arm64) files PASS; both SKIP files before the install.

## 2026-11-10: the phones' keyboards: field types, Enter, a selection's Backspace, showing it again (Maliit)
- Causes: the app told the shell only "a field has focus" (MSG_TEXT_INPUT {id, 0/1}), so Maliit always got free text with predictions (a
  password field too) and a plain Enter key, and a field-to-field move sent nothing. InputMethodManager.showSoftInput (TextView's tap in a
  focused field) stayed in Robolectric's shadow, so a keyboard the user swiped away came back only on the next focus change. The keyboard's
  Backspace arrives as an IME deletion (deleteSurroundingText(1, 0)), which by Android's contract leaves a selection alone; and Lomiri, never
  told the cursor or the selection, then restored the word it remembered (`ok go ` with `ok` selected became `go go `). Enter went to the app
  as KEYCODE_ENTER: a desktop Enter does type a newline (tests/vm/ime-fields.py); the phones' Enter now goes as an IME's.
- Fixes: HostTextBridge sends the focused field's EditorInfo on every focus change (MSG_TEXT_INPUT {id, 1, inputType, imeOptions}), state 2
  on showSoftInput (the shadow's visibility handler, reset after each call so every tap counts), state 3 with its text around the cursor
  (500 characters each side, cursor and anchor in UTF-16 units; none while composing, none for a password). The shell maps inputType to
  text-input-v3's content hint and purpose (textinput.c `text_input_kind`, now also sent to v3 compositors) and from that to Maliit's
  contentType/hiddenText/prediction/correction/autocapitalization (capitals only for CAP_* flags, as Android's keyboards), the Enter label
  (Go, Search, Send, Next, Done, Previous; none for IME_FLAG_NO_ENTER_ACTION) and the surrounding text, cursor, anchor and hasSelection
  (maliit.c sends the whole state each time: the server replaces it). Maliit's Enter goes as an IME commit of "\n" (in order with its
  commits), which HostTextBridge.ime turns into performEditorAction for a field with an action, as LatinIME does; an IME deletion with text
  selected deletes the selection (commitText("")).
- Phones, own instance `kbd` (the installed CLI, this tree's shell and host-classes.jar on a copy of runtime 0.4.11; the Sailfish shell built in
  the Sailfish build root; Sailfish's instance with `ro.hardware.egl mali`), hello.input's new email (IME_ACTION_GO), password and number fields,
  uinput taps, the compositors' screenshots. Volla X23 (Lomiri) and Volla Quintus (Lipstick) alike: `ok go `, a long press selects `ok`,
  Backspace -> ` go ` (one Backspace: the keyboard got hasSelection), Enter + `hi` -> `\nhi go `; the keyboard swiped down (`imInitiatedHide`,
  no keyboard in the screenshot), a tap in the field shows it again; the email field shows the `@`/`.com` layout with a `Go` key and its
  Enter runs action 2 (no newline), the password field `Done` and no predictions, the number field the digit pad.
- Tests: tests/vm/ime-fields.py (group input; test input `delete:<n>`): a key Enter and an IME "\n" type newlines in the multi-line field, an
  IME deletion with all selected empties it, an IME "\n" in the email field runs IME_ACTION_GO, the fields' EditorInfo and a tap's show request
  reach the shell. Group input passes on Parallels (own tree; the two runs after the last code change).

## 2026-11-10: Termux's shell without the sandbox: Android groups, program names, `ls -a`, its login's ~/.termux (bionic_translation 0061, 0062)
- `id` listed the desktop user's groups: bionic's getgroups now answers the groups zygote gives an app (0061; inet 3003 with INTERNET,
  everybody 9997, cache 20000+appId, shared 50000+appId: NativeLoader.appGroups, as the sandbox sets them), carried to the app's programs in
  `APKLAYER_OWN_DATA=<pkg>:<uid>,<gid>...:<root>`. Termux: `uid=10446 gid=10446 groups=10446,3003,9997,20446,50446`, Android's shape.
- Programs named themselves "apklayer" (android_platform.c's getprogname default): btload sets getprogname and glibc's
  program_invocation_name from the program's argv[0] before entering it, as bionic's libc init does (`ls: cannot access '/nope'`).
- `ls -a` listed no `.`/`..`: 0021 dropped them after a per-thread readdir buffer let a nested listing overwrite the entry Gecko's
  ScanStoreDir held. 0062 returns glibc's entry on LP64 (bionic's layout, in the stream's own buffer as bionic's): dots back, the held entry
  stays the stream's (tests/native/app-uid.c checks both).
- `ls` printed libjsig's "signal chaining ... deprecated": exec_preload.c drops HotSpot's libjsig from the LD_PRELOAD it gives programs.
- Termux's login printed "cannot create regular file .../home/.termux/": Termux builds its shell's environment itself, so its programs lost
  the path layer's variables (APKLAYER_DATA...), and `mkdir -p` walking /data/data then "com.termux" relative to it wrote into the shared
  native android-root's data/ (from_root). exec_preload.c now restores the path layer's variables with the runtime's, and paths.c makes
  the instance's /data/data/<pkg>, /data/user/0/<pkg>, /data/user_de/0/<pkg> links to the app's tree, so a walk lands there
  (tests/native/path-layer.c). Installs that ran Termux before may hold a stray `<native>/android-root/data/` tree: delete it.
- Termux 0.119.0-beta.3 (corpus APK) on UTM, own tree and prebuilts (SurfaceFlinger screenshots): login silent, ~/.termux/termux.properties
  created, `id` as above, `ls -la` with `.` and `..`, no libjsig line. Groups native, startup, files, activities pass on UTM.
- Changed native libraries: bionic_translation's libc_bio, libapklayer-preload (paths.c, exec_preload.c), libandroid_runtime (paths.c,
  app_native.c), btload; host classes (NativeLoader).

## 2026-11-10: the display mode is the screen too (system-apps `display` regression from 2026-11-09)
- Since the screen became the output (67a829ee), Display.getRealMetrics/getRealSize and the maximum window metrics followed the max bounds
  but Display.getMode() kept Robolectric's mode, the window's pixels (ShadowDisplayManager.createDisplayInfo): DisplayCheck failed with
  "mode differs from the size" (Parallels: real 768x1024, mode 325x579). HostConfiguration.apply now gives the default display one mode of
  the screen's size (turned like the window, so at rotation 0 it is the real size) before it applies the max bounds; without a shell all
  of them stay the window. DisplayCheck also compares getRealSize and getMaximumWindowMetrics.
- Checks: Parallels (own tree) system-apps, activities, window-geometry, mobile, gpu pass; x86 PC (own tree) system-apps, activities pass.

## 2026-11-10: release 0.4.12 (runtime 0.4.11)
- Halium phones (Sailfish OS, Ubuntu Touch): every native service crashed on 0.4.11 (libhybris's EGL bound to libdl_bio's `__loader_dlopen`, exported for bytehook in 0054, and the linker's gdb notification ran before btload's main): the `__loader_*` symbols are in a hidden version only bytehook's GOT lookup reaches (bionic_translation 0059) and no notification before main (0060); checked on the Volla Quintus and the Volla X23. Also runtime-bundle.sh no longer nests a tree when a link copy fails part way.

## 2026-11-10: Halium phones after 0.4.11: libdl's __loader_* no longer taken by libhybris (bionic_translation 0059, 0060)
- Regression in 0.4.11 on Sailfish (Volla Quintus) and Ubuntu Touch (Volla X23): every Android service of the stack exited with status 139.
  btload's constructor (resolve_gl_stubs) calls the device's libEGL.so.1 (libhybris), whose Android linker (q.so: android_dlopen calls
  `__loader_dlopen` through the PLT) bound to libdl_bio's `__loader_dlopen`, exported since 0054 for bytehook: the shim linker loaded the
  vendor's libraries before main and crashed in apkenv_link_image_one on the still-NULL `_r_debug_ptr` (gdb on the phone).
- 0059: the three `__loader_*` are exported under a hidden symbol version that unversioned references never bind to; libdl's GOT/PLT
  relocations still name them (bytehook). 0060: no gdb notification before `_r_debug_ptr` is set. plt-hook asserts the versioned export.
- Checks: Sailfish RPM (sailfish-rpm.sh, version 0.4.11 with this fix) + runtime 0.4.11 on the Quintus: the ut instance's services run,
  UiBench at depth=1, screenshot drawn; Ubuntu Touch click built on Parallels (click.sh) + runtime 0.4.11 on the X23: UiBench depth=1,
  screenshot drawn. Parallels (own tree): native, native-bridge, startup, activities pass; x86 PC (own tree): native, native-bridge pass.

## 2026-11-10: DocumentsUI's overlay NPE and first-start's blank capture
- DocumentsUI's Application.onCreate runs ThemeOverlayManager.applyOverlays: with CHANGE_OVERLAY_PACKAGES granted (now on both arches; UTM's old logs
  granted it later and skipped the call) its AsyncTask iterated OverlayManager.getOverlayInfosForTarget, which Robolectric's ShadowOverlayManager
  leaves to the null "overlay" proxy: an NPE (FATAL EXCEPTION, the app stayed up). ShadowHostOverlayManager answers as OverlayManagerService on a
  device without overlays: an empty list (getOverlayInfo null). DocumentsUI logs `OverlayManager.setEnabled() result: true`, no NPE (PC, Parallels).
- The blank DocumentsUI window (first-start 1 of 2 on the PC, "1 colours") was the test's timing, not the NPE: it captured 10 s after the boot
  settings, but FilesActivity's onCreate waits up to 5 s for ProvidersCache's first load, and the cold MediaProvider registers ~6 s after its start
  on the PC; FilesActivity started 6.8-7.0 s after the boot settings there (3 s to spare), 3.2-5.5 s on Parallels. first-start now captures 10 s
  after FilesActivity started (and says when that was). first-start: PC 9 of 9 (5 before the test change), Parallels 3 of 3.
- system-apps fails `display` on both hosts (mode differs from the size: PC 1920x2880 vs mode 834x1484, Parallels 768x1024 vs 325x579), the same
  with the overlay shadow taken out (Parallels): not this change; likely 67a829ee (real size = the shell's output). Every other check passes.

## 2026-11-10: input-inject's long presses wait for the app (the third long press that "ran no long click")
- Cause: the test, not the app. It held the finger until the long click ran but failed when the script's next steps (hover:8/11/14) came
  first. The steps run on the app's clock, which follows real time through a stall: under parallel load the app stalled around a press,
  its DOWN came after the next step (a Parallels log of 2026-11-09: hover:9 press, hover:10, then the DOWN, then hover:11) or with both
  remaining steps at once, and the long click due 400 ms after the DOWN came after the step the test took for its deadline. Reproduced by
  stopping the app's JVM for 2.8 s over the third press: DOWN, hover:13, hover:14 within 0.2 s, the long click 0.4 s later (the PLAN's
  signature: no long click, then the release as a tap). Unstalled, about 40 parallel runs (up to 5 input-inject beside activities) all ran it 0.40-0.42 s after the DOWN.
- Fix (tests/vm/input-inject.py): the steps that come during a press wait for its long click and action mode, then run in order, as a
  person goes on once the toolbar shows; the app's end is the deadline (an unreleased press or a missing action mode fails there). The
  hover:13 empty done now also runs when the third press was still held (it was skipped). With the JVM stopped 2.8 s over the first or third
  press, 1.8 s over the second, the test passes; Parallels, own tree ~/apklayer-lp: `tests/run input activities` 5 of 5.

## 2026-11-10: release 0.4.11 (runtime 0.4.11)
- Native bridge (x86_64 hosts, arm64 apps): a guest thread's pthread record maps to its host thread (binary_translation 0004, native_bridge_support 0001, bionic_translation 0058): Unity/il2cpp's GC no longer scans the wrong stack (PGR's "irrecoverable stack overflow" and black screen); Sailfish OS and Ubuntu Touch: the phone's own on-screen keyboard (Maliit) types into Android fields, with its height as the window's inset; the screen (max window metrics, real display size, the drawing cache limit) is the monitor, not the first window (FCL's guide overlay); headless runs without a shell no longer throw.

## 2026-11-09: the screen is the output, not the window (FCL's "too large to fit into a software layer")
- A tester's FCL log repeated `GuideOverlayView not displayed ... needs 19043840 bytes, only 2636288 available` (4 x 608 x 1084: the first,
  portrait window; the window had turned 1932x1084). Two causes: Robolectric's ShadowViewConfiguration answers the drawing cache limit as
  max(480x800, the context's window pixels) x 4, and its qualifiers make WindowConfiguration's max bounds the window (DeviceConfig.setBounds),
  so WindowManager.getMaximumWindowMetrics and Display.getRealSize/getRealMetrics were the window too. On Android all three are the screen.
- The shell's CONFIGURE carries the output's pixels (the largest output's mode; 9 ints, send_msg takes up to 16); AppHost.hostResize makes
  them the max bounds, turned like the window as a phone's display turns with it (at least the window); HostConfiguration re-applies them
  after each qualifiers change; ShadowHostDisplay.ViewConfigurationShadow answers ViewConfiguration's own value (4 x max bounds), and the
  ViewConfiguration cache is dropped once the screen is known (one made before the shell's first configure, cached per density, kept 480x800).
  The window's bounds (current metrics, display metrics, the qualifiers) are unchanged; without a shell (headless) nothing changes.
- Check: hello.acttarget logs max/real/window/cache, relaunch.py (group activities) asserts max = real = output px (Parallels: 1024x768,
  window 480x400, cache 3145728). Parallels, own tree ~/apklayer-a25: activities, gpu, window-geometry, mobile pass.

## 2026-11-09: Unity games through the native bridge: il2cpp's GC gets its own stack (PGR black screen on x86_64)
- Tester report (x86_64, NVIDIA, 0.4.7/0.4.9/0.4.10): Punishing: Gray Raven (arm64-only, Unity 2022.3 il2cpp) stayed black after MainActivity
  ("An irrecoverable stack overflow has occurred", later "double free or corruption (out)"). Reproduced on the x86 PC (Intel) with main:
  UnityMain died in il2cpp_init (SEGV_ACCERR, Unity's tombstone), the same with -Xss2m, so not a stack size. Not bionic_translation 0050-0057.
- Cause: il2cpp's Boehm GC asks pthread_getattr_np(pthread_self()) for its stack. Under the bridge a guest thread's pthread_self() is its
  own bionic record (binary_translation 0001), which the proxy handed to the host: glibc took it for the initial thread and the tid lookup
  failed, so the guest got the host main stack; and the guest's pthread_attr_getstack read glibc's attr, never the guest stack the proxy
  writes into bionic's fields. The GC scanned from its guest stack upwards into the first unmapped/guard page (HotSpot printed the
  "irrecoverable stack overflow" when that page was the JVM thread's red zone). With that fixed the GC's first stop-the-world aborted:
  `pthread_kill failed at suspend` (pthread_kill got the record too).
- Fix (generic): binary_translation 0004 (GuestPthreadToHost: a record to its host pthread_t), native_bridge_support 0001 (every proxied
  function taking a pthread_t maps it), bionic_translation 0058 (attr stack getters read bionic's fields). Test: hello-arm64 "own stack".
- Result on the x86 PC: PGR draws its Unity screens up to the game's data-download check ("Disk space is insufficient ... 43141 MB/13717 MB",
  the PC's free space; stopped there, nothing downloaded). Groups native-bridge, native-bridge-vulkan, startup, native, gpu pass (x86 PC).
  The tester's "double free or corruption (out)" did not occur on the PC (neither before the fix, where the GC fault came first, nor after).
- Libraries: Build log; libberberis_arm64 and libberberis_proxy_libc (x86_64) are in the lock, not yet in the prebuilts branch.

## 2026-11-09: the bionic linker no longer keeps a copy of every library (bionic_translation 0055): phone instance 1387 -> 785 MB

  region (composer 47 MB, SurfaceFlinger 40 MB) holding libGLES_mali.so's bytes: `apkenv_load_library` read each whole library file into a

  | before | 1385-1387 MB | 991 MB | 395-398 | 310 | 278 | 105 | 75 | 48 | 17 |

## 2026-11-09: Termux reaches its shell without the sandbox: the app's uid for Android code (bionic_translation 0056, 0057)
- Without the sandbox every app process runs as the desktop user (uid 1000 on UTM: Android's system uid); Termux's bootstrap second
  stage stops on `id -u` below 10000. 0056: `libc_bio_set_app_uid` makes bionic's getuid/geteuid/getgid/getegid/getresuid/getresgid
  answer the app uid (gid = uid) and stat/lstat/fstat/fstatat/statx report the real uid's (gid's) files as the app uid's. The path
  layer sets it with the own-data mapping (`APKLAYER_OWN_DATA=<pkg>:<uid>:<root>`, inherited by the app's programs), only when the
  real uid is not the app's (the sandbox: nothing changes). Java agrees: libcore's stat maps the owner the same way, `Os.getuid` and
  family return `Process.myUid()` (Robolectric left them 0).
- Then `ln` crashed at exit (SIGSEGV, exit 139): gnulib's close_stdin reads bionic's FILE fields on `stdin`, which was glibc's FILE.
  0057: bionic's stdin/stdout/stderr point at the `__sF` entries the stdio wrappers already map to glibc's streams, laid out as an idle
  bionic FILE; fscanf/fwprintf/fwscanf wrappers added.
- Termux 0.119.0-beta.3 (corpus APK) on UTM: bootstrap installed, the welcome text and a `~ $` prompt; `id` prints uid=10000
  gid=10000, `ls -la ..` lists home and usr owned by 10000 (SurfaceFlinger screenshots). Leftovers in docs/PLAN.md "After the
  releases" 1. Tests: tests/native/app-uid.c, std-streams.c, path-layer.c (uid in the child's environment); groups native,
  native-bridge, startup, activities, files pass on UTM.

## 2026-11-09: bytehook works under bionic_translation; FCL's crash screen after a game crash (bionic_translation 0054)
- bytehook (ByteDance's PLT hook library; FCL 1.3.4.5 ships 1.0.10) returned 3 (INITERR_SYM): it reads the linker's ELF at
  getauxval(AT_BASE) and its .symtab from /system/bin/linker64 (the host's ld.so: stripped, and not the linker of Android code).
  Then it needs libdl.so in dl_iterate_phdr as an ELF whose dlopen/dlclose reach __loader_* through GOT slots (its dlopen monitor;
  without the monitor every hook task fails), dladdr agreeing with that report (it verifies a GOT value before patching), and
  .dynamic pointers relative to the load bias (glibc relocates them in place: bytehook crashed reading libc_bio's at twice the bias).
- What changed, Android-accurate where our linker's structures allow: libdl_bio is the linker of Android code. Its dl entries call
  exported `__loader_*` through the GOT (linked `-z now`, as Android binds); dl_iterate_phdr reports it as /system/lib64/libdl.so
  (its real phdrs, an unrelocated copy of .dynamic) and dladdr names it so; bionic getauxval(AT_BASE) is its ELF header and the path
  layer serves /system/bin/linker64 as its file (it keeps .symtab; debian/rules no longer strips it). The other host libraries stay
  listed for unwinders but without PT_DYNAMIC: one of FCL's hooks had patched the path layer's dlsym import (a bytehook trampoline in
  its GOT), whose dlsym(RTLD_NEXT) then answered NULL and the game JVM jumped to 0 in statfs. `bionic___cfi_slowpath(_diag)` answer dlsym on libdl
  (bytehook's arm64 init at API >= 26 patches a return into both; INITERR_CFI before). Nothing faked from Android's linker internals:
  no g_dl_mutex (bytehook finds bionic_translation's mutex layout incompatible and uses its thread-local count, as it is built to).
- Checked: bytehook 1.0.10 built from source with NDK 30 (x86_64, arm64; API 26 and 29, i.e. its pre-O `dlopen` monitor and its O+
  `__loader_*` one), a test program under btload: init 0, a hook_single on a library dlopened after it, hook_all("exit") with
  CALL_PREV (x86 PC and Parallels). FCL on the x86 PC: "Successfully initialized exit hook", Minecraft 26.3 to the title screen,
  F3+C held 12 s (test input) -> "Game crashed!", JVMCrashActivity in `:crash` with the game's log; its Exit -> the launcher comes back
  (MainActivity in a new process). Test tests/native/plt-hook.c (group native). Groups native, native-bridge, startup, activities pass
  (x86 PC, own tree); native (bionic suite) passes on Parallels arm64 (own lean tree, removed). Not checked: FCL on arm64 (Parallels has
  no Vulkan for the game); Flatpak's debuginfo step may strip libdl_bio's .symtab (bytehook would return 3 there).

## 2026-11-09: android.os.SharedMemory is real: a memfd, mappable in the process it is sent to (docs/PLAN.md "libcore on HotSpot")
- Robolectric's ShadowSharedMemory kept a temp file per create and mapped only its own files, so a SharedMemory from another process failed
  ("Cannot find the backing file", and its size lookup threw). ShadowHostSharedMemory: nCreate is a memfd (Os.memfd_create, now real through the
  runtime shim's libcore.io.Linux natives instead of Robolectric's temp file), nGetSize the descriptor's size, nSetProt without PROT_WRITE seals
  future writes (F_SEAL_FUTURE_WRITE, libcutils' ashmem on memfd). map() cannot build libcore's DirectByteBuffer(int,long,FileDescriptor,Runnable,
  boolean) on HotSpot: it maps the descriptor reopened through /proc/self/fd with FileChannel.map (the JDK's mapped buffer, MAP_SHARED, freed by
  its cleaner); unmap() runs that cleaner. No new native mapping code: Os.mmap is not among the runtime's real Linux natives.
- Test: binder (hello-binder-server/client) passes a SharedMemory written and sealed by the server; the client maps it read-only, checks the text,
  is refused a writable mapping, and checks two mappings of its own region share memory.

## 2026-11-09: window manager semantics for hidden and relaunched activities' windows
- A stopped activity's windows are hidden as WindowManagerService does: through their app visibility (ViewRootImpl.dispatchAppVisibility to
  the decor, the dialogs and the popups over them), not by setting their views INVISIBLE; a window added while its activity is hidden comes
  up hidden (ShadowWindowManagerGlobal.addToDisplay leaves out ADD_FLAG_APP_VISIBLE: Robolectric 0026, runtime/patches AppVisibility). The run
  loop's scan for windows added to stopped activities (HostWindows.hideAddedToStopped) is gone. As on Android a hidden dialog keeps
  `isShown()` true and reports `getWindowVisibility()` GONE; Dialog.show() on a hidden dialog no longer makes it visible again, and an app's own
  Dialog.hide() is no longer undone at restart.
- Activities stack in their task's order (WindowOrder.activityOrder: start order, registered at launch) instead of the add order of their base
  window: a relaunched activity (a configuration change; Robolectric's recreation gives it a new token and a new window) keeps its place. A
  paused activity behind a translucent one was drawn and tapped over it after a font-scale change (AppHost iterates the stack top first, so the
  lower activity's new window came last). A recreated activity that is not the top one loses the focus Robolectric's recreation gives it.
- Tests (input group, Parallels ~/apklayer-dlg): stopped-windows checks the late dialog GONE (isShown true) and off the screen;
  `stopped-windows.py translucent` (hello.input's Translucent): a translucent activity over a paused one with a dialog shown before and one
  shown while paused, then `apklayer settings set font-scale 1.3` relaunching both: red on the screen and both taps reach the translucent one
  (without the relaunch fix it fails: the paused activity drawn over it). input and activities pass; input-inject's third long press failed
  beside activities on this tree and on the tree without these changes alike (the open input-inject item).

## 2026-11-09: an empty IME done no longer deletes a selection (input-inject's third long press)
- Root cause: the desktop's input method (GNOME's IBus) answers each text-input enable with a `done` that carries no preedit and no commit.
  HostTextBridge turned every such done into `setComposingText("")`, which with no composing region replaces the selection
  (BaseInputConnection.replaceText): the word a long press had selected was deleted and its action mode ended. In parallel runs the seat's
  keyboard focus came back to input-inject's window when another group's window closed (`keyboard enter`, `text input enabled` in the shell
  log), right after a long press: the field emptied, and Back had no action mode to end (or, earlier, the next step found no selection).
- Fix: as zwp_text_input_v3 defines done (only an existing preedit is replaced), the empty preedit removes only a preedit the IME placed
  in that field; otherwise a done without text is no edit.
- input-inject sends such an empty done during the third long press's selection and checks the selection and action mode survive it
  (fails without the fix, every run), and that a preedit the IME cancels still goes. UTM, own tree: input-inject alone 5 of 5 pass;
  `tests/run input activities` 3 of 3 pass (before: input failed 2 of 2 in that pairing). The long press already releases on the app's long click, not after app-clock steps (2026-11-08); no hang
  or deadlock reproduced (no silent app in these runs).

## 2026-11-09: Sailfish OS (Volla Quintus): Lipstick's switcher, its close, media = device, the Back-only bar
- Switcher: every apklayer window is the session shell's (one Wayland client, one pid), and Lipstick ties a window to its launcher entry by the
  client's pid and the entry's Exec (JollaSystemInfo.isWindowForLauncherItem, matchingPidForCommand), except for entries with
  `X-apkd-packageName` (Jolla's AppSupport), which it ties by the window's wl_shell class (read from the lipstick binary: the key, then
  `QWaylandSurface::className`). The shell already sets the class to the package and the title to the app's name, so app entries now carry
  `X-apkd-packageName=<package>` (cli/desktop.cpp); `run`/`list` rewrite a Sailfish entry without it. With the key Lipstick's uninstall of such an
  entry asks com.jolla.apkd (absent: it fails, as the not-RPM-owned entry did before) and its removal calls apkd's forceStopApp (absent, ignored).
  How the covers look (titles, icons, one per app) needs a human check: the phone is locked with a code and its display off, so Lipstick's
  screenshot (`devel-su -p`, works) is blank.
- Close from Lipstick: a cover's close is `QWaylandSurface::destroySurface`, which sends qt_extended_surface.close since the shell makes an
  extended surface; the shell sends the app MSG_CLOSE, the message the desktop title bar's close sends. New test input `TEST_CLOSE` (kind 8,
  tests/lib/instance.py `CLOSE`) drives that path; the shell logs `closed by the compositor`. On the phone: UiBench beside Files (Files launched
  by `lca-tool --triggerdesktop`, Lipstick's invoker); closing Files ended its JVM, its `apklayer run` and its invoker (`the desktop closed the
  window`, `ending: window closed`), UiBench kept running; then the same for UiBench.
- media = device: hello.nativeplayer (`red-h264-320x240.mp4`) decoded through `c2.mtk.avc.decoder` (device logcat), then SurfaceFlinger
  crashed (SIGSEGV in /vendor/lib64/libgpu_aux.so under libGLES_mali, after `GuiExtAuxCreateBufferQueue: Cannot get mms_hidl service`): MediaTek's
  Mali driver samples the decoder's YUV buffers through libgpu_aux, which needs `vendor.mediatek.hardware.mms@1.x::IMms`; the instance's libhidl
  looked for it in its own (absent) hwservicemanager, and on this phone the service is `oneshot disabled` (init starts it on demand) and never
  ran. Fix (cli/providers.cpp, cli/device_hals.cpp): graphics = device also lists `vendor.mediatek.hardware.mms` in host_hals.conf, and the stack
  asks the device's init to start the declared HIDL HALs it lists (`setprop ctl.interface_start <fqname>`, what Android's libhidl does for a lazy
  HAL; a running one stays as it is; works as the desktop user, no SELinux there). Checked with mms stopped first: the stack started it,
  `found on the host`, PLAYER PASS, SurfaceFlinger's screenshot red, SurfaceFlinger kept running. The X23 (Helio G99) never reached libgpu_aux.
- Back-only bar (main after 0.4.9): the shell of this tree (built in the Sailfish build root, glibc 2.30) composes the bar into the virtual
  display (`navigation bar composed by the display (no subsurfaces)`); SurfaceFlinger's screenshot shows Back alone; a tap on Back closed Files'
  open drawer (test input touch).
- How it ran: the shell and CLI of this tree from a private overlay (`APKLAYER_BUILD`/`APKLAYER_ROOT`, /userdata/apklayer/agent-overlay), the
  RPM's files untouched; the overlay, the test app and the edited entries were removed afterwards, mms stopped again, instance ut stopped. Not run:
  test groups on the VMs (the shell change is one test-input case and a log line).

## 2026-11-09: activities of another android:process run in a process of their own; Process.killProcess ends only that process
- An activity whose android:process differs from the caller's process starts through the hub at once (ShadowHostInstrumentation, any thread, as
  Android's startActivity is a call to the system), in a window process keyed `<package>#<process>`: `apklayer run` with APKLAYER_PROCESS
  (the spare takes it), its own window and log (`app-<package>:<suffix>.log`), Application created, same uid, its own providers only
  (HostProcessName.windowProcess). The caller is stopped below it and resumed once it finishes or its process dies; startActivityForResult
  works across processes; a window process that dies answers its callers RESULT_CANCELED (HubActivities.died; receivers take one answer).
- Process.killProcess(myPid) ends the JVM at once (SIGKILL's effect, not Robolectric's record); another pid only when the hub lists it as
  this app's. An activity below the killed process's top one comes back in a new process once no window of the app is up (RESTART_ON_DEATH,
  as ActivityManagerService restarts a dead process's stopped activity; no saved state, only that one activity). A kill right after a local
  activity start stays ApklayerExit's restart idiom. getRunningAppProcesses lists the app's processes with their names (RUNNING_PROCESSES).
- Per-process script steps: `-Dapklayer.script.<suffix>`; `apklayer screenshot <package>:<suffix>`. The hub's app launches append to the log
  the CLI tees into (it truncated it under the CLI's writes).
- FCL 1.3.4.5 on the x86 PC (own tree ~/apklayer-x86/proc, a copy of the fcl tree's xf1): JVMActivity is not in `:jvm` (that is GameService,
  a foreground service); `:crash` is JVMCrashActivity. Quit Game on the title screen -> GameMenu.onExit -> killProcess(myPid) -> the launcher
  (MainActivity) comes back in a new process; the second launch reaches the title screen and quits again the same way. The crash screen runs
  in `:crash` (started by an intent to the running launcher) and its Exit ends only that process, the launcher stays. A real game crash
  (F3+C debug crash) does not reach it: the guest JVM's exit(-1) is FCL's to hook through bytehook, whose init fails (INITERR_SYM), so the
  process just exits (gdb: guest exit(-1), then the host JVM's exit). Test app hello-act-process (`hub.py processes kill`, group activities).
