- x86 PC (Intel N100, ANV), FCL 1.3.4.5 x86_64 (its GitHub release APK, native, no bridge) in an own tree (~/apklayer-x86/fcl, instance xf1),
  the game data (.minecraft with 26.3, assets, libraries, account) copied from Parallels (untouched there), FCL's x86_64 JREs from its assets.
  Scripted taps: Launch, FCL's Vulkan check, "Krypton Wrapper does not support this version" -> Continue; Minecraft picks its Vulkan backend
  on "Intel(R) Graphics (ADL-N)" with VK_KHR_android_surface and VK_KHR_swapchain through our loader; title screen, Singleplayer, a new world
  ("steve joined the game"), the touch D-pad moves the player (the tutorial advances), sound streams reach PipeWire ("apklayer playback").
- bionic_translation 0053: OpenAL Soft aborted the game right after its context ("OpenJDK exited with code : 6"): Android code calling
  strerror_r got glibc's GNU variant (char *) and read the pointer as an error number; `bionic_strerror_r` is POSIX's now.
- Debug aid: APKLAYER_PTRACER=1 lets an unprivileged gdb attach to app JVMs (Yama ptrace_scope 1; the hub starts them). Found the abort.
- Not a bug of ours: Krypton (FCL's GL renderer) fails Minecraft 26.x's "glGetError mismatch" check (SDL's eglGetProcAddress against
  LWJGL's glXGetProcAddress of gl4es); FCL itself warns that Krypton does not support this version; Vulkan is the backend. Parallels has no
  GPU Vulkan (its host's only device is llvmpipe, `vulkaninfo`), and llvmpipe gets no swapchain (present is opt-in there), so the game has no
  backend on Parallels.
- Open: quitting the game (or its crash) leaves the last frame: FCL runs JVMActivity in `:jvm` and JVMCrashActivity in `:crash` and ends the
  game with Process.killProcess(myPid); apklayer runs a package's activities in one process and killProcess is Robolectric's no-op, so the
  crash screen never starts and a second game launch would need a second JVM in the same process. Needs activities of another android:process
  in their own app process (docs/PLAN.md). OSHI's JNA probe fails (libGLESv3_CM.dll, only SystemReport's info). Groups gpu, gpu-window,
  native-surface, native, native-bridge, vulkan pass on the PC (own slots xf3/xf4).

## 2026-11-09: FCL's game aborted on a second copy of a system library (bionic_translation 0052)
- Owner's run with the EGL fix: past JLI_Launch, MioLibPatcher and the Vulkan load, then `descriptor_database.cc:678] File already exists
  in database: frameworks/av/drm/libmediadrm/protos/metrics.proto` and an abort: libmediadrmmetrics_full's constructor ran a second time.
- The shim linker matched loaded libraries by base name and namespace only; the same file opened by another path or from another namespace
  (0051 opens absolute paths from the app namespace) became a second copy. 0052 reuses a loaded file found by st_dev/st_ino, across
  namespaces, as Android's linker does, and prints `[bionic_linker] <path>: already loaded as <path> (app N vendor N)` on stderr when it does:
  the next run shows which load it was. Not reproduced outside FCL; bionic suite (10) passes, the JRE under btload and in-process runs.
- Installed into ~/apklayer-fc on Parallels; test groups not run yet (the owner checks FCL first).

## 2026-11-09: FCL's game hung at its first GL frame: lock order in apklayer's EGL fixed
- Seen by the lead (owner's run, gdb attach read-only): RenderThread held EglWindow.cpp's windowsLock in swapWindow and called
  `host<>("eglSwapBuffers")`, a bionic dlsym that waits for the shim linker's lock; the game thread held that lock (inside a dlopen, a
  library constructor) and waited for an EGL lock. Every `host<>`/`gl<>`/`gbmFn<>` call did a dlsym, many under windowsLock.
- libapklayer_egl: the host's EGL, GL and GBM entry points are resolved once in the library's constructor (Egl.cpp kHostNames, a name
  missing from the list is logged and looked up per call); Gles1.cpp fills its hook tables outside its lock (published atomically). A static
  local's first initialisation (hostGles1/2, translator) still loads a library under its guard: only on a thread's first ES table fill.
- bionic_translation keeps apkenv_dl_lock across constructors, as Android's linker keeps its own: not changed.
- Built for arm64 only (Build log); installed into ~/apklayer-fc on Parallels. Test groups not run yet (the owner checks FCL first).

## 2026-11-09: FCL's game JVM gets past VM init (bionic_translation 0050, 0051)
- Cause of "Error occurred during initialization of VM: java.lang.StackOverflowError at java.lang.String.<clinit>" (and of a SIGBUS at a
  garbage pc in a later try): `pthread_attr_getguardsize` had no bionic_ wrapper, so FCL's JRE 25 (bionic HotSpot) ran glibc's on the
  bionic attr; it read bionic's stack_size field (0038 fills it) as the guard, so HotSpot computed a stack of size 0 for its main thread
  and protected 16 KB above the stack (over the next mapping: libjvm.so's own start under btload). 0050 wraps the remaining attr
  functions (guard size, scope, inheritsched) and fixes setstack's wrapper (misspelt `settstack`). Test: tests/native/attr-stack.c.
- 0051: an absolute path dlopened from the app namespace opens outside the search path (Android allows the app's data): FCL's DLOPEN of
  every jre25/lib/*.so failed with "library ... not found"; the search path buffers hold 32 directories in 4096 bytes (were 8 in 512).
- Checked on Parallels without FCL (its JRE copied into a test slot): the JRE's `java -version` under btload crashed (SIGSEGV in the shim
  linker on libjvm's PROT_NONE start) and runs after 0050; FCL's JRE 25 started by JLI_Launch inside our HotSpot 21 (libjsig preloaded,
  FFM harness) runs a program with its own implicit null checks, StackOverflowErrors on two threads and GC while the host JVM takes
  NullPointerExceptions on another thread: both JVMs' faults reach their own handlers (the guest finds libjsig through RTLD_DEFAULT and
  installs first; host faults reach the host through libjsig's chain). bytehook_init's 3 (INITERR_SYM, as bytehook's codes read):
  FCL logs "Failed to initialize native hooks!" and goes on to JLI_Launch; what those hooks do in the game was not checked. Groups native, native-bridge, native-surface, startup pass (Parallels, own tree).
- Not yet seen: the game in FCL after the fix (the owner drives FCL; their result is the next step).

## 2026-11-09: `apklayer stop` ends a shell a racing launch started (hub-services' leftover apklayer-shell on Parallels)
- cli/stop.cpp: the shell was ended only before the hub; a launch racing the stop could start a new one, and the final sweep spares the session's shell, so it outlived the stop (hub-smoke: `assert not left`, also at -j 1). The stop now checks again after the sweep (endShellUnlessShared: kept while another instance's stack runs). Parallels: hub-services passes 2 of 2 after it (failed 2 of 2 before), sysprov and the Settings round's groups pass.

## 2026-11-09: Camera1 (android.hardware.Camera) apps get rotate-and-crop, also when the window turns while the camera is open (docs/PLAN.md "Camera")
- Cause: Camera.open and Camera.getCameraInfo read the app's CameraCompatibilityInfo in JNI (android_content_res_CameraCompatibilityInfo_toNative)
  through field ids that register_android_content_res_CameraCompatibilityInfo sets; apklayer's runtime never called it, so Camera1 passed
  garbage and cameraserver's Camera2Client never turned the frames (portrait windows showed the scene sideways). bridge/native/runtime_shim.c
  registers it with the other camera natives. camera2 passes the compat info as a parcel and was not affected.
- A Camera1 app that sets its display orientation in onConfigurationChanged did it before cameraserver changed the open camera's mode, so
  Camera2Client kept the rotate-and-crop preview transform in landscape (bars turned on screen). AppHost.hostResize now tells cameraserver the
  window shape before the configuration change, and HostCameraShadows sends WINDOW_SHAPE as a two-way call (returns after
  notifyDisplayConfigurationChange). Without the reorder the landscape screenshot fails ("black black yellow ..."); Service.cpp's comment only.
- Tests: hello.camera's Portrait takes -Dhello.camera1 (Camera.open, the preview on the TextureView, NV21 preview-callback frames, the
  documented setDisplayOrientation value from onConfigurationChanged); camera-portrait.py with CAMERA1=1 (group camera, a third step):
  NV21 frames turned/upright/turned, SurfaceFlinger screenshots of the back camera in portrait, landscape and portrait again, and the front
  one. Parallels (own tree): camera passed 6 of 7 runs (one failed "the preview is not upright and cropped", the step not recorded),
  camera-pipewire passes (one earlier failure: the app stalled reading a jar under load 11). x86 PC (x86pc, -j 2): camera passes (the first
  run after the full rebuild started no app, the rerun passed).

## 2026-11-09: the lazy extractor stays while its objects are held (native-media flaky on Parallels, DEAD_OBJECT)
- Cause, confirmed in the failing runs' logs (Parallels and the x86 PC): only the extractor ended (stack.log `exited: extractor (status 0)`,
  its registrar's `has clients: 0`) while the media server stayed. MediaExtractorFactory::CreateFromService drops media.extractor once it has
  an IMediaExtractor, so the app's AMediaExtractor and the media server's FrameDecoder kept IMediaExtractor/IMediaSource proxies to a process
  that exited: `-32 (DEAD_OBJECT)`, short audio, wrong samples. LazyServiceRegistrar counts the registered services' clients only.
- Fix (aosp/hals/media/services/MediaServices.cpp, libapklayer_mediaserver): the extractor also stays while one of its binder nodes created
  after registration has a strong reference (BINDER_GET_NODE_DEBUG_INFO on libbinder's driver fd, which IPCThreadState::setupPolling hands
  out; the kernel's and apklayer-binderd's both answer it); the registrar's callback checks before it unregisters, and a thread rechecks every
  5 s, since servicemanager does not report the handed-out objects' end. It still ends 5-10 s after the last object goes (stack.log: an extractor
  start and exit per run, kept across several client cycles in one run).
- Not changed, checked: the media server, cameraserver and codec2 keep the plain registrar. Their clients hold the service for their process's
  life (IMediaDeathNotifier and MediaMetadataRetriever::sService for media.player, CameraBase's gCameraService and CameraManagerGlobal,
  Codec2Client's Cache), and the media server's audioserver callbacks would pin it forever under a node check. On a device none of these
  services is lazy (init starts them).
- Memory: nothing changes when idle (the extractor is not running; the phone's 974 MB of PLAN item 8 stands); while an app holds an
  extractor or a track the extractor (~55 MB on the phone, as each media server) stays until the app lets go, as on a device.
- Tests, Parallels (~/apklayer-lzx, the new library in its own prebuilts copy), lazy services on: before the fix native-media 2 of 4 failed
  (`H.264 frame null`, `AAC decoded sample count 0`); after it 6 of 6 pass; codec2, native-player, camera, audio-system, startup and
  hub-services pass (run times of 12-42 s for native-media follow the VM's load from other trees, load average ~8). x86 PC (~/apklayer-x86/lzx,
  -j 2): native-media failed before (`Failed to instantiate extractor`, the extractor had exited), passes 3 of 3 with the x86_64 library.
- Libraries (not in the prebuilts yet; both locks set): see the Build log.

## 2026-11-09: setup checks the free space; apklayer's data on /userdata on a phone with a small home (Sailfish, the Quintus at 0.4.9)
- The 0.4.9 update on the Volla Quintus had 211 MB free on its 6.8 GB system partition (the home); the lead moved `~/.local/share/apklayer` to
  `/userdata/apklayer/share` by hand. Now: common/fetch.cpp `runtimeRoom` compares statvfs's free space of the runtime's directory with what the
  bundle needs (its size and unpacked size from releases.json before downloading, the unpacked size in the bundle's zstd frame before unpacking;
  a tenth more), fails early with what is needed, what is free and where, and the command that moves the data; when only removing the runtimes
  other than the current one makes room it removes them first and says so (never the current one). The bundle downloads beside the runtime
  (`runtime/.downloads`), not into ~/.cache, so one file system is checked. runtime-bundle.sh records the unpacked size (`zstd --stream-size`),
  releases-json.py lists `size` and `unpacked_size`; older bundles are counted by decompressing.
- `apklayer setup --move-data <dir>` (cli/datamove.cpp): copies `~/.local/share/apklayer` to `<dir>`, then puts a symlink in its place (no instance
  may run). The first setup does it by itself when the home's file system is below 16 GB and `/userdata/apklayer` is writable on another one
  (the RPM's `%post` makes it, mode 1777): `/userdata/apklayer/<user>/share`. Existing installs are never moved without the command; a link made by
  hand is used as it is. Ubuntu Touch: nothing (its /home is on userdata).
- Tests: manager/tests/updater_test.cpp (fake statvfs: refusal, the old runtime removed, current kept, the frame's size; its bundles now have the
  parts runtimeProblem wants, so the unpack checks pass again): 38 ok on Parallels. tests/vm/setup-space.sh on Parallels (a 100 MB loop-mounted data home,
  the branch's CLI against the 0.4.7 deb): 120 MB refused before unpacking with the move command, the old runtime removed to fit, current kept,
  `--move-data` and a second move refused, the big bundle fits afterwards, a first setup with /userdata/apklayer moves there: PASS.

## 2026-11-09: MediaMetadataRetriever's H.264 frames (thumbnails: Gallery, file managers, MediaStore)
- getFrameAtTime/getScaledFrameAtTime returned no picture for H.264 (`avdec_h264: No valid frames decoded before end of stream`, the
  Codec2 work C2_CORRUPTED). Cause, in GStreamer (reproduced with a plain appsrc ! decodebin pipeline, no apklayer code): the codec-config
  buffer (SPS/PPS, which ReorderedDecoder queued as its own buffer) is an access unit without a slice; avdec_h264's frame threads report it
  as a decode error, and when it surfaces only in the end-of-stream drain, the drain stops and every pending picture is dropped. That is
  the case when a stream has fewer units than decoder threads: FrameDecoder's one sync sample with end of stream, or a few samples.
  Parallels (12 CPUs) lost the picture of any stream under 12 units; the x86 PC (4 CPUs, ffmpeg's thread count for the size) did not.
  Fix (bridge/hals/media/codec.c, the host library: no Android rebuild): config buffers (negative timestamp) are held and sent with
  the next access unit. VP8/VP9 have no config buffers (a single frame with end of stream decodes); HEVC and AV1 are AOSP's soft decoders.
- Tests: codec-reorder-test (codec2 group) decodes SPS/PPS then one sample with end of stream (fails before the fix on Parallels);
  hello-native-codec's retriever check (native-media) asserts a red H.264 frame from getFrameAtTime, getScaledFrameAtTime and
  OPTION_CLOSEST at 200 ms (the shortcut is gone; failed before the fix). codec2, native-media, native-player pass on Parallels and on
  the x86 PC (-j 2).
- Found on the way, not fixed: with the lazy media services (default since today) native-media is flaky on Parallels (about one run in
  two: `FrameDecoder: source failed to start: -32 (DEAD_OBJECT)`, `extracted sample bytes/PTS`, audio checks with short output). The
  extractor (and the media server) end once no client holds the service handle, while clients still use objects from them
  (IMediaExtractor/IMediaSource, IMediaMetadataRetriever): LazyServiceRegistrar counts only the registered services. With
  `APKLAYER_LAZY_SERVICES=0` three runs in three pass. PLAN "Next phase" item.

## 2026-11-09: FCL's androidnsbypass crash on arm64 fixed (bionic_translation 0049); FCL starts its game JVM
- Owner's crash (Parallels, FCL's Vulkan check loading libandroidnsbypass.so): on aarch64 its constructor finds the linker's private API by
  scanning dlopen/android_dlopen_ext/dlclose/dlsym for their first `bl` (offset read unsigned) and calls the target as __loader_<name>; our
  C entries in libdl_bio began with backward PLT calls, so it mprotected and jumped into the JDK's lib/modules mapping (SIGSEGV). bionic_translation
  0049: the four entries are asm laid out as Android's libdl (bti, frame, caller in the next register, forward `bl` to a stub that branches to
  apkenv_loader_<name>, which takes the private API's arguments); other architectures keep the C entries. Test: tests/native/loader-entry.c
  (group native) runs the same scan and calls the targets.
- FCL on Parallels after it: the Vulkan check passes (Venus, Vulkan 1.4.318), Launch of 26.3 (downloaded by the owner in FCL) opens
  JVMActivity with its touch controls, the in-process game JVM (JRE 25, JLI_Launch through bionic_translation) starts and fails at once:
  "Error occurred during initialization of VM: java.lang.StackOverflowError at java.lang.String.<clinit>", and its exit ends the app
  process. Also seen: dlopen of the JRE's libraries by absolute path in app data (jre25/lib/server/libjvm.so, ...) fails in the shim linker
  (a path outside its search path), FCL then finds them another way; bytehook_init returns 3.

## 2026-11-09: storage while the hub is down, bounded media watches, drives in the sandbox (docs/PLAN.md "Open by area", Storage)
- Catch-up at hub start (HubVolumes.watch, HubMediaWatch): the hub records the drives mounted as it last saw them (`<instance>/volumes-mounted`)
  and the time up to which every change went to MediaProvider's scanner (`<instance>/media-scanned`, written after each flush). A hub start that
  is not a boot compares: a drive mounted meanwhile is announced as on a hot plug (broadcasts, callbacks, ExternalStorageService scans it), a
  removed one ejected/unmounted/removed; on internal storage and the drives still mounted every folder and file whose mtime is at or after the kept
  time (2 s slack: vfat) goes to the scanner (ACTION_MEDIA_SCANNER_SCAN_FILE; a folder's scan finds its deleted entries too). The first start of an
  instance (nothing kept) announces its drives and has ExternalStorageService scan internal storage, as a first boot; a boot (always-on) leaves it to
  HubBootBroadcasts as before (HubBootBroadcasts.pending).
- Bounded inotify (HubMediaWatch): fanotify's FAN_MARK_FILESYSTEM needs CAP_SYS_ADMIN (unprivileged fanotify, 5.13+, has inode marks only), so
  inotify stays, with a budget (APKLAYER_MEDIA_WATCHES, else a quarter of fs.inotify.max_user_watches, at least 64): a volume's top and its media
  folders (DCIM, Pictures, Movies, Music, Download, Documents, ...) first, the rest while it lasts. What is over it is scanned at the next hub start or
  mount; while any folder is unwatched (or inotify dropped events) the kept time stays at this hub's start. `apklayer status` shows
  `media watch  N directories watched, M not (budget B of inotify's L per user; ...)` from `<state>/media-watch`.
- Sandbox (APKLAYER_SANDBOX=1): the drives reach a sandboxed app through apklayerd's file broker, as the granted home does: APKLAYER_BROKER_HOME
  is a ':' list (the home when granted, `/media/<user>`, `/run/media/<user>`), and apklayerd (cli/broker.cpp) serves a drive path to an app holding a
  storage permission (READ/WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE, READ_MEDIA_*, granted or a legacy app's) or declaring MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE or
  WRITE_MEDIA_STORAGE (MediaProvider), read and write as the user. HostVolumes uses USER when the JDK's user.name is "?" (an app uid has no
  passwd entry: the drives were looked for under `/media/?`). Also fixed: sandboxed processes had no APKLAYER_STORAGE since 2026-11-07 (the CLI
  drops it at its start, cli/main.cpp, and sandbox-exec is the CLI), so they saw the host paths for /storage and MediaProvider found no volume for
  them; sandboxSpawn hands it on as it does LD_PRELOAD (APKLAYER_SANDBOX_STORAGE).
- Mounts outside `/media/<user>` and `/run/media/<user>` stay out (documented in PLAN: udisks2 would list fstab and /mnt mounts too, which are the
  system's).
- Test: group `volumes` gains the catch-up (tests/vm/volumes.py, hello-files' `hello.hotplug=catchup`): with the instance stopped a drive is
  mounted and a picture written on internal storage, the next start lists the drive and indexes its DCIM/pre.png and the picture; then both are
  removed while stopped and the start after has neither; those two run with a budget of 3 and read `apklayer status` (directories not watched).
  New optional group `volumes-sandbox` (the same with APKLAYER_SANDBOX=1). hello-files touches MediaStore before MediaStore.Images.Media: as the
  first MediaStore use, the nested class's initialization crashed the app JVM (SIGSEGV in ConstantPool::klass_at_impl, Class.getDeclaringClass0
  under Robolectric's ShadowMap; PLAN Storage). Hotplug skips what the hub's start announced before the mount (a drive an earlier run left in
  the record), and accepts the instance's /storage at its host path. volumes.py: when the umount is busy it ends this instance's processes using
  the drive, as vold does (the instance's MediaProvider kept a scanned image mapped: Skia's file mapping, until a GC), else detaches lazily
  (another tree's instance on the same desktop scanning it); both groups are `alone` (every instance's hub sees every drive).
- Also: Files.readString of /proc/sys/fs/inotify/max_user_watches returned its first byte ("6") on JDK 21: readAllLines.
- Results (Parallels, own tree ~/apklayer-sbx, instances sbx1/sbx2): volumes (21 of 21), volumes-sandbox, files, sysprov, sandbox, hub-services,
  services pass. volumes-sandbox failed once in five runs at "catchup present internal file" (the hub sent the scan; the sandboxed MediaProvider
  did not index the picture within 30 s; it ran right after the unsandboxed volumes group in the same instance), passing when run again.

## 2026-11-09: Bluetooth against real devices (Volla X23 Ubuntu Touch, Volla Quintus Sailfish OS, the x86 PC), fixes
Tested with hello.bluetooth's real-device roles (tests/apps/hello-bluetooth Real.java: `-Dbt.real=scan|pair|server|client|gatt`) and tests/vm/bt-peer.py
(the far side on a plain BlueZ: an auto-yes default agent, an RFCOMM echo channel, an LE GATT peripheral). Fixes went to the phones as a private overlay
(the CLI copied, new bluez.py and host-classes.jar through APKLAYER_ROOT/BUILD/NATIVE, a drop-in for UT's always-on unit), removed afterwards.
- Works now: discovery and LE scan on both phones (each sees the other and the PC); pairing UT -> Quintus by numeric comparison in the hub's dialog (the
  key equal on both sides), UT -> PC answered by the app itself (ACTION_PAIRING_REQUEST, setPin, abortBroadcast: no dialog); RFCOMM by UUID both ways
  between the phones (secure and insecure, two accepts in a row on one server socket), by channel (createRfcommSocket) UT -> PC and Quintus -> PC
  (insecure from an unpaired device: no bond made); GATT from both phones to the PC's peripheral (services, reads, a write, a notification) with the
  real MTU (517) and autoConnect to a device that starts advertising later; the hub's pairing dialog closes when the pairing ends unanswered or the app dies.
- bluez.py: server sockets had no RFCOMM channel in their SDP record (RegisterProfile without Channel: nothing could connect; Channel 0 now); BlueZ's
  handed-over sockets are non-blocking (the relay ended at the first read with no data); one dispatcher per app and service (`serve`) holds the
  registration and queues connections for the accepts (a registration per accept left the gap in which a connection was refused, and BlueZ registers a
  UUID once); BT_SECURITY on channel sockets and RequireAuthentication on profiles for secure/insecure (RequireAuthorization off: no per-service
  question); channel sockets connect through ctypes (Sailfish's Python has no AF_BLUETOOTH addresses); a GATT connection or a bond to a device BlueZ
  has not seen recently discovers it first (BlueZ acts only on known devices; Android's stack pages any address), autoConnect retries when it is seen
  again; the MTU op (the characteristics' MTU); BlueZ's Cancel ends a pairing question; on Sailfish (agents only for its privileged group) Pair goes on
  with the system's agent instead of never starting.
- Host: a session helper outlived its app (the hub's pipe ends: ParcelFileDescriptor.close forgets the descriptor without closing it on the host JVM,
  also PARCELABLE_WRITE_RETURN_VALUE's; closed through a stream now), so each app run left a BlueZ client with its discovery and agent; devices from
  intents (ACTION_FOUND's EXTRA_DEVICE is unparcelled anew) had type 0 and no name (HostBluetooth.KNOWN); requestMtu reports BlueZ's MTU;
  connectGatt passes autoConnect; the pairing question is closed by the asking app's session (bond ended, Cancel) or its death (HubInstallDialog.dismiss).
- Packages: the deb depends on python3-gi, the RPM requires python3-gobject (Sailfish had no PyGObject: Bluetooth, location and settings helpers failed).
- tests/vm/answer-dialog.py reads an always-on hub's dialogs from the journal. Group `bluetooth` (dbusmock) passes on Parallels.

## 2026-11-09: media = device works on the Volla X23 (the device's hardware Codec2)
- "Failed to create video/avc decoder" was no failed creation: the 64x48 fixture is below `c2.mtk.avc.decoder`'s `size min="64x64"` and the device's
  software AVC decoder is not in its codec xml, so MediaCodecList matched nothing. With a 320x240 fixture (`red-h264-320x240.mp4`, hello-native-player
  takes its size from the name) the component was created, then every work failed: the device's logcat (`lxc-attach -n android -- logcat`) showed
  `Codec2-types: Invalid rect info in C2GraphicBlock`. apklayer.prop's pool mask (gralloc BLOB linear blocks) sends the input as a gralloc handle,
  which the Android 12 HAL's CreateLinearBlock does not take (ION/dmabuf only) and reads as a graphic block. Fix: `media-device.prop` (cli/stack.cpp)
  also sets `debug.stagefright.c2-poolmask=327680` (ION | BUFFERQUEUE, Android's default). No library changes: the listener, the output
  IGraphicBufferProducer and bufferpool already work over the host hwbinder context.
- Checked on the phone (instance `c2`, userspace instance binder, `graphics = device`, the installed 0.4.7 runtime; the CLI change applied as an
  instance prop override, the same file loaded last): hello.nativeplayer `PLAYER PASS` through c2.mtk.avc.decoder (SurfaceFlinger screenshot red);
  NewPipe plays Big Buck Bunny 720p through it; `hello.nativecodec -Dhello.device=true` (new: AAC and H.264 round trips with whatever codecs MediaCodec
  picks, 320x240): `c2.mtk.avc.encoder` 12 frames, decoded by c2.mtk.avc.decoder at luma 81; AAC encode/decode through the device's software store.
- CPU, NewPipe 1280x720 (same video, ~25 s window, % of one core, summed over the instance's processes and the device's Codec2 HAL): device 293%
  (HAL 37%, app 116%, SurfaceFlinger 76%) against the GStreamer path 388% (libapklayer_codec2's openh264 122%, app 116%, SurfaceFlinger 75%).
- Still opt-in (the probe stays quality 0, the manager says "available, opt-in"): checked on this one device. Own media path unchanged: codec2,
  native-media, native-player pass on the x86 PC.

- Found by the AVC/audio-silent round: the x86 PC's shared prebuilts (~/apklayer-x86/lead/prebuilts) were behind the x86_64 lock in ten libapklayer_* libraries (audio, camera, cameraserver, codec2, egl, gnss, mediaserver, sensors, servicemanager, surfaceflinger) and Parallels' ~/apklayer-lead and ~/apklayer-st in eight: every test there installed and ran old native code (audio-silent's "failure" on the PC was the pre-fallback audio HAL). All three now match the locks (rsync from the prebuilts branch, allocator included).
- tools/check-prebuilts.sh <dir>: the prebuilts' android-libs against deps/android-tree-files*.lock by the host's architecture; tests/run's build step runs it on $APKLAYER_PREBUILTS and stops with the stale files listed. Checked: passes on the PC and both Parallels sets, lists Parallels' untouched ~/apklayer-build.
- A test tree's prebuilts must be real files (`cp -al`, hard links: no disk), not a `cp -as` symlink farm: meson install copies android-root as cp -a does (the tree's own links stay links), so farm links point outside the guest linker's root (native-bridge: `library "libc++.so" not found` for app_process64).

## 2026-11-09: Sailfish OS, Back and Home: the navigation bar without subsurfaces
- Cause: Lipstick (QtWayland 5.6) offers no wl_subcompositor, so the shell's navigation bar (a subsurface, navbar.c) was never created; apps got a 0 px bar and had no Back or Home.
- Fix: without subsurfaces the bar is a layer of the app's virtual display, as SystemUI's bar window is on a device. The display's owner offers it (`apklayer_display_set_navbar_callback`, shell message 17 NAVBAR_LAYER); the shell then reserves the bar's inset as before and sends the bar it draws (same cairo drawing, ripple included) as a memfd on every change (116 NAVBAR_PIXELS, 0 x 0 when hidden or immersive); libapklayer_vdisplay copies it into a buffer of an opaque layer on top of the app's layer stack at the bottom edge, which SurfaceFlinger composes into each frame. Touches on the window's bottom `nav_h` pixels are the bar's (touch.c). Home lowers the window through Lipstick's qt_surface_extension (`lower`: jolla-home hides an application window to the home screen; protocol XML added), and `raise`/`onscreen_visibility` resume the app. Compositors with subsurfaces keep the subsurface bar unchanged. `APKLAYER_SUBSURFACES=0` simulates a compositor without them (test: `mobile.py --no-subsurfaces` in group mobile).
- Sailfish phone (instance ut, Files, through a second shell of this build on its own socket so the owner's running UiBench kept the session's shell): the shell logs `navigation bar composed by the display`, the app is told a 135 px bar at 450 dpi, SurfaceFlinger's screenshot shows the bar under Files' content; a tap on Back closed Files' open drawer, a tap on Home sent the app to stopped (minimized) and a raise resumed it. Whether Lipstick visibly lowers the window to the home screen needs a human check (the phone is locked; Lipstick's screenshot needs the privileged group).
- Parallels: mobile (both variants: the bar in the virtual display's picture, Back ends UiBench), gpu-window, input pass (input once failed `input test stalled` / IME timing when run beside other groups on a fresh stack, passes alone).
- libapklayer_vdisplay rebuilt (Build log); both locks updated, not yet in the prebuilts.

## 2026-11-09: Sailfish OS, a second app opened only the first (desktop entries)
- Cause: Lipstick launches an entry through libcontentaction as `invoker --type=generic --id=<entry> --single-instance <Exec>`, and invoker's single-instance lock is keyed by the Exec binary (`/run/user/<uid>/single-instance-locks/usr/lib/apklayer/cli/apklayer/instance.lock`): every app is the same CLI, so with one apklayer app open any other one only raised it.
- Fix: app entries carry `X-Nemo-Single-Instance=no` (libcontentaction then leaves out --single-instance); `apklayer list`'s refresh writes an entry without the key again on Sailfish OS (cli/desktop.cpp). Checked on the Sailfish phone: with the key, `lca-tool --triggerdesktop apklayer-com.android.documentsui.desktop` started Files (FilesActivity) beside a running UiBench; without it nothing started.
- Seen there (fixed above, same day): Lipstick offers no wl_subcompositor (globals: wl_compositor 3, wl_shell, android_wlegl, qt_* extensions, alien_manager), so the shell's navigation bar (a subsurface) was never created.

## 2026-11-09: native-media's AVC round trip flake (encoder fed black frames) and audio-silent on the x86 PC
- native-media, about one run in two on Parallels: `AVC round trip luma 47` (and passing runs averaged ~60, not 81). Per picture, a quarter of
  the round trip's pictures were black (Y 0, U 0), and the encoder's packets showed it was fed black input. Cause: MediaCodec's byte-buffer
  encoder input is a gralloc YUV block that CCodec (GraphicBlockBuffer) keeps mapped after queueing it; on GBM drivers without NV12 (virtio-gpu
  on both VMs) the graphics HAL's separate-plane fallback locks through a private copy written back at unlock, so the encoder in the codec2
  service mapped the buffer before the app's unlock and read the zeros from before the app's writes. Fix: YUV buffers only the CPU uses
  (CPU read/write, video encoder: software encoders' input, software decoders' byte-buffer output) are plain shared memory, planes
  contiguous (bridge/hals/graphics `apklayer_gfx_alloc_memory`, packed 3; the allocator picks it, Build log): every lock in every process maps
  the same pages, as a device's gralloc. GPU-used buffers are unchanged. The test now checks every picture's luma, not only the mean (which
  hid four black pictures in twelve); graphics-test checks a second mapping sees writes before the unlock.
- Not the same cause: MediaMetadataRetriever's H.264 frame (PLAN, "C2_CORRUPTED on a single-sample EOS input") still fails; the decoder
  there gets no picture (`avdec_h264: No valid frames decoded before end of stream`), a decode-side issue, left open.
- audio-silent on the x86 PC: the stack aborted (`getOutputSamplingRate` check: the HAL's `Failed to initialize the driver: -112`). Cause: the
  PC's `~/apklayer-x86/lead/prebuilts` is behind the x86_64 lock: its libapklayer_audio.so (5b86b604..., 2026-11-08 09:35) predates the
  silent fallback; nine more libapklayer_* differ too (audio, camera, cameraserver, codec2, egl, gnss, mediaserver, sensors, servicemanager,
  surfaceflinger). No code change: with the locked libraries (from the prebuilts checkout) the group passes. (Parallels' ~/apklayer-st/prebuilts
  is behind the arm64 lock in eight of them as well: camera, cameraserver, codec2, egl, gnss, mediaserver, sensors, servicemanager.)
- Runs (own trees, prebuilts = symlinks to the host's set plus the locked libapklayer_* and the new allocator): before the allocator, the
  per-picture check failed every run on Parallels (also with the locked libraries); after it native-media passed 6 of 6 there (round trip luma
  82 each time) and 5 of 5 on the x86 PC; audio-silent passed 5 of 5 on the PC. codec2, native, native-bridge, native-surface, native-player,
  native-audio and audio-aidl/-track/-system/-silent pass on both, native-bridge-vulkan on the PC; audio-late-install and once native-media
  (3 s of silence at app start) failed only inside a batch on a loaded host (`playing 8 s of PCM took 10 s`) and pass on their own.

## 2026-11-09: `apklayer stop` leaves nothing of the instance running; an app ends with its hub
- Cause (the two follow-ups of "the always-on unit's hub no longer loses to a stray `apklayer hub`"): stop ended the run.* records before the hub, so
  the still-running hub restarted a component host stop had just ended; that launch took the spare (a JVM the holder started, no child of the hub)
  and started a new holder (`app-spare --after N`, detached), which the hub's own shutdown cleanup does not reach and no record listed any more.
  Reproduced on Parallels with the old CLI by the new test: after stop the holders, the hubserver JVM, its component-host launcher and its helpers
  (ip monitor, udevadm monitor) were left.
- Fix (cli/stop.cpp, stopStack moved out of stack.cpp): the hub first (no restarts), then the records, the stack and binderd as before, then, holding
  start.lock (no launch starts a stack meanwhile), every process of the instance until none is left: those whose environment has the instance's
  APKLAYER_INSTANCE and APKLAYER_LOGS (what nativeEnv gives everything apklayer starts; a shell that only exports APKLAYER_INSTANCE is not one), and
  under the sandbox those in the stack's user namespace; never stop itself and its callers, nor the session's shared window shell. Then the
  leftover run.* records go.
- HubClient: a hub that dies takes what the process registered with it (activities, bindings, receivers, alarms, notifications, pending intents), so
  rejoining the next hub would mean replaying all of it. As an Android app process dies with system_server, the app now ends (`the instance hub
  ended: this app process ends with it`, exit 0) instead of running hubless; component hosts already ended from their loop, the hub's own package is
  not affected. Parallels: uibench ended 0.7 s after its hub's SIGTERM.
- Test: tests/vm/stop-race.sh (group hub-services): hello.hubclient bound to its service in a hub-started host, a `component-host` launch started
  right before `apklayer stop`; afterwards no process carries the instance and no run.* record is left (fails with the old CLI, listing the above).
  Parallels (own tree): hub-services, startup, providers, services, activities, broadcasts pass.

## 2026-11-09: storage hot plug and MediaStore scanning (docs/PLAN.md "Open by area", Storage)
- Hot plug, in the hub (HubVolumes): `udisksctl monitor` (through `stdbuf -oL`: into a pipe its output is block-buffered) says when to look again at
  the drives mounted under `/media/<user>`/`/run/media/<user>` (HostVolumes.Removable). Per changed volume, as StorageManagerService.onVolumeStateChangedLocked:
  every running process's StorageManager reads its volumes again and runs its listeners (ENDPOINT_VOLUME, ShadowHostStorageManager.changed:
  StorageEventListener and StorageVolumeCallback with the volume in its new state; MediaProvider updates its volume cache on it), then
  ACTION_VOLUME_STATE_CHANGED (WRITE_MEDIA_STORAGE: ExternalStorageProvider's roots) and the public broadcast with EXTRA_STORAGE_VOLUME:
  ACTION_MEDIA_MOUNTED; on removal ACTION_MEDIA_EJECT, ACTION_MEDIA_UNMOUNTED, and ACTION_MEDIA_REMOVED once `/dev/disk/by-uuid/<uuid>` is gone.
  A gone drive's `/storage/<uuid>` link is removed (also at hub start).
- MediaStore, with MediaProvider's own code: the volume's state goes to its ExternalStorageService (HubStorageService, StorageSessionController's
  call, bound for the one call): a mounted volume is attached and scanned (REASON_MOUNTED), a gone one detached. Files that change on internal
  storage or a mounted drive (apps' and the desktop's writes; on a device FUSE tells MediaProvider) reach its scanner as
  ACTION_MEDIA_SCANNER_SCAN_FILE (MediaReceiver): HubMediaWatch, inotify on every directory but Android/, a path sent once quiet for 1.5 s,
  several in one directory as one scan of it. An always-on instance's boot also announces its mounted volumes (internal storage scanned at boot).
  MediaScannerConnection.scanFile already worked (MediaProvider's scan_file call).
- What it took: `/storage/<uuid>` resolves to the drive's directory in the path layer (paths.c: the link followed there; the scanner's walk from a
  volume root that is a symlink found only the link), and realpath spells a path inside the drive as `/storage/<uuid>/...` (`/mnt/media_rw/...`
  as asked) again (paths_preload.c; MediaProvider canonicalizes and looked for `/media/<user>/...` among its volumes). MediaProvider's database
  backup (leveldb through its FUSE daemon) is off (persist.sys.fuse.backup.* in apklayer.prop): each attach waited 20 s for a daemon. A component
  host that scheduled jobs no longer ends at once when its last service goes: 0.5 s later, and not while a job it planned to run at once has yet to start
  (HostJobs.due: WorkManager schedules a chain's next work as its last job ends, and the host's halt killed it; MediaProvider ran one work item
  per process start). A client that asks for a host while the last one is ending gets a new one (HubDirectory.launch: it waited 20 s, "Cannot start
  com.android.providers.media.module for provider media").
- Test: group `volumes` (tests/vm/volumes.py, hello-files' hotplug scenario): a vfat loop image with DCIM/pre.png mounted at
  `/media/<user>/<label>` while the app runs (root: `sudo -n`, or `sudo -A` with SUDO_ASKPASS), then removed: the broadcast, callback, volume in
  getStorageVolumes and MediaStore, pre.png indexed by the mount scan, a new file on the drive and one on internal storage indexed, the deleted
  one dropped, EJECT/UNMOUNTED/REMOVED in order, the volume and its link gone; 14 of 14 on Parallels in 15 s. Also pass on Parallels (own
  instances): files, view, sysprov, system-apps, services, broadcasts, providers, hub-services. The 0.5 s grace applies only to a process that
  scheduled a job: services' respawn (a host ends after each unbind) needs the immediate end.

## 2026-11-09: Bluetooth channel sockets, server sockets, pairing requests (docs/PLAN.md "Bluetooth and USB")
- `BluetoothDevice.createRfcommSocket(int)`/`createInsecureRfcommSocket(int)` (the hidden calls serial apps reach by reflection): `bluez.py rfcomm
  <address> <channel>` connects an AF_BLUETOOTH RFCOMM socket itself and relays it as the service sockets do (the hub accepts a channel 1-30 in place of
  the UUID). When the app closes a relayed socket the helper now shuts the connection down (before, it lasted until the device closed it).
- Server sockets: `listenUsing[Insecure]RfcommWithServiceRecord` (ShadowHostBluetoothServerSocket, new): the hub starts `bluez.py listen <uuid> <name>`,
  which registers the service with BlueZ's Profile API in the server role, prints `LISTEN`, takes one NewConnection, unregisters and relays it;
  accept() hands it over as a connected socket and starts the next listener at once, accept(timeout) throws "Try again", close() ends the waiting
  listener and wakes a blocked accept. One helper per connection because the app's pipes cannot carry a new descriptor. BluetoothSocket.getRemoteDevice
  is the shadow's (Android 16 keeps it in an Optional the constructor fills: it threw for our sockets). Robolectric's newInstance names a server socket
  constructor this android-all lacks: plain newInstanceOf.
- Pairing: ACTION_PAIRING_REQUEST goes to the app first as an ordered broadcast (variant, key); an app that calls setPin (and aborts) answers BlueZ
  itself, else the receiver after the app's (the system's dialog on Android) asks the hub. The agent registers as DisplayYesNo (Android's capability,
  was NoInputNoOutput): RequestConfirmation asks the person to compare the passkey (HubInstallDialog.ask), DisplayPasskey/DisplayPinCode show the key
  to type on the device. BLUETOOTH_PIN carries the pairing variant and key.
- Tests: the bluetooth group's mock bridges ConnectProfile to an app's server profile (the app's server socket talks to its own client socket), and two
  more devices pair with setPin 0000 (no dialog) and by passkey comparison (answered yes); the channel socket must fail with the kernel's reason
  (Parallels: No route to host; the x86 PC's real adapter: Host is down after paging). Parallels and the x86 PC: bluetooth passes. On the x86 PC's
  real BlueZ `bluez.py listen` registered the server profile (LISTEN), nothing paired.

## 2026-11-09: camera rotate-and-crop follows a window turned while the camera is open; checked on a real webcam (docs/PLAN.md "Camera")
- The mode was fixed at open. AOSP's live path: on a device WindowManager tells system_server's CameraServiceProxy, which calls cameraserver's
  notifyDisplayConfigurationChange; CameraService then asks the proxy's getRotateAndCropOverride again for each open client and
  setRotateAndCropOverride changes the open camera's requests (Camera3Device's AUTO override), no reopen. apklayer's proxy stand-in
  (aosp/hals/camera/services/Service.cpp, media.camera.proxy) now does that part: an app process tells it its window shape (one apklayer
  transaction, oneway: package, 0 or 90 degrees, a process token whose death drops the entry) from HostCameraShadows.windowShape when the
  shape flips, and only to a running cameraserver (media.camera.proxy is not a name the stack starts it for); the proxy answers 90 for a
  back camera and 270 for a front one, as CameraProviderManager's portrait rotation, NONE for a landscape window, then calls
  notifyDisplayConfigurationChange itself. Opens keep reading the camera compat info.
- Tests: camera-portrait.py: with the back camera open hello.camera's Portrait turns its window to landscape (setRequestedOrientation; the
  preview turned back by the display rotation as Camera2Basic does) and back: results 1 -> 0 -> 1, YUV frames upright (all bars) then turned
  again, SurfaceFlinger screenshots with all eight bars across in landscape and upright cropped bars after the return. With the old
  cameraserver it fails ("a landscape window kept rotate-and-crop", the result stayed 1). New optional group camera-real (CAMERA_REAL, default
  "MacBook Pro Camera"): the same app on a real PipeWire camera (Parallels passes the Mac's FaceTime camera through; the provider reports it
  as a back camera, orientation 90): the frames' size and brightness, the result modes, and the portrait screenshot after the return must
  match the landscape screenshot's centre slice as it is (correlation of 18x32 grey grids: upright 0.75; turned 90/180/270 0.02/-0.10/0.05,
  mirrored 0.47). Parallels (own tree): camera, camera-pipewire, camera-real pass; x86 PC (own tree, x86_64 library): camera passes.
- Not changed: a portrait desktop monitor gives a portrait window, so it is treated as a turned phone (rotate-and-crop on); a webcam on a
  turned monitor may or may not turn with it, which apklayer cannot know (docs/PLAN.md).

## 2026-11-09: camera rotate-and-crop for apps in a portrait window (docs/PLAN.md item 6, "Camera")
- A portrait window reports rotation 0 on the instance's portrait-natural display, so an app turns the preview by the sensor orientation (90)
  although the backend's cameras stay upright on the landscape desktop: sideways previews in every portrait window (the default window shape on
  the VMs, 325x579). AOSP's own mechanism, end to end: the app process sets Android 16's CameraCompatibilityInfo (rotateAndCropRotation 90, what
  WindowManager hands a fixed-orientation activity on a rotated display) for any portrait window (HostCameraShadows.windowShape, called from
  AppHost.hostResize; not for a Halium device camera); CameraManager and Camera1 pass it at each open; cameraserver unchanged turns it into
  ROTATE_AND_CROP_90 for a back camera, 270 for a front one. The provider (libapklayer_camera) lists availableRotateAndCropModes NONE/90/180/270/AUTO
  and AUTO in its default requests (two lines in the vendored Goldfish CameraDevice.cpp, docs/PATCHES.md) and BackendCamera turns and crops each
  processed output: the centre slice of the frame at its aspect turned, each output's crop from it, 90 counterclockwise as the reference HAL
  (hardware/google/camera EmulatedFrameSource). Not used: the `media.camera.proxy` stand-in (it would need the activity's orientation from the hub).
- Tests: group camera gains camera-portrait.py: hello.camera's Portrait activity (screenOrientation portrait) checks the capture result's mode (1 back,
  3 front) and the turned YUV frame, and SurfaceFlinger screenshots of its TextureView preview show the bars upright and cropped to the centre 3:4
  slice (yellow-cut, cyan, green, magenta, red, blue-cut; mirrored for the front camera); without the change it fails (bars across). hello.camera's
  Main is landscape-locked (its checks are for the cameras' own orientation). Parallels: camera and camera-pipewire pass (with the lazy
  cameraserver: started on use, exited after the app); the x86 PC (x86_64 libraries): camera passes.

## 2026-11-09: the always-on unit's hub no longer loses to a stray `apklayer hub` (the restart that left instance ut dead)
- Cause: `apklayer hub` that finds hub.lock held waits for that hub and returns 0 (cli/run.cpp runHub). In apklayer-instance@<id>.service that return ends the unit's main process with success, and its ExecStopPost `apklayer stop` then ends the instance, the other hub included. The other launcher comes from the old stack: processes started while the stop runs survive it (on Parallels the hub restarted a component host `stop` had just killed; the host and its spare holder outlived the sweep, the host's HubStartup ran `setsid apklayer hub`, a `wait hub` started a stack). Over a restart that launcher takes the free lock before the unit's new hub.
- Fix: in its own always-on unit the hub takes over. It ends the launcher that holds the lock (the pids in hub.pid), then starts its own hub. A launcher now writes its pid to hub.pid as soon as it holds the lock, so a launcher still starting its stack can be ended too; a launcher outside the unit keeps the old behaviour (it waits, then returns). ExecStopPost and the lock are unchanged.
- Test: tests/vm/hub-unit.sh (group hub-services) writes a unit for the test instance with the test's environment. It starts a stray hub, then the unit: the unit must stay active, its MainPID must be hub.pid's launcher, with one hub JVM; then the same checks after `systemctl --user restart`. Before the fix it fails with "the unit is deactivating once the stray hub ended". Parallels (own tree): hub-services, startup, push pass.
- Volla X23 (instance ut): with deb lazy4 the same stray sequence left the unit inactive with no hub (reproduced). With deb 0.4.7~git20261109.hubunit (this commit, built on Parallels with tools/release/build-deb.sh, ~/speed78/apklayer-hubunit.deb) the unit's hub ended the stray hub (exit 143) and stayed the only one; restart, and dpkg -i followed by restart, gave one hub owned by the unit. The phone is back on lazy4 (runtime 0.3.4), active with one hub.

## 2026-11-09: GLES windows without the per-frame copy (docs/PLAN.md "Graphics performance" (c))
- libapklayer_egl (new EglAttach.cpp): eglSwapBuffers hands the gbm_surface's finished buffer itself to the Android window. The bo is wrapped as one of our gralloc buffers (one GraphicBuffer per bo, kept, so SurfaceFlinger imports each once) and attached to the window's BufferQueue (Surface::attachBuffer through dlsym; the Surface found by the ANativeObjectBase layout, checked against libgui's vtable); buffers SurfaceFlinger released come back through detachNextBuffer (release fence waited) before Mesa needs one, and when all four gbm buffers are out the swap waits for a release in dequeueBuffer. The copy stays for what this cannot take: a window that is no libgui Surface, a 10-bit, half-float or 565 config, a format other than RGBA/RGBX asked of the window, a queue size other than the surface's (the frame of a resize), a failed attach. MSAA configs present their resolved buffer. Linked libui and libutils (libnativewindow loads them anyway). glbench's `window-msaa` mode (4x samples) joined window-formats.
- x86 PC (N100 UHD, x86_64 egl b9889035 against 88ac65ee), glbench 1600x1200, the app process's GPU time from DRM fdinfo (drm-engine-render): vsync window clear 2.07 -> 0.89 ms GPU per frame (busy 12.4 -> 5.3%), draws 3.67 -> 2.44 ms (22.0 -> 14.6%), 60 fps either way; swap interval 0: clear 1.25 -> 0.73 ms (GPU 78 -> 39% busy), draws 1.81 -> 1.22 ms with 422 -> 495-501 fps; clear's frame rate fell 623 -> 529-534 fps (p50 1.31 -> 0.71 ms but p95 3.2 -> 5.7 ms: at 600 fps all four buffers are out and the swap waits for SurfaceFlinger's release), the fill scene is GPU-bound and within noise (8.5 -> 8.0 ms).
- Tests: x86 PC gpu, gpu-window (resize), texture-view, window-formats (msaa and the deep configs), fences, otr2 pass (otr2 once failed beside other groups with "no screenshot", then passed three times alone). Parallels (arm64 egl 7b159ae1, virgl): gpu, window-formats, fences pass; gpu-window and texture-view pass at -j 1 and fail at -j 2 with the old library too (leftover processes, TextureView's finish line).

## 2026-11-09: the "~1 s slower Calculator" was the start trace's end point, not a regression (docs/PLAN.md "app start speed on phones" 7)
- Volla X23, instance ut always-on, the same CDS source (archive written by Calculator, instance restarted) for each deb, `apklayer run` in the unit's environment, 3 runs: 0.3.3 speed1 (runtime 0.3.1) `activity resumed` warm 8.3-8.7 s, cold 16.3-16.5 s; 0.4.7 lazy4 (runtime 0.3.4) 9.6-9.8 / 17.3-17.7 s. But 0.3.3 drew the first frame after that mark (HWUI's first-frame `Davey` FrameCompleted: warm 13.1-13.4 s, cold 20.9-21.0 s), 0.4.7 before it (9.6-10.0 / 17.3-17.7 s): to the first frame the new deb is ~3.6 s faster in both modes.
- How: the first frame (window surface, Mali EGL, mapper, first draw: 4.7 s in 0.3.3's run loop) now runs in ActivityController.visible()'s main-looper idle (a JFR ClassLoad stack: ShadowChoreographer.doFrame under ActivityTask.launchNow -> visible -> idleIfPaused), because a vsync was due already, so it counts before `activity resumed`; in 0.3.3 it was scheduled 92 ms after that mark. LambdaForm classes in that phase 919 -> 2048 (the drawing natives' call sites, SurfaceControl, Canvas, Paint, text) are that frame's, not a binding regression. Ruled out on the way: the heap flags (-XX:InitialHeapSize=32m, TrimNativeHeapInterval: 9.6 s warm with the JDK's defaults too), soft-reference clearing of LambdaForm caches (same count with SoftRefLRUPolicyMSPerMB=1000000), robolectric 0024 (0.4.7 with 0.3.3's ShadowDisplayEventReceiver: 9.6-10.0 s).
- Fix: StartTrace marks `first frame <activity>` (ViewTreeObserver.registerFrameCommitCallback on the task's first activity, -Dapklayer.perf only), the start's end that does not move with where the looper runs the frame; on the phone it lands 25 ms after Davey's FrameCompleted, on Parallels (UiBench) ~100 ms after `activity resumed`. Tests (Parallels, tree apklayer-st): startup, hub-services, activities pass (the latter two failed once on a first run: a shell left from an earlier test, no shell.log yet; passed on the rerun).
- Found on the way (fixed in the entry "the always-on unit's hub no longer loses to a stray `apklayer hub`"): `systemctl --user restart apklayer-instance@ut` while a spare of the old stack waited for the hub (`apklayer wait hub` from its scope) started a second `apklayer hub` beside the unit's; the unit's hub then exited 0 and its ExecStopPost stop ended the instance (seen twice, after a deb change).

## 2026-11-09: graphics round (Vulkan presentation by default, Godot 4; x86 PC)
- Vulkan presentation is on by default where the host can do it: cli/env.cpp asks `vulkaninfo --summary` once per session and, for a GPU device that is not Venus (the VMs: it aborts on imported dma-bufs) or a CPU device, sets APKLAYER_VULKAN_PRESENT (the driver's existing switch) and APKLAYER_VULKAN_VERSION; APKLAYER_VULKAN_PRESENT=0 turns it off. HostSystemConfig then declares android.hardware.vulkan.version (the host's API up to 1.3), .level 0 and .compute 0 with their versions, and ShadowHostPackageManager answers hasSystemFeature(name, version) as PackageManagerService (Robolectric answered false for every version). Godot checks them before it creates its Vulkan view ("this device does not meet the requirements for Vulkan support" before).
- Godot's Vulkan context then failed with "Required extension VK_KHR_android_surface not found": volk opens libvulkan.so.1 before libvulkan.so, and bionic_translation found the host's glibc loader under that name. bionic_translation 0048: a cfg.d line `app:<name> <target>` maps only lookups from the app namespace; bt-cfg.py maps app:libvulkan.so.1 to Android's loader, while our driver (a system library) keeps opening the host loader by that name.
- Intent files take String[] extras (`extra[].<name>=a|b`), for Godot's command_line_params. Test group `godot` (tests/vm/godot-vulkan.py; the Godot 4.3 android_editor.apk from godot-builds installed by hand): a one-scene Mobile-renderer project runs, Godot reports "Vulkan 1.4.318 - Forward Mobile - Using Device #0: Intel - Intel(R) Graphics (ADL-N)" and the screenshot is 100% the scene's magenta.
- x86 PC (own tree, prebuilts 234b56ec): godot, vulkan, vulkan-present, native-bridge-vulkan, gpu (hwbitmap included: fixed by 2026-11-08's pthread_gettid_np) and otr2 pass; vulkan with APKLAYER_VULKAN_PRESENT=0 reports no swapchain and passes. Not run: Parallels/UTM (Venus or llvmpipe only: the probe declares nothing there).
- Measured, not changed: the GL swap's blit costs 0.62-1.31 ms GPU per frame on the N100 (834x1484 to 1080x2400); OTR2 at 2448x1374 runs ~30 fps with and without Swappy. The host-ES1 path cannot run on the PC or Parallels (no host ES 1 context). Present fences at the real present still need a kernel fence the composer can signal. Details: docs/PLAN.md "Graphics performance", "Graphics".
## 2026-11-09: storage an ordinary app sees, as on Android 16 (PLAN next work 7)
- Measured first (hello-files' new `VOLUMES=1` scenario, group files): getExternalFilesDir was `Android/data/<pkg>` itself and getExternalCacheDir
  `<sdcard>/<pkg>-cache` (Robolectric's, a folder per app in the sdcard's root), Environment.isExternalStorageManager() was true for every app (Robolectric
  allows every app op), getStorageVolumes listed only a hand-built primary volume, and the desktop's removable drives were nowhere.
- Now, as Android 16 (StorageManagerService.getVolumeList, VolumeInfo.buildStorageVolume, ContextImpl): external files `Android/data/<pkg>/files`, cache
  `Android/data/<pkg>/cache` (ShadowHostContextImpl); what an app kept in `Android/data/<pkg>` moves into `files/` once and the old `<pkg>-cache` goes
  (HostStorage); "all files access" only for apps that declare MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE (the op's default mode defers to the permission, which
  app-op permissions get at install); getStorageVolumes builds the emulated and public volumes with Android's own code from the storage service's list
  (HostVolumes gained the private internal volume, so the primary is "Internal shared storage" with UUID_DEFAULT).
- Removable drives: a drive the desktop mounts for the user (`/media/<user>/<label>`, `/run/media/<user>/<label>`) is a public volume
  `/storage/<fs uuid>` (a link in the instance's storage dir; `/mnt/media_rw/<uuid>`, ExternalStorageProvider's path, maps there in paths.c): apps see it
  in getStorageVolumes (removable, its label), MediaStore lists its volume (`4c3a-9050`), DocumentsUI gets its root. Checked with a vfat loop image
  mounted at /media/parallels/APKTEST on Parallels. Read once per process (no hot plug yet); a sandboxed app does not see it (its uid cannot read /media/<user>).
- Home and Linux root stay DocumentsUI's roots only (not in apps' getStorageVolumes): the desktop's files reach an app through the picker, or the sandbox's home access.
- SELinux labels: not implemented. No app needs them: of the ~40 local APKs (corpus and ~/Downloads) only Termux names getFileContext (its file-info diagnostics) and OsmAnd's
  "security.selinux" is libarchive's xattr name; no system app (MediaProvider, DownloadProvider, DocumentsUI, ExternalStorageProvider) asks.
- Amaze's "8 folders for 4" (here 26 for 13) is Amaze's own race, not storage: jdb on the app shows loadlist run from onViewCreated and again from a
  posted initViews; both LoadFilesListTasks reset and add to the same view model's counters (the first is cancelled, its loop keeps counting). The listing is right.
- Tests (Parallels, own instances): files (with VOLUMES=1), view, sysprov, providers, sandbox pass; Amaze, Fossify Gallery, F-Droid and NewPipe draw.
  NewPipe's download folders are `file:/storage/emulated/0/{Movies,Music}/NewPipe` (no SAF), served by the path layer; a download itself was not driven.

  sudo asks a password); usb and usb-serial run alone (one usbip-vudc). Parallels: usb-serial and usb pass.

  example joins its thread in both). geoclue1 on the Sailfish phone (192.168.41.153, ctypes run of the installed backend): the session reaches

## 2026-11-09: the always-on phone instance with less memory, third round: the native services (docs/PLAN.md "app start speed on phones" 8)
- Measured (Volla X23, smaps per service): nearly all of a native service's PSS is anonymous memory, and it follows the number of
  libraries it loads (~0.25 MB each: the libraries' static allocations): gnss 27 libraries 9 MB, mediaserver ~200 libraries 55 MB.
  The sensors HAL loaded 258 (libandroid_runtime, hwui, pdfium, stagefright...) for 101 MB: it linked libandroid for its `--probe` only.
  The probe opens libandroid itself now: the HAL is 6 MB (sensorfw backend; the device's sensors HAL is not used).
- cameraserver, the media server, the extractor and codec2 start on use and end once no client holds them, as AOSP's lazy services:
  servicemanager's access policy (aosp/hals/servicemanager/Access.cpp) writes each lookup of their names to a FIFO the stack daemon
  polls (cli/stack.cpp); the services register through LazyServiceRegistrar (NDK: AServiceManager_registerLazyService) and end with
  _exit from the active-services callback (the registrar's exit() aborted codec2 under btload). Clients wait as on a device
  (waitForService and getService's polling ask again; Java's getService("media.camera") waits for cameraserver in 1 s steps,
  ShadowHostServiceManager). The stack no longer waits for media.camera before it is ready. `APKLAYER_LAZY_SERVICES=0` (in ~/.config/apklayer/env: the stack is often started by apklayerd or the hub) starts them
  with the stack and keeps them; not under the sandbox.
- Who starts them on the phone: the media provider's boot scan (media.player, then codec2 through the media server), Open Camera,
  NewPipe's WebView (Chromium asks CameraManager: cameraserver; MediaCodecList: the media server and codec2); each ends 5-10 s after its
  last client.
- SurfaceFlinger's 110-120 MB: ~37 MB is the Mali driver's per-context blob (the composer has the same block once it drew), ~10 MB
  Skia's SkSL; dma-bufs and gralloc buffers are not in its PSS.
- A service registered twice (instantiate(), then the registrar) lost servicemanager's client guarantee and, with a client that got it
  through a registration callback only, never heard of clients and stayed: a lookup right after registering restores it.
- Phone (deb 0.4.7~git20261109.lazy4 built on Parallels, the five libraries copied into runtime 0.3.4; rollback: ~/speed78/apklayer-mem2.deb
  and ~/speed78/orig-libs): settled, no app yet, 1293 MB (native 642) -> **974 MB** (hub 346, spare 305, native 306); lazy off in the same
  deb 1204. After NewPipe, Calculator, Open Camera and a NewPipe video, everything ended: 1374 -> 1032 MB. Launches unchanged (PLAN 8's
  table: NewPipe cold 21.0-21.4 / warm 12.2-12.5 s, Calculator 17.5-18.3 / 10.0-10.5 s, lazy off 21.3-21.5 / 12.4-13.0 / 17.8-18.2 /
  10.1-10.8 s). Open Camera: the camera connects 0.1 s after the activity resumes, preview 2.1 s later, as before; NewPipe plays video.
- Tests: Parallels (~/agent-lazy, private library directory) gpu, startup, hub-services, camera, codec2, native-surface, native-media,
  native-player, native-audio, audio-aidl, audio-track, audio-system, audio-silent pass (native-media's AVC round trip fails about one run
  in two with lazy services on or off); x86 PC (~/apklayer-x86/lazy, xk1/xk2) gpu, startup, hub-services, camera, codec2, native-surface,
  native-media, native-player, native-audio, audio-aidl, audio-system, audio-track pass; audio-silent fails there with lazy services on or off.

- Measured where a host JVM's ~320 MB goes on the Volla X23 (NMT summary, smaps): heap 90 MB committed for 30-43 MB live, the CDS archive's
  read-write half ~33 MB private per JVM, ~110 MB of Android native allocations NMT does not see, metaspace/code/symbols ~40 MB.
- SettingsProvider runs in the hub's JVM, as in system_server (HubHost, cli runHub; not under the sandbox, or with APKLAYER_SETTINGS_IN_HUB=0).
  Local hub calls clear the calling identity; the hub's own pid holds every permission; a peer is known by its app uid too; settings changes from

## 2026-11-08: tester reports (0.4.7 deb, NVIDIA x86_64): MojoLauncher's StackOverflowError, FCL's androidnsbypass
- MojoLauncher (git.artdeell.mjlaunch): its Application wraps its base context (a ContextWrapper of the same ContextImpl), so ShadowHostContextImpl's
  redirect of the state services (location, battery, ...) to the application's base came back to itself forever. The base is unwrapped to its ContextImpl
  first. Test: hello-system's `wrappedbase` scenario (its own launch of tests/vm/system-apps.sh; the Application wraps its base only then, since
  ActivityThread casts the base to ContextImpl for a manifest receiver, as on Android).
- FCL (com.tungsten.fcl) / Pojav: androidnsbypass resolves the linker's private API from libdl.so and ld-android.so; nothing answered, it fell back to a
  memory scan for linker64 and called null. Now `bionic___loader_dlopen/_android_dlopen_ext/_dlclose/_dlsym` and the namespace calls
  (`__loader_android_create_namespace` returns a dummy namespace, link calls succeed) in bridge/native/android_platform.c, found through bionic_dlsym's
  global `bionic_<name>` lookup; `ld-android.so` and `libdl_android.so` map to the shim's libdl (bridge/native/bionic_translation.cfg);
  `android_update_LD_LIBRARY_PATH` (FCL's setLdLibraryPath through libdl.so, a null call before) adds existing directories to the app namespace
  (bridge/native/native_loader.c; the shim linker's 8-directory limit stays).

  "Obtaining private API dlFuncs using libdl.so" and "linker namespace funcs from ld-android.so" (before: the same failure as the tester's log). FCL itself:
  agreement, storage alert, runtime install (JRE 8/17/21/25, LWJGL; JRE 8's pack200 files fail: FCL runs libunpack200.so as a program, exit 127) by scripted
  taps; a game launch (it needs a Minecraft version downloaded) was not driven. system-apps: wrappedbase passes in both launches; media fails there
  (MediaProvider does not start in that tree's setup, unrelated).
- Java on a deb: `APKLAYER_LOGCAT=1 apklayer run` takes the same JVM (checked: <native>/jdk/bin/java on the PC). The runtime ships no JDK of its own; the
  deb's JVM is the system's (or the CDS image jdk-image.sh builds from it when the JDK has jmods), so Ubuntu's 21.0.12 in the tester's crash report is
  expected. The spare message only means that launch started its own JVM (the spare was made without the logcat property).

  the runtime that a deb keeps inside it (source trees keep it outside, so the VM groups never saw it). Now bound after. Sandboxed apps' own
  `android.util.Log` lines were lost (their app log is the user's 0600 file in a read-only state dir); host shader caches (Mesa's, NVIDIA's) go
  to the app's own `cache/host`.
- apklayerd's file broker (cli/broker.cpp, bridge/native/broker.c): what the app's uid is refused (EACCES) but the user may do, apklayerd does as
  the user and hands over the fd: GPU nodes (`/dev/dri/*`, `/dev/nvidia*`, `/dev/kfd`, `/dev/dxg`; each logged in apklayerd.log) for any
  sandboxed app, its own app log, and for apps with home access open/opendir/stat/fopen/mkdir/unlink/rmdir/rename/access in the home (never
  apklayer's own trees there, never through a link as the last name). Relative paths of the *at forms are made absolute from the fd first.
  Rootless (the idmapped mounts it replaces need root); also gives DocumentsUI's Home root the files closed to "other".

  vendor directories, each manifest's `library_path`): driver-agnostic, for NVIDIA's files in a home too.

  refused the namespace's uid 1000 when the user's uid is another.

- Checks (Parallels, arm64): with `/dev/dri/renderD128` closed to app uids (0660 + the seat's ACL, as Debian's NVIDIA nodes) group gpu passes
  sandboxed through the broker. A deb built from the branch (0.4.7+sbx3), installed, `apklayer setup --bundle` in a fresh data home: UiBench
  and F-Droid run sandboxed (own uids, drawn, F-Droid updating its repositories); the owner's 0.4.7~git20261108 deb was put back after. Groups
  activities services broadcasts providers pendingintents notifications binder files keystore gpu gpu-window webview push sandbox permissions:
  all pass unsandboxed and sandboxed (webview failed once sandboxed beside another group, cookie null; passed on 3 reruns). Not run: the x86 PC
  (AppArmor restricts user namespaces there and no profile exists: the owner runs `sudo sh tools/host-setup/sandbox.sh
  ~/apklayer-x86/<dir>/repo/b/cli/apklayer` once), an NVIDIA host (tester offline: docs/INSTALL.md Troubleshooting lists what to check).

## 2026-11-04: parallel rounds (instances, windows, manager, media, installer, identity, corpus with current apps)
- 2026-11-08: `apklayer remove-all [--yes]` (cli/purge.cpp): everything apklayer keeps for the current user — every instance stopped and its always-on unit off, folders the sandbox's app uids own taken back, then the data, state, cache and config trees, the apps' desktop entries, the Apklayer submenu files and the user units; without --yes it lists them. docs/INSTALL.md "Uninstall": that, then `sudo apt purge apklayer apklayer-dev`.
- 2026-11-08: release 0.4.7 (runtime 0.3.4, prebuilts bc10db71): Unity games (il2cpp found through the app's class loader, a debuggerd-like default fault handler instead of an endless crash loop, SIGPIPE blocked in app JVMs, SensorService without sensors, a vsync per DisplayEventReceiver, real memory info, app logs capped at 256 MB): Project Offroad reaches its menu, PGR loads; arm64-only apps on x86_64 deb installs (the Android root in the kernel's spelling); Play's device filter sees microphone, wifi, camera and location (Aurora downloads PGR); the userspace binder (opt-in, APKLAYER_BINDER=userspace); x86_64 hwbitmap and keystore fixes; mobile full-screen host dialogs with touch; silent audio without a sound server.
- 2026-11-08: Unity games ([PROJECT : OFFROAD], Unity 2022.3.62 il2cpp; PGR, 2022.3.52). Offroad crashed on Parallels with a null call (pc 0) from libunity and then printed "CRASH: No handler for signal 11" 1.2 million times (a 536 MB app log). Causes and fixes, each generic: (1) Unity finds libil2cpp by calling `ClassLoader.findLibrary("il2cpp")` through JNI on the app's class loader, past the converter; AppClassLoader returned null, so Unity showed "Failed to load Il2CPP." and went on to call the il2cpp_register_log_callback pointer its failed loader left null (PGR's "Failed to load Il2CPP." is the same): AppClassLoader.findLibrary answers as a PathClassLoader. (2) Unity's crash handler chains to the previous SIGSEGV handler, which was SIG_DFL (libjsig's empty chain slot), and returned into the fault: bionic_translation 0046 reports a debuggerd-like default handler there (note, SIG_DFL, re-raise), test tests/native/fault-chain.c. (3) A write to a closed pipe on the main thread (AppSpare's started byte) raised SIGPIPE, which HotSpot chains to the app's handler: app JVMs start with SIGPIPE blocked, as ART's threads. (4) libsensor (NDK ASensorManager) waited 300 s for "sensorservice" on each call when no sensors provider is set: SensorService always runs (no HAL, no sensors). (5) Unity's render loop waits for its UnityChoreographer looper's frames, and Robolectric's single next-vsync time went to the main thread's receiver only: robolectric 0024 gives each receiver its own due vsync. (6) ActivityManager.getMemoryInfo reads /proc/meminfo (PGR warned the RAM was under 2 GB). The launch's tee stops writing app-<pkg>.log at 256 MB with a note. Offroad now shows its garage menu.
- 2026-11-08: arm64-only apps on x86_64 deb installs (owner: APKPure on the x86 PC, `linker: CANNOT LINK EXECUTABLE "/system/bin/arm64/app_process64": library "libc++.so" not found`). Not an exec: app_process64 is the guest main executable Berberis loads for the guest linker in the app process itself (guest_loader kAppProcessRelativePath). The guest linker picks its ld.config.arm64.txt section by the realpath of that program (read from /proc/self/fd, mapped back to Android spelling by apklayer_android_spelling). Under the deb the Android root is reached through ~/.local/share/apklayer/runtime/current -> <version>: the kernel spells the resolved path, which matched neither root, so the program stayed a host path, matched no `dir.system` section (realpath("/system") is the instance root and was mapped back) and the linker fell back to its default /system/lib64 (the x86_64 side). Every arm64-only app failed on a deb install; a source tree (no symlink) passed. Fix: paths.c resolves the Android root with realpath at load. Test: native-bridge reruns hello-arm64 with APKLAYER_NATIVE through a symlink (failed with the old paths.c with the owner's exact message, passes now). x86 PC (own tree ~/apklayer-x86/exec64): native-bridge, native, activities pass; reproduced with the owner's deb (0.4.2, runtime 0.3.2) in a scratch instance (since removed). APKPure itself not run: not installed on the PC and no local APK (apkpure.com is behind a bot check). No ~/los24 build.
- 2026-11-08: Aurora "App not supported" (owner: Punishing: Gray Raven, com.kurogame.gplay.punishing.grayraven.en) was apklayer's feature list. Aurora's native profile sends PackageManager's system features at checkin, and ours had 10 (touchscreen, faketouch, screen, audio.output, a few software ones). Play applies the features an app's permissions imply (CAMERA, RECORD_AUDIO, CHANGE_WIFI_STATE: camera, microphone, wifi, required unless the manifest says otherwise): its details answered versionCode 0 and the purchase DeliveryResponse status 2. Found with a host-JVM probe over Aurora's own gplayapi classes (anonymous token, our properties): Aurora's Pixel 9a profile passes, ours with only the Features line swapped passes, a ddmin over the Pixel's extra features leaves exactly microphone, camera and wifi; SDK 37, screen 325x579/160 dpi, GL ES 3.2, the extensions, ABIs and the empty shared library list do not matter for it. Fix: apklayer-features.xml declares android.hardware.microphone (every audio backend captures) and android.hardware.wifi (WifiManager over the host network, as Android-x86 on Ethernet); HostSystemConfig adds camera and camera.any when the stack runs cameraserver over a camera HAL (HostCameraShadows.declared) and location, location.gps, location.network when it runs the GNSS HAL, as it does Bluetooth for an adapter. Parallels, own XDG dirs and instance with the deb's host-classes.jar plus the new class: Aurora's fresh anonymous login carries the new features, the details show v4.9.1 (1792678636), the purchase passes, Aurora downloads base, config splits and the 1.2 GB asset pack, the hub's install question is answered and PGR installs (splits AssetPack, config.arm64_v8a, config.en, config.mdpi). PGR starts (Kuro SDK splash, libunity loads) and then shows Unity's "Failed to load Il2CPP." (PLAN). Existing Aurora logins keep the device profile they checked in with: log out and in again (Anonymous) after the update.

On by default in 0.4.5 it broke real installs: a clean deb install could not start any app (the runtime folder was unpacked 0700 by mkdtemp: sandboxed app uids could not read the preload or the host classes; now 0755, and setup-runtime.sh repairs existing installs at the package update), and an NVIDIA tester's apps fell back to llvmpipe (likely NVIDIA's /dev/nvidia* nodes missing from the app view; open, PLAN).

- 2026-11-08: x86 PC suite fixes. `apklayer instance autostart` writes the unit where the user's systemd manager reads it (the manager's own XDG_CONFIG_HOME, from `systemctl --user show-environment`, else ~/.config) and runs systemctl with that value: a shell's private XDG_CONFIG_HOME hid the unit from systemd; push's autostart phase checks that path and removes what it wrote. Bundles (.xapk/.apkm/.apks) keep one ABI split, the first of the machine's ABIs that has one, as PackageManager picks one primary ABI (an x86_64 host with the arm64 bridge kept both). The shell ignores the desktop's activation for a window whose focus test input drives (another window on the shared desktop deactivated input-inject's window mid long press, and a window's focus loss removes the long-press callback, as on Android). The bottom-sheet test converts the app's pixels to the window's logical pixels and reads the handle where the sheet is (200% on the PC); the settings test expects the title bar's light redraw only on a dark desktop. On the PC (-j 2, own data home with the arm64 system apps, microG 0.3.17, the x86_64 WebView and UiBench installed) pass: activities services broadcasts providers pendingintents notifications binder files hardware gnss permissions push push-microg devices settings portal webview (alone) gpu-window texture-view window-formats native-audio codec2 sysprov installer dex2jvm sharedlib accounts input startup.
