- 2026-11-08: silent audio without a sound server (owner's decision). When no PipeWire or PulseAudio answers, the audio HAL's output and input streams (aosp/hals/audio/Stream.cpp) run on AOSP's own DriverStubImpl, the emulator's stub (linked already through libaudioserviceexampleimpl): output is discarded at real time, recording delivers zeros at real time (the stub fills noise), and the HAL logs once per direction `[audio] no sound server answers (<what the backend needs>: <error>): playback is silent, at real time`. Apps see a working output: OpenTTD's AudioTrack no longer fails with -19, and after a focus loss its game thread stops instead of drawing with destroyed textures. The choice is made when AudioFlinger opens its output (at the stack's start): a server started later is used from the next stack start. Groups `audio-silent` (the original AudioTrack with PIPEWIRE_REMOTE/PULSE_SERVER pointing nowhere: 48000 frames presented over >= 0.95 s, the log line) and `openttd-silent` (optional, OPENTTD_APK: no `Error -19 initializing AudioTrack`, no `Invalid texture` after `blur`; with main's HAL 196 of them). Parallels (arm64, its own tree and prebuilts copy): audio-silent, audio-aidl, audio-track, audio-system, native-audio, audio-late-install pass. x86 PC: the same pass, and openttd-silent. audio-late-install failed once on each host on a cold instance (`RATE wall=9.6 s for 8 s of PCM`, underruns=1, in the same second as the first activity's CDS archive write, 1.3-2.4 s) and passed on every rerun, with main's HAL too: the PipeWire path is unchanged.
- 2026-11-08: `tests/run sandbox` failed on UTM once the AppCDS work and the default sandbox met: every sandboxed app ended with `cannot run <native>/jdk/bin/java: No such file or directory`. The VM's tree is reached through a link (~/apklayer -> ~/apklayer-m1); the sandbox binds the runtime at its real paths in an empty home, so the JDK image's java (and the class path, named through the link) was not there, and a mount point made through the link turned it into an empty directory. cli/sandbox.cpp appView now puts the host's links on the way to the runtime's paths back into the hidden tmpfs, after the runtime's binds. CDS under the sandbox: app JVMs (and their spares) get no dump arguments (sandboxSpawn drops -XX:+RecordDynamicDumpInfo and -Dapklayer.cdsdump: an archive is code other apps' JVMs map, so no app uid writes it); the hub dumps the instance's archive when sandboxed (HubHost), and sandboxed apps map it read-only (-Xlog:cds: the dynamic regions mapped in hello.sandbox).
- 2026-11-08: open items round (input, toasts, notification channels, storage stats, Os.accept, launch order). Toasts queue as NotificationManagerService's mToastQueue (one on screen, in show order, at most 5, cancel of a queued one, show of a queued one keeps its place) and Toast.Callback hears shown and hidden (per process: shortcut). Notification channels are the hub's per package (HubNotificationChannels, NOTIFY_CHANNELS; PreferencesHelper's rules: a later create changes name, description, a lower importance and an unset group; a deleted channel comes back with its settings), kept in `<instance>/notification-channels/<package>`, removed by uninstall and clear-data; every process of the app sees them (groups stay per process). StorageStatsManager: another app's stats and the user's need PACKAGE_USAGE_STATS (no app op store: the op at its default). Os.accept fills in the peer (InetSocketAddress's holder, an IPv4-mapped address as IPv4; UnixSocketAddress's sun_path) and retries EINTR. The first launch's message is queued before Application.onCreate runs, as ActivityThread's launch transaction behind bindApplication (the hub's configuration still runs ahead of it: AppHost.beforeFirstLaunch), so the app's own posts run after the first activity's onCreate. Windows: a dialog a stopped activity shows is hidden with it (WindowManagerService adds it without ADD_FLAG_APP_VISIBLE); a FLAG_ALT_FOCUSABLE_IM window takes keys but is no IME target (mayUseInputMethod). input-inject holds a long press until the field's long click ran (the steps of a stalled app came back to back: a tap). The paused activity's dialog over a newer translucent window was fixed by WindowOrder (65cba81e). Back in a WebView selection stays Chromium's (PLAN). Tests on UTM (its load at 17-26 from other runners): system-apps (26 of 26 with toast, storagestats, accept, launchorder), notifications (with channels), activities, broadcasts, sysprov, view pass; input passed alone (with alt-ime and the late dialog), its scripts stalled at hub start or exit in loaded parallel runs. The toast check waits up to 10 s for the first window (the first frame of a cold app took 7 s on the loaded VM, with or without these changes).
- 2026-11-08: installer, jobs, shared libraries, accounts, patches (PLAN open items). Installer: a committed session stays a session (getSessionInfo, isCommitted/sealed) until its final status, installed from its own directory, and survives a hub restart: committed again with a new status receiver it asks the person again (PackageInstallerSession.markAsSealed); a committed session takes no more writes. Jobs: the hub holds each job's state (HubScheduler): constraints checked against the host (HubJobConstraints: NetworkRequest.canBeSatisfiedBy on the host's network, charging, battery and storage not low), setOverrideDeadline, a waiting job starts on the hub's network or power events, a running one is stopped (ENDPOINT_JOB_STOP, onStopJob with the reason) when its constraint goes; the failure count lives in the hub (JOB_RETRY), so the backoff grows across the app's process ends: this was why WorkManager ran work "before schedule" in a loop (its own attempt count kept growing, the process-held one restarted at 1); schedule() or cancel() of a running job stops it (STOP_REASON_CANCELLED_BY_APP) and its late jobFinished is ignored; JobWorkItems queue in the app (dequeueWork/completeWork, the job ends with the last, work enqueued while it runs is dequeued, undone work runs again). Boot broadcasts are ordered (one app at a time), FLAG_RECEIVER_NO_ABORT ignores an abort. Shared libraries: uninstall refuses a provider of a static/SDK library an installed app uses (cli: DELETE_FAILED_USED_SHARED_LIBRARY, exit 8; the hub answers STATUS_FAILURE_CONFLICT); `<uses-native-library>` checked from target 31 against the build's vendor and partition public library lists (a required missing one: INSTALL_FAILED_MISSING_SHARED_LIBRARY); ApplicationInfo.sharedLibraryInfos (builtin, dynamic, static, SDK, native); com.android.location.provider, javax.obex, com.android.future.usb.accessory, com.android.media.remotedisplay, com.android.mediadrm.signer and com.android.nfc_extras staged as JVM classes (tools/stage root, prebuilts 836cbfd5). Accounts: getAccountsByTypeAndFeatures asks the authenticator's hasFeatures per account. Patches: `+` lines name docs/PATCHES.md (not the gone scripts/ or instancehub flag); meson resets bionic_translation to its revision when an edited patch no longer matches the patched tree; ~/los24 frameworks/native comments edited to match its patch (no rebuild). UTM (own tree ~/wt/a60f, private data home, slots a60f1-3): push (with jobs, retry, ordered boot), installer, sharedlib, accounts, services, broadcasts pass; under a 3-way load (VM load 17) installer, services and broadcasts each failed once on unrelated steps and passed on the 2-way rerun; system-apps skipped (its system apps are not installed in the private data home).
- 2026-11-08: microG push with every app closed (docs/PLAN.md "Plan: background push" 6-7). Installing microG Services turns the instance always on (once per instance, the CLI says so). /system/etc/microg.xml: device registration and Cloud Messaging on by default. - 2026-11-07: Screenshots on NVIDIA (`apklayer screenshot`: "capture failed", SurfaceFlinger `Buffer failed to allocate: -12`). SurfaceFlinger's capture buffer is GPU render target plus CPU read (usage 0xb33), which the graphics HAL asked GBM for as LINEAR | RENDERING (| SCANOUT); NVIDIA's GBM (615.71, RTX 5060 Ti) refuses LINEAR with RENDERING (EINVAL, also through gbm_bo_create_with_modifiers2 with LINEAR) and maps no tiled buffer (gbm_bo_map ENOMEM). LINEAR | SCANOUT allocates, but its EGL renders to no pitch-linear image (glEGLImageTargetTexture2DOES 0x502, SurfaceFlinger aborts in Skia). Now the HAL retries such a buffer tiled (LINEAR dropped) and a single-plane RGBA buffer the driver cannot map is read through the GPU (bridge/hals/graphics/readback.c: the dmabuf as an EGLImage, glReadPixels into a copy at the buffer's stride; host EGL loaded with dlopen). Both run only after a failure: Mesa's path is unchanged. NVIDIA tester box: UiBench 3440x1440 PNG correct; VM: gpu, gpu-window, texture-view pass.

- 2026-11-07: windows and first start. FLAG_DIM_BEHIND draws: a SurfaceFlinger color layer at dimAmount over the display, a child of the window's container under the window (a relative layer -1 to the container drew above the window on our SurfaceFlinger), hidden while the window is not visible (WindowSurface, Robolectric 0022). A sub-window goes with its removed parent window (WindowState.removeImmediately): no relayout, no input (WindowOrder.keys -1). An activity fixed to one orientation keeps its window shape through drag resizes: the shell takes the largest size of the last shape inside the dragged box (Android desktop windowing keeps a fixed app's aspect ratio on a drag; letterboxing does not apply to freeform windows there), so OTR2 dragged to 600x1000 gets 600x517 and every press hits (otr2-aspect: 8 of 8). An orientation turn now fills 85% of the work area like the first portrait window (a landscape turn: 1031x579 on UTM's 1213x992 work area, window-geometry checks it); the first window still keeps a smaller display shape as it is. First start on a fresh data home: DocumentsUI closed because ProvidersCache loads every documents provider's roots one after another under its lock with a 5 s limit and the cold MediaProvider/DownloadProvider/ExternalStorage hosts (databases created) took ~2 s each; at boot count 1 the hub creates the documents providers in parallel (new group first-start: 0 of 3 fresh starts kept Files up before, 3 of 3 after). The `boot settings not written: NullPointerException` did not reproduce (7 fresh starts); the hub now logs its stack.
- 2026-11-08: per-app sandbox, milestone two (`APKLAYER_SANDBOX=1`, still off by default; docs/PLAN.md "Plan: per-app sandbox"): an app's /data is Android's view (its own data; data/local/tmp and data/apklayer read-only; system apps also data/system), no longer keystore2's database and every app's state; Android's groups (own, cache and shared app gids, everybody 9997, inet 3003 with INTERNET; gids 3003/9997 mapped from the top of the subordinate range) instead of the user's 1000; `ShadowHostRealBinder.getCallingUid` is the binder driver's sender euid under the sandbox (the clearCallingIdentity token; oneway calls no longer trust the parcel header); GeoClue, BlueZ and the settings watcher run through apklayerd as the user (`apklayer portal helper`; the buses refused app uids: "The connection is closed"); a launch's intent file is moved into the app's own tmp; `apklayer stop <pkg>` ends sandboxed apps (terminate() skipped a pid whose environment it cannot read and whose command line is an Android process name; push's closed app took the message itself); a spare per app (spawned through sandbox-exec as the app last launched; the binder driver keeps the opener's credentials and a JVM cannot change mount namespace, so no shared spare; the started pipe 0622 for the app uid): UiBench warm 1.91 s sandboxed vs 1.86 s unsandboxed, ~2.3 s before; the shared storage open to every app uid (0777 dirs, default ACL); the native dir's symlink targets (the prebuilts' jars) and, with home-access on a 0750 home, the home's entries in the view; an explicit APKLAYER_PICKER answers before DocumentsUI; system apps managing all storage see the home. The deb ships the AppArmor profile (tools/host-setup/apklayer.apparmor, loaded by postinst where AppArmor has abi 4.0), the render-node rule (tools/host-setup/70-apklayer-render.rules), apklayerd's user unit (not enabled) and depends on uidmap. Tests: the sandbox group checks groups, /data, intent file, GeoClue; binder (an app uid may not add a service), hub.py's WebView profile check and hello-system's sysprops know the sandbox. Suite with the sandbox on (UTM, final code): activities services broadcasts providers pendingintents notifications binder files keystore gpu gpu-window webview push hub-services devices sandbox portal system-apps settings all pass (no `permissions` group exists in tests/groups.toml); the sandbox group needed apklayerd restarted first (an on-demand daemon an earlier group started keeps that client's environment, without the test's picker). Without the sandbox after the changes: binder portal push system-apps webview pass (webview failed once on a cookie check, passed on the rerun); activities could not be installed (another agent's hello.actcaller with another signature in the shared apps dir).

Checked on the NVIDIA tester box (property 196610). setup-runtime.sh refuses a native dir without native-runtime.jar (a CLI built for a source tree had linked every entry of / into the tester's runtime; repaired by hand).
- 2026-11-07: Runtime permissions are asked, as Android's permission screen does: Activity.requestPermissions (and the ActivityResult API, same intent) shows the hub's dialog (HubPermissionPrompt on HubInstallDialog's window) once per permission group, "Allow <app> to <group>?" with PermissionController's groups and texts, Allow / Don't allow. Answers go to the instance's permissions-granted / permissions-revoked, the manager's store (`apklayer permissions` lists them): a granted or manager-revoked permission is answered without a question, a first "Don't allow" is asked again (shouldShowRequestPermissionRationale true), a second is final. The result arrives on the main thread through Activity.dispatchRequestPermissionsResult while the app's looper runs. Unasked runtime permissions are no longer held (apps targeting API 22 or lower keep the install grant). Found on the way: Activity's request short circuit asked Context.getPermissionRequestState, which Robolectric's null binder answered GRANTED, so no request ever reached the host (ShadowHostContextImpl answers it now). RoleManager's request intent: the role dialog ("Set <app> as your default browser app?"), RESULT_CANCELED at once for phone roles without telephony: QUIK starts. Tests run with APKLAYER_PERMISSIONS=grant (tests/lib/vm.py, the old install grant); group `permissions` (hello-permissions) turns prompts on and answers by test input.

- 2026-11-07: Apps under a decimal-comma host locale (nl_NL, id_ID) drew no text or icons and UiBench died at start (`progress_large_material`: "The Path must start at (0,0) and end at (1,1)"). HotSpot's `setlocale(LC_ALL, "")` gave `LC_NUMERIC` the host's comma, so Android's native float parsing (VectorDrawable path data, fonts.xml axes, PathInterpolator) read "0.2" wrong. The runtime shim's `JNI_OnLoad` now puts `LC_NUMERIC` back to C, as in every Android process; Java's own locale stays the user's. The gpu group runs UiBench a second time under nl_NL.UTF-8.

- 2026-11-05: fences are real sync_files: no more `Fence: sync_file_info returned NULL` / `Invalid present fence`. Where the fake fences came from: only the composer's present fences. The arm64 VMs and the prebuilts still ran a composer from 2026-10-27 that returned an eventfd as every present fence (the sync_file composer of the frame-pacing round was built but never installed for arm64 nor locked). That composer exported one syncobj, created signaled, at every present, so every fence carried the composer's start time: SurfaceFlinger's frame timeline found every present before its frame's start and dropped it ("Unknown jank", present time replaced by the end time, 64 of 64 frames). Now (PresentFence.h) the syncobj is signaled again at each present (DRM_IOCTL_SYNCOBJ_SIGNAL: a fresh stub fence stamped with the hand-over time) and exported. Without a render node (the Halium phones) the fence is a GPU fence through EGL (GpuFence.h: EGL_ANDROID_native_fence_sync on a 1x1 pbuffer, libEGL loaded with dlopen; Mali there); with neither, an eventfd, and a warning says so. The fence signals at the hand-over to the shell, not at the compositor's real present: userspace cannot make a kernel fence that signals later (sw_sync needs debugfs and root), so PRESENT_FENCE_IS_NOT_RELIABLE stays. The other fence paths were already real: EGL's swap fence is the host's eglDupNativeFenceFDANDROID (virgl: sync_file) or -1, Vulkan's comes from the host's semaphore SYNC_FD export, the mapper returns -1, vdisplay and camera only wait on or pass back fences, Codec2 passes none. Parallels (virgl), glbench window 772x579: SurfaceFlinger logged 355 `sync_file_info returned NULL` and 356 `Invalid present fence` per 10 s of the run (~50 per second while drawing), UiBench (gpu-window) 47 per 10 s in the app's log and 53 in SurfaceFlinger's; now 0 in both. glbench clear 49.1 -> 59.8 fps (p95 29.3 -> 18.3 ms), draws 54.0 -> 59.9 fps (p95 26.4 -> 17.3 ms) (the vsync timeline of the frame-pacing round, now installed). Frame timeline: 0 of 64 frames "Unknown jank" (64 before). The EGL path tested on Parallels by forcing it (`APKLAYER_PRESENT_FENCE=gpu` in the composer's environment): no log lines, 0 unknown. New group `fences` (glbench window; counts both lines in every instance log and the frame timeline's unusable present times); run-android.py takes `GLBENCH_DUMP_ARGS`. Groups on Parallels: fences, gpu, gpu-window, texture-view, vulkan, otr2, codec2, view pass; webview, activities, native-surface fail on main's libraries as well. Not checked: the Halium phone and the x86 PC (unreachable).
- 2026-11-05: asynchronous JobScheduler jobs (WorkManager's SystemJobService): Robolectric's ShadowJobService only recorded jobFinished, so a job returning true from onStartJob never ended for HostJobs (stayed bound, never rescheduled). ShadowHostJobService (extends ShadowHostService: stopSelf in a job no longer risks the IntentService ClassCastException) lets the framework's jobFinished, setNotification and update*NetworkBytes reach HostJobs' callback; HostJobs ends a run once (jobFinished from any thread, or onStartJob returning false, which used to leave the service bound), applies the job's backoff policy (JobSchedulerService's formula) instead of a fixed 30 s, settles the job before unbinding (Robolectric's destroy idles the main looper), posts setNotification's notification; ShadowHostJobScheduler keeps one pending list per process, cancelAll cancels the plans too, enqueue plans once. New hello-system scenario `asyncjobs` (system-apps: 24 scenarios). Parallels: jobs and asyncjobs pass (job 79: finished, again in 10000 ms, ran again, finished); push job passes.

- 2026-11-05: Ubuntu Touch install finished (Volla X23, deb 0.3.1~git20261105 built on Parallels, tested from the extracted deb as phablet; docs/HALIUM.md "The apt install on the phone"). The user's two problems had four causes: the runtime bundle held links to the build machine (`android-root`, `system-apps`) and every command died setting it up; the manager used instance `default` (the phone's is `ut`) and so failed at binder and offered pkexec, which Ubuntu Touch does not have; the runtime setup and the installer ran PATH's `java` (the phone's is named in ~/.config/apklayer/env); and Lomiri's scale reaches apps only as GRID_UNIT_PX (the Wayland output says 1), so the manager drew its desktop layout at desktop size. Fixed in runtime-bundle.sh, common/fetch.cpp (incomplete runtimes refused/redone), cli (setup without the automatic bundle setup, java step, `--version` defaultInstance, findJava for setup-runtime and install, binder command per platform, WebView switch file per instance without root: the old pkexec step would have chmodded the phone's /data, which is the device's Android userdata), manager (SLINT_SCALE_FACTOR from GRID_UNIT_PX/8, the CLI's default instance, java row, no-pkexec message), apklayer-binder.service after Halium's binderfs mount. Checked on the phone: `apklayer setup` rc 0 (runtime 0.3.1 set up in 29 s, 10 system apps in 3 min), the manager at 1.75 (compact layout, bottom navigation, instance ut, steps Done), Calculator installed through the manager's Install dialog, drawn on the Mali GPU from the CLI and from `lomiri-app-launch` of its X-Lomiri-Touch entry (720x1508, maximized).
- 2026-11-05: OTR2 input after a window resize (maximize, drag, scale change): the picture filled the window but a press at x reached the game at x x old/new (UTM probe after a maximize at 200%: presses at 360-400 px selected the portrait drawn at 548 px, factor 1.5 = 2388/1594). Root cause: OTR2 reads its window size only at APP_CMD_INIT_WINDOW and maps every pointer by it; a size change reaches a GameActivity game as APP_CMD_WINDOW_RESIZED, which OTR2's handler only logs (libgame.so disassembly: the WINDOW_RESIZED and CONFIG_CHANGED cases return; INIT_WINDOW calls its onWindowChanged), and it kept rendering at its old size (setBuffersGeometry 1396x785) that SurfaceFlinger scales to the window. On Android a maximize crosses OTR2's size buckets (sw600dp, large; its configChanges lack smallestScreenSize and screenLayout), so the activity is relaunched and gets a new window; the host resizes in place. Fix: `NativeWindowResize` (host) hands a GameActivity its own surface again (surfaceDestroyed, surfaceCreated, surfaceChanged: TERM_WINDOW then INIT_WINDOW) once a new size has settled for 300 ms (a drag re-announces once). libapklayer_egl connects to the window before reading its size: Surface's cached default size is refreshed only by connect and dequeueBuffer, so a surface created right after a resize had the old size until its first swap. The UI is unchanged (the game draws the same layout at the new size). Tests (UTM, all pass): otr2-input (tap at 100%, click and tap at 200%: drag resize, maximize, restore), otr2-probe (scale 100 -> 200 -> 100 % while the game runs, click and tap), gpu-window, view, activities, window-geometry, input, native-surface, otr2. otr2-aspect (windows of other shapes after drag resizes, the portrait located from the drawn frame): at 100% 1600x500, 900x850 and 1137x613 hit; at 200% only 1137x613 hits, 1600x500 and 900x850 miss (open, not yet explained; the game's buffers keep the window's shape, 0.875 of it); a portrait 600x1000 window misses at both scales: the landscape-locked game picks a landscape buffer (1000x600) that SurfaceFlinger stretches into the portrait window and maps touches in its landscape space (Android would letterbox a sensorLandscape activity in landscape bounds; docs/PLAN.md). Parallels: not completed (title wait counted green pixels for a 797x448 window; its window is 579x325; now a share).
- 2026-11-05: OTR2 (Off the Road 2) took no mouse or touch input on the owner's x86 PC: the dialog's OK button and Back did nothing. Root cause: the manifest's `screenOrientation` (OTR2: sensorLandscape) never reached the host. Robolectric's `Activity.getRequestedOrientation` starts at -1 and nothing seeded it from the ActivityInfo, so `AppHost.followOrientation` (docs/history P2.3) never reshaped the window: OTR2 got a portrait window (448x797 on UTM, 417x742 @200% on the PC) while the game, locked to landscape, drew a 797x448 landscape surface that was stretched into it; its pointer coordinates (portrait) then fell outside or beside its landscape UI. Fix: `ActivityTask` sets the requested orientation from `info.screenOrientation` before `onCreate`, so the shell makes a landscape window (797x448 on UTM). Not a scale bug: the shell already sends content pixels (logical x scale) and the host injects them unchanged (checked at 100% and 200%, tap and click). New group `otr2-input` (tests/vm/otr2-input.py): the title screen's second character portrait is selected by a touch tap at 100% and 200% and by a mouse click at 200% (frame colour compared in screenshots before and after); before the fix the same taps selected nothing. `HostInput` touch debug lines (`apklayer.debugkeys`) now print the position.

- 2026-11-05: OTR2 on the x86 PC (arm64-only, Berberis) ran at 12-15 fps: Swappy (Android Frame Pacing, in libgame.so) derives SurfaceFlinger's offset from Display.getPresentationDeadlineNanos, which Robolectric left 0, and its vsync filter then released a frame only every ~67-70 ms (strace: two filter threads sleeping ~70 ms before each wake). ShadowHostDisplay reports a refresh period as the deadline (what SurfaceFlinger reports with its default 1 ms sf offset): 30 fps, Swappy's own pacing (58 fps with swappy.disable=1, GPU 94% busy). bionic_translation 0040: a byte copy of a pthread attr (the bridge's proxy pthread_create) no longer frees the original's glibc attr (SIGSEGV in pthread_attr_destroy at every second OTR2 start). Test: native attr-copy.
- 2026-11-05: OTR2 black screen with microG installed (Parallels default instance): Play Games' GamesResolutionActivity calls Activity.startIntentSenderForResult with microG's PendingIntent, which Robolectric's shadow never sent; the translucent activity stayed on top and the game paused. startIntentSenderForResult now sends through the hub (PI_SEND_FOR_RESULT) and the PendingIntent's activity answers onActivityResult; an activity that finishes in onCreate (microG's GamesSignInActivity without an account) answers at once instead of an NPE in ActivityTask.launchFirst; a start handed to an app process that is exiting goes to a new process. Test: pendingintents forresult.
- 2026-11-05: frame pacing (owner's x86 PC, glbench window 1600x1200, vsync on: draws scene 55.2 -> 59.9 fps, p95 frame 25.6 -> 17.5 ms; clear 59.1 -> 59.9 fps, p95 18.3 -> 17.7; swap interval 0: 59 -> 760 fps clear, 492 fps draws): the composer has a vsync timeline (VsyncTimeline.h: steady grid at the output's period, phase-locked to the shell's frame callbacks; getDisplayKnownVsyncSample answers from it), real sync_file present fences (PresentFence.h: DRM syncobj) with capability PRESENT_FENCE_IS_NOT_RELIABLE (their signal time is not a vsync); libapklayer_egl caches the EGLImages, textures and FBOs per buffer (no import/create/destroy per swap), eglSwapInterval sets the Android window's swap interval (0 = async BufferQueue), eglPresentationTimeANDROID sets the buffer timestamp. gpu, gpu-window, texture-view, otr2 pass on the PC.
- 2026-11-05: tests/bench/glbench: one GLES render loop natively and through apklayer (hello-glbench), bpftrace scripts for the swap path; finding: Android windows run at ~50 fps because SurfaceFlinger's vsync model has no phase (docs/PLAN.md "Graphics performance").
- 2026-11-05: hardware plugin API public: plugin.h with the ABI version, fully specified domain headers, plugin dirs for out-of-tree plugins, the apklayer-dev deb (headers, apklayer-plugin.pc), docs/PLUGINS.md, examples/plugin-vibrator-hello, group plugins.

Results in tests/corpus/results.md; open items in PLAN.

Sensors (sensorfw, iio) and vibrator (hfd, feedbackd, force feedback) host backends; an unset domain runs the best probe().

Every app's target SDK is now its APK's (it was 23 for all, in ApplicationInfo and the compatibility switches). - Hardware providers: fake sensors, GNSS and vibrator behind AIDL HALs, `providers.toml` / `apklayer providers`, apps' SensorManager and Vibrator are the real ones (group `hardware`); a service the stack restarted no longer survives `apklayer stop` (children get the default signal mask). The noble kernel modules are a recommendation, not a dependency.
- codec2 group hang: `ro.vendor.api_level` made the soft AV1 interface query gralloc through servicemanager, and `apklayer exec --probe` ran without a stack on the dead stack's property store (`servicemanager.ready=true`), so libbinder slept forever: the probe runs on a stack with a bounded wait, and the stack daemon removes its property store when it ends. Hub package scan skips install staging dirs.
- Manager updater (releases.json, SHA256-checked deb and runtime downloads, atomic runtime switch keeping one previous version), first-run screen (binder check, runtime, system apps, microG, F-Droid client from their own repositories), `apklayer --version`, converterAbi recorded per install and `apklayer reconvert <pkg>|--all-stale` (data kept), the data home's data-format record; group reconvert.

Test app hello-rotated. Full suite on UTM after the correctness, cleanup and corpus merges: 43 of 44 (vulkan-present).

- Correctness round: getAuthToken for an account the caller cannot see asks the user (hub dialog); ro.build.version.incremental never `eng.<user>`; same-process startService/stopService run the app's own service; ServiceManager answers names only the native stack registers ("SurfaceFlinger" for microG's DroidGuard); tests for native path mapping and rebind across a dying host. - microG signature spoofing follows LineageOS's full rule (microG-signed, Google's certificate in fake-signature); Meet's error 9 was the missing microG Companion; SigningCheck covers it.
- microG's DroidGuard bind (own package, action+package, service in the `:unstable` process) reaches its service: a component-less intent is resolved before the process is chosen; a same-process bind binds our service or returns false (never Robolectric's null-binder bind); binds during a service's destroy wait for a fresh instance; IME labels without other apps' resources; scenario `services own`. microG 0.3.17 gets a DroidGuard result and reaches Google's account lookup.
- App data per instance confirmed (webview and webview-input pass in parallel; the webview group checks the app's dataDir and WebView profile are the instance's).
- Fennec YouTube video: the hub lost GeckoView's media-process binding (it kills itself in onUnbind and rebinds on every codec change): the launch-failure count was reset under the package but counted under package#process (after 3 normal exits binds got null), and a relaunch depended on the order of process exit, binder death and failed delivery (each now calls ensureHost). EGL14/EGL15/EGLExt/GLES10-32 call Android's real JNI (Robolectric faked them: glGenTextures 0). runtime_shim reads APKLAYER_RUNTIME_DEBUG once (getenv raced Gecko's setenv). Checks: services respawn, native-surface SurfaceTexture in another process, fennec-media YouTube time advances.
- Keys and the IME go to the topmost window that takes key focus: a long press's floating toolbar (a FLAG_NOT_FOCUSABLE PopupWindow) took them (microG's sign-in stopped typing); a missing system service logs Android's one warning line; group webview-input; instance.inject press/release. microG 0.3.17 (microG's repo): Google answered the 0.2.24 client with Error=MissingDroidguard.
- `apklayer run` of a running app hands the intent or launcher start to that process through the hub (RELAUNCH; new activity or onNewIntent per launchMode, launcher start brings the task forward; servicetool call; the shell asks to raise the window with xdg-activation): one Gecko, second URL loaded.
- microG: the text magnifier crashed the JVM (SurfaceSession's native create returned Robolectric's 0: a null SurfaceComposerClient): SurfaceSession and PixelCopy run their real code; with the real WebView provider CookieManager, WebStorage and WebViewDatabase are the provider's (microG's sign-in read a null cookie from Robolectric's store). Groups magnifier, webview (cookie check).

- Windows: per-window scale (wp_fractional_scale_v1, wp_viewporter; the bar drawn at the window's scale), the first size fitted to configure_bounds (the unmaximize hack is gone, maximize fills the work area), every wl_output tracked (NewPipe's 329x1280 strip came from a stale output); window-geometry group on both VMs.
- apklayer-manager (`manager/`, its own meson project): Slint 1.18 C++ (prebuilt, fetched with pinned hashes by manager/fetch-slint.sh) with Material Components 1.1; Material 3 navigation bar/rail, apps (install, open, stop, clear data, uninstall, info), instances (create with binder setup through pkexec, remove), per-instance Android property overrides (`<instance>/overrides.prop`, loaded first and last at stack start), device status. CLI: `list --tsv`, `info`, `clear-data`, `stop <pkg>`, `instances`, `instance create|remove`, `prop [set|unset]`, `status`. Checked by manager backend-test and tests/ui.sh on UTM.
- After processes carry their Android name: `apklayer stop`, the corpus runner, the test helpers and hangdump find processes by APKLAYER_INSTANCE, recorded pids or `pgrep -x java`, not by "apklayer.host.Main" in a command line; the shell always logs buffer imports (first-frame checks need no debug flag).
- Media: Fennec plays WebM, MP4 and YouTube with sound, NewPipe plays YouTube with sound (tests/vm/fennec-media.sh, newpipe-media.sh, groups fennec-media and newpipe-media). Fixes: robolectric 0014 uses the real Surface(SurfaceTexture) native; bt 0030 dladdr returns the full path (Gecko's utility process found no decoder libraries); the Codec2 adapter accepts an end-of-stream with timestamp -1 (every H.264 video ended in UNKNOWN_ERROR); the hub fills audioserver's permission controller with the framework's AudioServerPermissionProvider (every Java AudioTrack failed with -38). Installed on both VMs: libapklayer_codec2 (f023deb2), bt 0030, overlay.
- PackageInstaller through the hub: sessions (create, write, commit, abandon; preapproval), legacy ACTION_INSTALL_PACKAGE/VIEW of an APK and ACTION_DELETE, split APKs and .apks bundles (`apklayer install base.apk split_*.apk`), updates keep data and check signer and version; the hub runs the host install, refreshes every app's package manager and sends PACKAGE_ADDED/REPLACED/REMOVED; non-privileged installers get the user confirmation, drawn by the hub in the instance's shell ("install unknown apps" kept per installer in `<instance>/install-sources`). F-Droid searched, installed, opened and uninstalled OpenCalc. Generic fixes: system broadcasts reach RECEIVER_NOT_EXPORTED receivers, FLAG_RECEIVER_REGISTERED_ONLY honoured, the ApkAssets cache is invalidated when the file changes, installed splits load (LoadedApk split paths, splitNames). New group `installer`.
- Corpus (UTM, merged main, 2026-11-04): 18 of 22 apps pass both launches with content (uibench, otr2, newpipe 0.28, nextcloud, telegram, whatsapp, cpu-z, gpstest, wireguard, shelter, vibrations, astro, device-info, sensors, microg, openttd; openvpn first launch); open: DRM Info (ClassCastException in its protobuf-lite init), DocumentsUI ("overlay" service missing), OpenVPN's second launch, Unciv (virgl on UTM; draws on Parallels).
- Audio: the HAL probe configures only output ports and leaves a device config another patch uses alone (the 'left channel lost' failure was a probe that aborted on the HAL's 4 ports and a silent capture); audio-host checks the tool's verdict first and reads the monitor to silence. The audio/codec start flags nothing read are gone (the stack starts what is installed). The path layer reads its environment once at load (getenv in every stat/open raced mozglue's setenv). Full suite groups for audio and media pass on both VMs.
- Corpus fixes: dex2jvm adds no checkcast where an interface type is required (DRM Info: R8 removed an empty interface, the join of two branches was Object); the conversion cache is keyed by the dex2jvm.jar hash; component hosts get the device's Build values before Application.onCreate (OpenVPN's :openvpn process returned early on "robolectric" and its log reader blocked the main thread). Unciv on UTM stays black by decision (virgl's sampler limit fails silently; no reliable detection). DocumentsUI gives up on six slow-starting providers (component-host start ~1.3 s each): in progress.
- ApkAssets cache keyed by load flags too (a shared-library load of the WebView got the normal-load copy: package id 127, "Package not found: com.android.webview"); system-apps starts from a fresh account store; ANDROID_ID and the process's own providers in component hosts (storage volumes before providers); `tools/host-setup/webview.sh [apk]` installs the provider. VM disks were full (agents' ~/wt copies, 30 GB): cleaned; agents now remove theirs.
- Manager: `apklayer permissions` (grant/revoke runtime permissions per instance) with switches in the UI; CLI errors shown; Slint installed with the binary; x86_64 hashes; the window shell ends only with the session's last stack. Tests: Back into the instance's own window (instance.inject) instead of kernel keys; VM test outputs per group.
- Corpus round with current F-Droid apps (18 new, 5 refreshed, both VMs): KeyStore.getEntry(alias, null) returns the key as libcore does (Element); the log handler writes to its own stderr copy (VLC reads fd 2 back: a full pipe hung every writer and the JVM's exit); providers are attached to the Application (as Android); Os.strerror is real (ErrnoException messages); dex2jvm's constructor scan keeps tracked objects across writes on other branches, and constructors R8 deleted but still calls get forwarders (Shelter); GNSS capabilities never null (GPSTest); the app registers with the hub at its first account/activity-manager use (Nextcloud from attachBaseContext); tests/corpus/run.py CORPUS_INSTANCE=<id>.
- Audio: the permission feed follows audioserver restarts, hub installs and late-registering apps; permission_checker published by the hub (recording works); the audio-track probe plays out its tail (47760/47760); new group audio-late-install.
- NativeLoader: the bionic search path holds only Android libraries (apps got a second, bionic-linked libc_bio/libpthread_bio); libapklayer-gles1 by plain dlopen (dlmopen workaround gone); eglReleaseThread sets the no-context GL hooks (libapklayer_egl 981c26d3, libapklayer_audioserver 425632dc on both VMs).
- Build values before app classes (static initialisers), release identity (user/release-keys), one platform certificate per instance; the per-app ShadowBuild.setType("userdebug") is now scoped to WebView provider creation.
- Settings survive stop: a component host waits for its other loopers' running work (SettingsState saves on its own handler thread) before it exits; the hub ends its hosts with SIGTERM + 4 s; a spare host exits on SIGTERM; system_config before the app's providers (a fresh data home crashed the settings host); sysprov fixed (non-privileged writes, dictionary refusal) plus an androidid scenario across stop.
- `run` fails after 10 s when the window shell does not listen (the stuck shell is ended; spawn returns a detached child's pid); `instance.stop()` reports unclean service exits and the gpu smoke fails on them; SurfaceFlinger's exit-time SIGSEGV (and navigateUpTo) confirmed gone since bt 0029 / c28ea48.
- Spare component host: provider/service host start 1.2-1.3 s -> 0.46-0.6 s (five-provider burst 8.4 s -> 3.3 s); DocumentsUI passes the corpus again. Full suite on UTM before it: 40 of 41 groups (only vulkan-present: no Vulkan presentation on the VMs).
- Device identity from one property store: `Build.*` and Java `SystemProperties` are copied from the instance's properties (tree build.prop plus apklayer's product in `bridge/native/apklayer.prop`; fingerprint and ABI lists set by `cli/stack.cpp`). Java code no longer reads "robolectric". New check `build` in hello-system (system-apps now expects 12).

## 2026-11-03: systematic compatibility (fakes report themselves, JDK scan, package info, install check, device identity)
- Every fake reports itself: `[fake] <service> <call>` on the first call per process (Robolectric null/deep proxies through `ShadowHostServiceManager`, missing or Robolectric-shadowed managers in `ShadowHostContextImpl`, `HostMediaStub`; `FakeLog`). Corpus oracle (`tests/corpus/run.py`, `content.py`): the window must differ from a capture taken before the launch and not be one colour, a second launch, `fakes.md` ranks fakes by the number of apps that hit them; each app runs under the VM lock.
- `JdkScan` (apklayer.tools) lists the libcore members native libraries (JNI strings) and app bytecode use that the JDK lacks: 1037 libraries and 759k classes scanned, 84 uncovered (most noise or ART internals). Added through `JdkMembers` (copied into java.base by JdkPatch): `Thread.stop(Throwable)`, `InetAddress.parseNumericAddress/clearDnsCache`, `DateFormat.set24HourTimePref/is24Hour`, `ECParameterSpec` curve name, `ByteBuffer.setAccessible/isAccessible`. `scripts/libcore-tests-hotspot.sh`: libcore's own tests on HotSpot, 1812 run, 123 fail.
- Hub package info: the uid is set once at parse time (as the package scanner does), so every component info the framework's generator copies carries it; the per-component uid loop and the uid-lookup overrides are gone (Robolectric's tables answer); a package installed after a JVM attached registers on its first lookup. Assessment: AlarmManagerService standalone in the hub needs ActivityManagerInternal/PackageManagerInternal (abstract, ~300 methods) and more, 800-1500 lines of glue against HostJobs' 84: HostJobs stays.
- `apklayer install` verifies its output (`VerifyInstall`): every DEX class in the jar, ASM structural check, java resources and service files reachable; a failure installs and caches nothing (cache key v13). Telegram: 12,166 of 12,166 classes in 0.9 s.
- Instances run side by side: an instance other than `default` opens `/dev/binderfs/{binder,hwbinder,vndbinder}-<id>` for `/dev/{binder,hwbinder,vndbinder}` (path layer, `bridge/native/paths.c`); `scripts/vm-binder-setup.sh <id>...` creates them. Checked: UiBench in `default` and `t1` at the same time, two servicemanagers as context managers, both windows up. Upstream edits are patch files now (`patches/bionic_translation`, `patches/aosp`, one `patches/apply`); the VM's bionic_translation tree was brought in line with the series (two comment-only differences, patch 0025 applied) and rebuilt.
- Apps and the WebView provider have their own child-first class loaders (`AppClassLoader`); Guava/BouncyCastle relocation and `APKLAYER_PROVIDER_FIRST` are gone (post-processing key v14: apps reinstall). Checked: UiBench, Aegis, NewPipe, microG (sign-in page in the WebView), Nextcloud, WebView probe.
- `apklayer screenshot`: the vdisplay sink registers its virtual-display token (`apklayer.vdisplay.<stack>`), `libapklayer_screenshot` captures it through SurfaceFlinger (`ScreenshotClient::captureDisplay`, CPU-readable linear buffer from the GBM allocator); the corpus judges the app's own window. AppOpsManager is initialized before any application exists (its static initializer ran Application.onCreate and deadlocked Nextcloud's WorkManager against it).
- Per-app linker namespace (bionic_translation patch 0026): an app's own libraries resolve in its namespace first, the device's in theirs (Joplin's bundled OpenSSL no longer replaces BoringSSL; the rename script is gone). Patch 0027: bionic stdio FILE functions on glibc (no more "invalid stdio handle"). App processes end like Android's, without native static destructors. AndroidCAStore lives in HostTrust (Conversations reaches its welcome screen). Telegram reaches its intro. OpenTTD needs GLES1, which the host's Mesa lacks (open). The native dir's lib*_bio copies (loaded before APKLAYER_BT) were from 10-29: replaced with today's build.
- Robolectric overlays are patches: `patches/robolectric/0001-0013` against external/robolectric (staged once into `<native>/aosp/robolectric-src`), replacing 11 copied files and the SandboxLoaderPatch bytecode rewrite.
- `scripts/` is gone: meson builds the shim, btload, preload, aprop and the ndk stubs; `tools/build`, `tools/stage`, `tools/gen`, `tools/host-setup`, `tools/debug`; the bionic_translation cfg is a checked-in file (first match wins), `android_stubs.c` is tracked; no sed edits remain. The corpus bar is one frame plus a live process within 30 s.
- Windows: inactive windows keep their activity resumed and only lose window focus (OTR2, Logseq draw); vdisplay installs its configure callback after the virtual display exists; shell readiness through a flock and a ready pipe; shell client fds valid before the first Wayland events; hardware-bitmap shadows removed (hello-hwbitmap passes).
- System providers: the real MediaProvider and SettingsProvider by default (install records provider authorities; /storage, /sdcard, /data/media/0 served from the instance's storage by the path layer; MessageQueue fd listeners fire (HostFdEvents); ABX through the framework's BinaryXml); /data per instance (keystore2 DB, SurfaceFlinger cache); the hub writes BOOT_COUNT as the system. system-apps 12/12 in two instances at once; Etar draws against the real CalendarProvider.
- Hub: the activities `again` flake (two shells, a queued second launch lost, a start during creation made a new instance) and the services "hub disconnected" failure fixed; one connection binds several services, onBind per intent, onRebind; installs commit atomically (info.properties last); a package installed while a component host runs is known (`late` phase). Keystore namespaces per app: libbinder's getCallingUid returns the work source for our own Linux uid (patches/aosp/frameworks/native/0005; built, installed in ~/android-arm64). Optional stack services restart (at most 5 times); hub readiness through a pipe from the hub JVM, no timed retries; vintf fragments per instance.
- Parallel check on main: 12 of 13 groups pass 4 at a time (gpu, keystore, services, broadcasts, providers, activities, notifications, pendingintents, hub-services, system-apps, files, view) in 152 s against 565 s sequential; webview regressed (renderer never reports).
- Fennec without Gecko-specific code: bt 0028 (getenv HOME set by the app), robolectric 0014 (Surface parcel natives), process name before attach, onCreate in every process; text fills traced to virgl (tests/vm/gles-int-branch.c). Tests: per-instance input injection through the shell (`APKLAYER_TEST_INPUT`, tests/vm/input-inject.py) lets the window groups run in parallel; portal falls back correctly (HubActivityClient.route). WebView's `--no-sandbox` switch file is copied into the instance /data by `run`.
- Caller identity: a oneway call is named by the uid its request header carries, accepted only for a running app's registered uid or the hub's 1000 (REGISTER refuses a uid other than the installed one); checkCallingPermission/checkCallingOrSelfPermission run Android's code; providers ask the hub for uri grants; account visibility follows AccountManagerService.resolveAccountVisibility (signature, GET_ACCOUNTS before O, explicit visibility). New refusal phases: services oneway, providers canonicalize/denied, accounts hidden.
- GL: ES1 contexts through gfxstream's GLES_CM translator (hardware/google/gfxstream, unmodified, built on the VM: libapklayer-gles1, `tools/build gles1`) when the host has no ES1; Android's libGLESv1_CM dispatches through our EGL's hook table; the ES2 stand-in is gone (tests/vm/gles1-translator.c passes on both VMs). Unciv on UTM: virgl claims 32 fragment samplers and a shader using more than 16 kills the context (tests/vm/gles-sampler-limit.c); it draws on Parallels. Fennec page text draws on Parallels (the UTM failure is virgl's).
- Corpus recheck: Lemuroid (startService from another thread returns before onCreate), Puzzles (bionic executables via btload, navigateUpTo), ASTRO, AnkiDroid, Rocks'n'Diamonds, Joplin, TIC-80 and the libgdx games draw (tests/corpus/results.md).
- bionic_translation 0029: sem_clockwait, sem_timedwait_monotonic_np and sem_getvalue unwrap the semaphore (glibc decremented bt's stored pointer: SurfaceFlinger's OneShotTimer threads spun at 133% CPU and crashed with 139/135); the stack logs unclean service exits at stop; navigateUpTo follows Task.navigateUpTo (activities phase `up`). The "content" service for code that calls it directly (ContactsProvider waited forever once an account existed). Startup: pre-instrumented framework jars (PreInstrument, `tools/build instrument`), the shared font/ICU asset cache, a larger JVM symbol table (warm UiBench ~3.5 -> ~2.5 s, Telegram ~4.7 -> ~3.6 s). Full suite: 36 of 39 groups in 338 s, 4 at a time.
- ES1 deployment reverted on the VMs: with the new libapklayer_egl, `tools/build cfg` maps libGLESv1_CM to Android's, and SurfaceFlinger crashed in it (its GLES2 calls bound to the v1 trampolines read a hook table our EGL fills only for ES1 contexts). Both VMs run the previous EGL with the host's libGLESv1_CM until our EGL sets a full gl_hooks_t for every context, as Android's libEGL does.
- GL: our EGL keeps Android's per-context hook tables (ES2/3 host entry points, ES1 translator, no-context no-ops, set on every eglMakeCurrent and at load), so Android's libGLESv1_CM is safe in every process (aarch64); app JVMs honour APKLAYER_ANDROID_LIBS; libapklayer-gles1 loads through glibc's dlmopen and reports its texture limits. OpenTTD draws its menu on both VMs. Installed on both VMs: libapklayer_egl (eeee4d51), preload, libapklayer-gles1, cfg.
- System settings: the hub writes what system_server writes at boot (HubBootSettings: BOOT_COUNT, DEVICE_PROVISIONED, USER_SETUP_COMPLETE, LOCATION_MODE from GeoClue, ENABLED_INPUT_METHODS/DEFAULT_INPUT_METHOD; `apklayer ime enable|disable <pkg>`); android_id stable (SettingsProvider wiped the SSAID table because getInstalledPackages was empty: robolectric 0010); component hosts finish due handler work on SIGTERM; app data per instance (`<data home>/apklayer/<n>/app-data/<pkg>`, the default instance migrates at launch); the path layer covers renameat2, linkat, readlinkat, symlinkat, creat, fchownat, xattrs, mkstemp/mkdtemp, scandir, freopen, mkfifo, inotify.
- Fennec: processes carry their Android process name in /proc/self/cmdline (Process.setArgV0); own-package services run in their declared process through the hub, also from component hosts and rewritten app calls (MediaManager of ":media" ran inside the tab process and aborted); a component host's own service stays local. YouTube opens.

### 2026-11-07 - gsi_x86_64 libberberis_arm64: errno of host calls reaches the guest (nbr agent)
- Why: with glibc-host-thread-id-handoff the guest's errno is its own thread record's, so errno set by a proxied host call (clock_gettime, stat64, ftell ...) never reached guest code. binary_translation 0003 wraps every host function's trampoline (MakeTrampolineCallable) to carry errno both ways.
- Command (OrbStack, `flock -n ~/los24-build.lock`): `patches/apply aosp/frameworks/libs/binary_translation ~/los24/frameworks/libs/binary_translation`, then `cd ~/los24 && source build/envsetup.sh && export APKLAYER_NATIVE_BRIDGE=arm64 && breakfast gsi_x86_64 userdebug && m libberberis_arm64`.
- Result (17:26-17:43, 16:12, Kati regenerated; ninja 57 steps): built, libberberis_arm64.so sha256 6667fa1a... (exports WrapHostCallForGuestErrno); 0003 stays applied in the tree. Not yet run on the PC (it was off).
- Second run (same day, same command under the lock): on the PC the proxied clock_gettime's errno still did not arrive: the proxy libraries' stubs are registered with is_host_func=false, which 0003 skipped. 0003 now wraps every trampoline; the old 0003 is reversed in the tree (`git apply -R` of the previous patch file) and the new one applied with patches/apply.
- Result of the second run (20:03, 11:28): built, libberberis_arm64.so sha256 691a6802...; on the PC native-bridge (errno ok, guest fault recovered) and native-bridge-vulkan pass with it. Staged only in the PC's ~/apklayer-x86/nbr prebuilts copy (since removed); the prebuilts branch still has aa543ebe.

### 2026-11-05 - libapklayer_composer (present fences signaled at each present; fences agent)
- Why: the composer exported one syncobj created signaled at every present: all present fences carried the composer's start time, so SurfaceFlinger's frame timeline found each present before its frame's start ("Unknown jank", present = end time). PresentFence.h now signals the syncobj anew at each present (fresh stub fence, timestamp = the hand-over) and, without a render node, passes the composition's GPU fence. Header and Composer.cpp only, no graph change.

### 2026-11-05 - libapklayer_composer (frame pacing agent)
- Why: SurfaceFlinger has no vsync timeline (getDisplayKnownVsyncSample UNSUPPORTED, shell frame callbacks do not form a model): windows run at ~50 fps. - Command (OrbStack): `flock -n ~/los24-build.lock` around `cd ~/los24 && source build/envsetup.sh && breakfast gsi_arm64 userdebug && m libapklayer_servicetool`; the output goes only into the agent's own lib64 copies.
### 2026-11-04 — libapklayer_audioserver: the --track probe plays out what follows the marker (audio agent)
- Why: audio-track counted 47601 of the tone's 47760 non-silent frames: its last 161 frames were still in the HAL ring and PipeWire (~100 ms past the mixer's marker) when the probe stopped and the stack exited. The probe now keeps writing silence until the presented timestamp passes the tone.
- Command (OrbStack, under `flock -n ~/los24-build.lock`): only `hals/audio/services/TrackProbe.h` copied into `vendor/apklayer` (the tree's copy was identical to main's), then `python3 tools/lib/orb-build-module.py libapklayer_audioserver hals/audio/services Service.cpp`: compiled, link failed (libaudioflinger.a and libaudiopolicyservice.a are not in out/). Second command: `flock -n ~/los24-build.lock -c 'cd ~/los24 && source build/envsetup.sh && breakfast gsi_arm64 userdebug && m libapklayer_audioserver'`.
- Result: built (11:36, 583 steps), system/lib64/libapklayer_audioserver.so sha256 425632dc78989752dc286ea38dcbd233b4057b896a3d9f81810f07938d745e23; installed only in the audio agent's `~/wt/audio/lib64` on UTM (audio-track: 47760 of 47760 non-silent frames, three runs). TrackProbe.h in `vendor/apklayer` is changed but not committed there (the next `tools/sync-aosp.sh` from main after the merge commits it); `~/android-arm64/lib64` still has the old library.
### 2026-11-04 — m libapklayer_egl: libapklayer-gles1 by plain dlopen, eglReleaseThread resets the GL hooks (nativepath agent)
- Why: NativeLoader no longer puts the native dir on the bionic search path, so Gles1.cpp's dlmopen workaround goes; eglReleaseThread left the thread's TLS_SLOT_OPENGL_API table of the released context.
- Command (OrbStack): `hals/graphics/egl/{Egl,Gles1}.cpp` copied into `vendor/apklayer` (the tree's copies were identical to main's), then `flock -n ~/los24-build.lock -c 'cd ~/los24 && source build/envsetup.sh && breakfast gsi_arm64 userdebug && m libapklayer_egl'`.
- Result: built (10:20, 11 ninja steps), system/lib64/libapklayer_egl.so sha256 981c26d3ff318553449df4e5582237e02d480947c29d0de67d2e2f36afd7cd42; installed only in `~/wt/nativepath/lib64` on UTM. The two sources in `vendor/apklayer` are changed but not committed there (the next `tools/sync-aosp.sh` from main after the merge commits them).
### 2026-11-04 — m libapklayer_codec2 (full build of the module)
- Why: the codec2 library rebuilt with tools/lib/orb-build-module.py for the EOS fix linked old objects without SoftCodecs.cpp, so the tree's software decoders (MP3, Opus, ...) were missing (native-media: audio/mpeg NAME_NOT_FOUND). ### 2026-11-04 — libapklayer_audio: the --probe configures only the output ports (audio agent)
- Why: since the input mix and microphone ports were added (ec9f119) the HAL has four ports; the probe asserted two and aborted before writing PCM (audio-aidl reported it as "left channel tone lost/swapped").
- Command (OrbStack, under `flock -n ~/los24-build.lock`): only `hals/audio/Probe.cpp` copied into `vendor/apklayer` (the tree's copy was identical to main's), then `APKLAYER_VARIANT=android_vendor_arm64_armv8-a_shared python3 tools/lib/orb-build-module.py libapklayer_audio hals/audio Probe.cpp`: compiled, link failed (the vendor-variant libraries it links, libfmq.so, libaudioutils.so ..., are not in out/). Second command: `flock -n ~/los24-build.lock -c 'cd ~/los24 && source build/envsetup.sh && breakfast gsi_arm64 userdebug && m libapklayer_audio'`.
- Result: built (14:06, 632 steps), vendor/lib64/libapklayer_audio.so sha256 bece35c16ec0ceb52e71439af177417da241742384e19d9a0d2403f4595633ac; installed only in the audio agent's lib64 copies (`~/wt/aud/lib64` on UTM and Parallels). Probe.cpp in `vendor/apklayer` is changed but not committed there (the next `tools/sync-aosp.sh` from main after the merge commits it).
- Third build: the stack now also runs AudioFlinger, whose patch shares the device port config; the probe resets only configs it created. Probe.cpp copied again, then `APKLAYER_VARIANT=android_vendor_arm64_armv8-a_shared python3 tools/lib/orb-build-module.py libapklayer_audio hals/audio Probe.cpp` (the vendor libraries exist now). Result: built, sha256 af9f0606...; the snapshot taken at the probe's start raced AudioFlinger's patch (1 of 4 runs failed). Fourth build, same command: the device config is left alone while another patch uses it at reset time.

### 2026-11-04 — libapklayer_codec2: EOS drain without a timestamp (media agent)
- Why: the AVC/VP8/VP9 adapter (ReorderedDecoder) rejected an end-of-stream work whose timestamp is -1 us (MediaCodec clients drain with an empty EOS buffer; GeckoView does), so MediaCodec reported UNKNOWN_ERROR at the end of every H.264 video.
- Command (OrbStack, under `flock -n ~/los24-build.lock`): only `hals/media/codec2/ReorderedDecoder.cpp` copied into `vendor/apklayer` (same as main before the edit), then `python3 tools/lib/orb-build-module.py libapklayer_codec2 hals/media/codec2 ReorderedDecoder.cpp`.
- Result: built (01:11), sha256 af4b582bc1320b91f30d0e0b44f27ba96a2cec461b50e23475d3958d2059ff91; installed only in the media agent's lib64 copy on the Parallels VM (`~/wt/fx/lib64`). The file in `vendor/apklayer` is changed but not committed there (the branch carries it; the next `tools/sync-aosp.sh` from main after the merge commits it).

### 2026-11-03 — libapklayer_egl: GL hook table for every context (egl agent)
- Why: with Android's libGLESv1_CM mapped, SurfaceFlinger crashed in its glGetString (reads TLS_SLOT_OPENGL_API's table without a null check) because our EGL set the slot only for ES 1 contexts; now ES 2/3 contexts get a table of the host's GLES entry points and no context a no-op table, as Android's EGL does (gHooks / gHooksNoContext). Only aosp/hals/graphics/egl/{Gles1.cpp,Gles1.h} copied into vendor/apklayer (Egl.cpp and Android.bp there were identical to this branch). Command: `flock -n ~/los24-build.lock -c 'cd ~/los24 && source build/envsetup.sh && breakfast gsi_arm64 userdebug && m libapklayer_egl'`.
- Result: built (10:44), sha256 7c64fcf17456953d5bf9cdfb1984564e302d3583d1e5bb187ddfec1307fbca03. Second build, same command: Gles1.cpp opens libapklayer-gles1.so through glibc's dlmopen (in app processes the native dir is on the bionic search path and bionic_translation linked the glibc library as an Android one: "failed to link libapklayer-gles1.so", OpenTTD got no ES 1 context). Result: built, sha256 eeee4d5179fb60fad0b38b652a8f942dae5ad19f4422644dd287b02b8d47f6aa.

### 2026-11-03 — libapklayer_egl: OpenGL ES 1 contexts (gl agent)
- Why: ES 1 apps (OpenTTD's SDL 1.2) got an ES 2 context in place of the ES 1 one the host's Mesa lacks; now an ES 1 context is the host's or gfxstream's GLES_CM translator (libapklayer-gles1.so, built on the VM) on a host GLES 3 context, and Android's libGLESv1_CM dispatches through the hook table our EGL stores in TLS_SLOT_OPENGL_API. Only aosp/hals/graphics/egl/{Android.bp,Egl.cpp,Gles1.cpp,Gles1.h} copied into vendor/apklayer (the tree's Egl.cpp was identical to main's). Command: `flock -n ~/los24-build.lock -c 'cd ~/los24 && source build/envsetup.sh && breakfast gsi_arm64 userdebug && m libapklayer_egl'`. Result: not run (this agent's session refuses `source build/envsetup.sh`); the four files wait in vendor/apklayer for that command. A full tools/sync-aosp.sh from a branch without them reverts them there. If Soong refuses `include_dirs` into frameworks/native, that is the line to change.

### 2026-11-03 — libbinder: the caller uid of an app JVM is its work source (keystore2 key namespaces per app)
- Why: keystore2 keys its database by the kernel's calling uid, which is the one Linux uid of every app JVM. `patches/aosp/frameworks/native/0005-libbinder-work-source-calling-uid.patch` (applied with `patches/apply aosp ~/los24`). Command: `cd ~/los24 && source build/envsetup.sh && breakfast gsi_arm64 userdebug && m libbinder`, under `flock -n ~/los24-build.lock`. Result: built (`m libbinder`, 10:32), `out/target/product/generic_arm64/system/lib64/libbinder.so`.
### 2026-11-03 — libapklayer_vdisplay: the configure callback after the display exists (gfx agent)
- Why: a first configure that arrived while `createVirtualDisplay` ran was dropped (`mToken == nullptr`), so the display kept 720x1280 while the app laid out at the fitted size (content rectangle smaller than the window). Only VirtualDisplay.cpp copied into vendor/apklayer (the tree's copy differed by this change only). Command: `flock -n ~/los24-build.lock -c 'cd ~/los24 && source build/envsetup.sh && breakfast gsi_arm64 userdebug && m libapklayer_vdisplay'`. Result: built twice (the first build compiled the old source: a concurrent sync-aosp.sh had put the tree's copy back; the second, 10:33, has the change). Installed only into the gfx agent's own lib copy on the VM (`~/wt/gfx/lib64`, md5 6f4a6dd1...), not into `~/android-arm64`; UiBench, Logseq and Unciv log `window resized: virtual display 720x1280 -> 452x804` (Unciv then -> 804x452). The change must reach main before the next sync-aosp.sh, or that sync reverts it in the tree.

### 2026-11-03 — `m` instead of the ninja-replay scripts (scripts/orb-build-module.py, scripts/orb-build-vulkan.py)
- Why: the two scripts replay Soong's compile commands by hand, from before builds were authorized (2026-10-27); one real `m` of the modules they rebuild confirms that `m <module>` replaces them, so they can be deleted. No source change. Command (under `flock -n ~/los24-build.lock`): `cd ~/los24 && source build/envsetup.sh && breakfast gsi_arm64 userdebug && m libapklayer_codec2 libvulkan vulkan.apklayer libapklayer_vktest`. Result: failed after 12:36 (analysis), 3 of 3655 steps: `vendor/apklayer/tools/vktest.cpp: 'android/hardware_buffer.h' file not found` (arm and arm64): the module's Android.bp lacks `libnativewindow_headers` (the replay script compiled it with other flags). `m` of a module name also builds its 32-bit variant, never built in this out/.
- Second run: `header_libs` of libapklayer_vktest gets `libnativewindow_headers` (aosp/tools/Android.bp, only that file copied into vendor/apklayer/tools); the arm64 outputs only: `m out/target/product/generic_arm64/system/lib64/libvulkan.so out/target/product/generic_arm64/system/lib64/libapklayer_codec2.so out/soong/.intermediates/vendor/apklayer/hals/graphics/vulkan/vulkan.apklayer/android_arm64_armv8-a_shared/vulkan.apklayer.so out/soong/.intermediates/vendor/apklayer/tools/libapklayer_vktest/android_arm64_armv8-a_shared/libapklayer_vktest.so`. Result: failed after 14:51 (analysis), 1 of 3918 steps: vktest.cpp now finds hardware_buffer.h but not its `android/rect.h` (`libarect_headers`, added to aosp/tools/Android.bp and copied into vendor/apklayer/tools). Not rerun: each attempt regenerates the graph (~15 min) and the remaining ~3900 steps rebuild much of the tree; scripts/orb-build-module.py and orb-build-vulkan.py stay until one `m` of these four outputs succeeds.
